# Console parity specification

The requirement is a console that **looks like the Google Cloud console and works like it**,
not generic Material styling with a cloud theme. This page is the checklist that requirement
is judged against, and the record of what evidence it rests on.

It also states plainly what could **not** be evidenced, because a parity claim with no
reference behind it is a preference dressed as a requirement.

---

## 1. Evidence and its limits

### What was used

Public Google Cloud documentation, fetched **2026-09-21**. Each page carries its own
`Last updated` date, recorded below. These pages document console **navigation paths, page
names, control labels and form fields** — the structure of each screen and what a user can do
on it.

| Reference | Last updated | What it evidences |
|---|---|---|
| [Change the appearance of the Google Cloud console](https://docs.cloud.google.com/docs/get-started/console-appearance) | 2026-09-18 | The **toolbar** as a named region; **Settings and utilities** control; **Light / Dark / Same as device** themes; theme changes do not reload the page |
| [Create buckets](https://docs.cloud.google.com/storage/docs/creating-buckets) | 2026-09-18 | Cloud Storage → **Buckets** page; **Create** button; the **Create a bucket** form and its named sections and fields |
| [Create a topic](https://docs.cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/create-topic) | 2026-09-18 | Pub/Sub → **Create topic** page; **Topic ID**, **Add a default subscription**, **Enable message retention**; **Create topic** button |
| [Access control with IAM](https://docs.cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/access-control), [Create and manage labels](https://docs.cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/labels), [Change subscription types](https://docs.cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/manage-subscriptions), fetched 2026-11-20 | 2026-11-20 | Pub/Sub **Topics** list: select one or more topics, **Show info panel**, its **Permissions** tab's **Add principal**; "Select a resource and change to the **LABELS** tab"; a subscription's details page **Edit**, **Delivery type**, then **Update** (#1847) |
| [Deploy a Cloud Run service](https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/deploying) | 2026-09-18 | Cloud Run page; **Deploy container** → **Create service** form; **Service name**, **Region**, **Authentication**, **Service scaling**, **Ingress**; the **Containers, Networking, Security** tab group |
| [Create Cloud Tasks queues](https://docs.cloud.google.com/tasks/docs/creating-queues) | 2026-09-18 | Cloud Tasks → **Queues** page; **Create queue** button; **Queue name**, **Region**; a green `check_circle` indicating a running queue |
| [Manage services](https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/managing/services), fetched 2026-11-20 | — | The Services list: **Select the checkbox next to the service**, then **Copy** (ending in **Create**) or **Delete**; **Filter services** > **Deployment type** > **Function**; the **Deployed by** column |
| [Manage revisions](https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/managing/revisions), fetched 2026-11-20 | — | The **Revision history** tab; "Click the three-dot vertical ellipse to the left of the revision, then click **Delete**" |
| [Cloud Run release notes](https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/release-notes), fetched 2026-11-20 | — | 2024-10-21 and 2025-02-07: "Cloud Run integrations are discontinued from the Google Cloud console and Google Cloud CLI", so Google's console has no Integrations tab to match |
| [Delete queues and tasks](https://docs.cloud.google.com/tasks/docs/deleting-appengine-queues-and-tasks), read 2026-11-20 | — | A task's checkbox, **Delete task**, then **Delete** at the prompt; **Purge queue**, then **Purge**; a queue's checkbox, **Pause queue**, then **Pause**; **Resume queue**; **Delete queue**, the queue name typed, then **Confirm** (#1848) |

[Deploy a Cloud Run service](https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/deploying), also last updated 2026-09-24, describes the same flow |
| [Chirp 3 Transcription](https://docs.cloud.google.com/speech-to-text/docs/models/chirp-3), read 2026-11-20 | not recorded | Speech-to-Text's **Transcriptions** page, **New Transcription**, its **Workspace** drop-down with **New Workspace** (Browse, create a bucket, Select, Create), **Local upload** or **Cloud storage**, **Continue** to **Transcription options**, **Submit**; the **Transcription details** page, where the audio can be played back in the browser (#2084). Google documents no word-by-word highlighting while it plays, so none is drawn |
| [Transcribe speech to text by using the Google Cloud console](https://docs.cloud.google.com/speech-to-text/docs/v1/transcribe-console), read 2026-11-20 | not recorded | First use asks where in Cloud Storage to keep configurations and transcriptions; the storage.buckets.get, list and update permissions a transcription needs (#2084) |
| [Layout parser quickstart](https://docs.cloud.google.com/document-ai/docs/layout-parse-quickstart), read 2026-11-20 | not recorded | **Upload Test Document** opens the **layout parser analysis** page, where the blocks or chunks parsed from the document are viewed, organized by detected types; **Edit Layout Config** (#2084) |
| [Creating and managing tags](https://docs.cloud.google.com/resource-manager/docs/tags/tags-creating-and-managing), fetched 2026-11-20 | not recorded | **Create tag key**: Tag key, Tag key description, **Tag values** (**Predefined values**, **Add value**, Tag value, Tag value description; **Dynamic values**); **Actions** > **View details**, **Edit** next to Description, **Manage access**; attaching from **Manage resources**: **Tags**, **Select scope**, **Open**, **Add tag**, Key, Value, **Save**, **Confirm**, and **Delete item** to detach (#1865) |
| [Deleting and restoring projects](https://docs.cloud.google.com/resource-manager/docs/delete-restore-projects), fetched 2026-11-20 | not recorded | Shutting down from **Manage resources** (**Delete**, the project ID, **Shut down**) and **Settings** (**Shut down**); **Restore** under **Resources pending deletion**, the organization chosen in the **Project picker** (#1865) |

**The Cloud Run edit is Google's on the service's tabs (#1852).** The current reference above,
dated 2026-09-24 and fetched again on 2026-11-20, edits on the service page's **Containers**,
**Networking**, **Security** and **Scaling** tabs and deploys with **View diff & redeploy**, then
**Deploy changes**; CloudBurrow's service page does the same, each tab's form reviewing the
settings it changes before it deploys. CloudBurrow also keeps **Edit and deploy new revision**
(#595), the whole form in one dialog; no dated reference for that label was found, so it is
CloudBurrow's own and no parity is claimed for it.

### What was **not** used, and the consequence

> **No authorized read-only console session was available, so no reference screenshots were
> captured.**

That has a specific and limiting consequence, stated here rather than buried:

- **Structural parity is specified and checkable**: which pages exist, how they are reached, what each is called, which controls they carry, and what those controls are labelled. Every item in §4 traces to a dated reference above.
- **Pixel parity is not specified and is not claimed.** Exact spacing, type scale, palette values, table row heights and icon metrics were **not** measured against a real console. Any checklist item asserting them would be invented, and [#43](https://github.com/cloudburrow/cloudburrow/issues/43) explicitly forbids inferring unseen screens.
- Screens with no reference above — billing, the IAM & Admin product, monitoring dashboards, the project chooser dialog's internals — are **out of scope** rather than approximated. A resource's own **Permissions** tab (#793) is in scope: it shows only the policy the resource's service stores, and says that nothing is enforced.

The support matrix therefore tracks **visual fidelity** separately from **API compatibility**,
and visual fidelity stays `Partial` until reference screens exist. Promoting it needs
screenshots, not a better-looking build.

### Assets and components

Original frontend code. No assumption is made that Google publishes the console frontend,
because it does not.

Where a published, permissively licensed Google asset exists it may be used and must be
recorded here and in the licence table of `internal/console/assets/icons/PROVENANCE.md`,
with its licence.

**As built, no Google asset is shipped.** Every icon the console draws — each product in the
navigation, the dashboard and the product catalogue, and each category heading — is a
CloudBurrow line drawing authored in `console.js` (`ICONS` and `CATEGORY_ICONS`) and rendered
as inline SVG. Google's published product and category icons were vendored for a while and
were removed on 2026-09-27 (#684), because no terms for redistributing them were ever found;
`internal/console/assets/icons/PROVENANCE.md` records what was removed and why.
`TestProductIconsAreVendoredAndSelfContained` fails if any file but PROVENANCE.md returns to that
directory or a screen points at one, and `TestCategoryIconsExistForEveryCategory` and
`TestConsoleKeepsItsOwnFallbackMarks` hold every navigation entry and category to a drawing of
its own. The category *names* are Google's product taxonomy; a name describes the product being
emulated and is not artwork.

The record of third-party assets is the licence table in that PROVENANCE.md. It is empty.
`TestEveryEmbeddedAssetIsOwnOrLicensed` fails when any file embedded from
`internal/console/assets` is neither on its list of CloudBurrow's own files nor in that table
with a source, a licence and the date the licence was checked.

**No web font is loaded at all** — a font fetched at runtime would break the
offline requirement, and one vendored into the repository would add a binary asset and a
licence obligation for decoration. System font stacks are used instead.

**One third-party asset is vendored: the terminal emulator.** The terminal drawer (#781)
draws with [xterm.js](https://github.com/xtermjs/xterm.js) 6.0.0 and its fit addon 0.11.0,
MIT-licensed, copied unmodified from their npm packages into
`internal/console/assets/vendor/xterm/` with their licence texts, recorded in the licence
table of `internal/console/assets/icons/PROVENANCE.md`, in NOTICE and in dependencies.json,
and pinned by hash in `TestVendoredAssetsAreLicensed`. It is served from the binary and loaded
only when the drawer is first opened.

The documentation above names icons by their [Material Symbols](https://fonts.google.com/icons)
identifiers (`add_box`, `check_circle`, `more_vert`), which is the one place the console's own
iconography is publicly pinned down; those names informed which icons exist, not what ships.

**Material Design 3 is used as a published Google design system, and it is not asserted to be
the console's design system.** The console's is internal and unpublished. Using Material 3
gets the family resemblance honestly; it does not make the result a replica.

---

## 2. Branding and the local indicator

CloudBurrow branding, not Google's. Specifically:

- The product name in the toolbar is **CloudBurrow**, never "Google Cloud".
- No Google logo, wordmark, product icon or category icon is used anywhere. Product and
  category marks are CloudBurrow's own line drawings (§1).
- A **persistent `LOCAL` indicator** is visible in the toolbar on every screen, at every
  viewport size, and is never dismissible. `TestViewportsDrawerBadgeAndTableScroll` reads it from the rendered pixels at each width in §5.

The indicator is not decoration. Someone with both a real console and this one open must be
able to tell which is which **without reading the data** — that is the test it has to pass.

---

## 3. Scope: screens exist only for operations that work

The console covers exactly what CloudBurrow supports, as
[compatibility.md](compatibility.md) records it.

| Area | In scope | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Cloud Storage | Three pages under one drawer row, **Buckets**, **Deleted buckets** (#789) and **Settings** (#792). Buckets list, bucket detail, objects and prefixes, create bucket (labels, default storage class, a zone for a Rapid storage bucket (RAPID class, #1443), uniform bucket-level access, object versioning, soft delete retention and object retention, on its own page; #852); upload, download, preview and delete objects (#295); an object's own page (generation, metageneration, finalize time, or *Not finalized* for an open appendable object (#1443), size, MD5 and CRC32C, headers, storage class, custom metadata, object contexts with their update times (#1345), holds); on an object's row and its page **Copy**, **Move or rename**, **Edit metadata** and **Edit storage class**; on objects checked in a bucket or folder, **Compose** (#790); on a bucket's page **Edit bucket** (labels, default storage class, object versioning, soft delete retention, pause hard deletion (#1702), retention period, default event-based hold, lifecycle rules, CORS configurations) and, while its retention policy is unlocked, **Lock retention policy**; the Objects tab's **Show** list, whose **Soft-deleted objects only** lists the bucket's soft-deleted objects with their generation and hard delete time, each with **Restore** (a **Deleted objects** tab before #1843); the **Deleted buckets** page, the project's soft-deleted buckets with their generation and hard delete time, each with **Restore**; managed folders in the browser, typed **Managed folder** (#789); **Create managed folder** on a bucket's page and each folder's, and **Delete managed folder** on a managed folder's row (#828); **Uniform bucket-level access** in Edit bucket; **Show versions** on a bucket's and folder's object list, every generation live and noncurrent, each opening its own page, with **Restore as live version** on a noncurrent one and **Delete version** on any; on an object's page and a generation's, **Edit holds and custom time** and, on a bucket made with object retention, **Edit object retention**; on a bucket's page **Run lifecycle now (CloudBurrow extension)** (#853); the **Settings** page, with the project's Cloud Storage service account and its HMAC keys: list, **Create key**, **Activate**, **Deactivate**, **Delete** (#792); on a bucket's page a **Notifications** tab (topic, event types, object name prefix, payload format, custom attributes) from `notifications.list`, each notification's own page from `notifications.get`, **Create notification** and, on each row and page, **Delete** (#791); the **Storage Intelligence** page (`/storage/intelligence`, #1519): the project's, the organization's and each folder's configuration, with **Enable (Standard)**, **Start trial**, **Disable**, **Inherit from parent** and **Edit filter** (`TestStorageIntelligenceThroughThePage`, #1591) | A preview is plain text or a raster image, never the document an object claims to be; see below. An object's page is addressed `_details/<bucket>/<object>`, as Google's console addresses it, because an object and a folder can share a name; its crumbs are the bucket and folders. Copy is the official client's `Copier` (`objects.rewrite`), to this bucket or another; Move is `ObjectHandle.Move` (`objects.move`), within the bucket; Compose is the `Composer` (`objects.compose`), 1 to 32 sources in the order checked, which the dialog shows and can reorder; Edit storage class rewrites the object onto itself as a new generation (STANDARD, NEARLINE, COLDLINE or ARCHIVE). Edit metadata is `objects.patch` on the JSON API against the metageneration just read, for Content-Type, Cache-Control, Content-Disposition and custom metadata, which it replaces: a key removed in the form is removed. **Nothing is replaced unless asked:** Copy, Move and Compose send `ifGenerationMatch=0`, so an existing destination is refused with the API's precondition failure, shown on the form; **Replace the destination if it exists** lifts it, and the console then asks for the destination's name back before sending. Every action is read back through the official client (`TestConsoleStorageObjectOperations`); in headless Chrome, `TestStorageObjectMetadataAndCopyThroughTheForms`. Edit bucket is `buckets.patch` on the JSON API against the metageneration just read, with every field the form holds; the lifecycle rules and CORS configurations are the JSON arrays the API takes, so a rule it does not accept is refused with its message. A label removed in the form is removed; an empty retention period, lifecycle or CORS removes it. **Lock retention policy** is the official client's `LockRetentionPolicy` with the metageneration the API requires; its confirmation says it is permanent and asks for the bucket's name, and once locked it is no longer offered. Restore is `ObjectHandle.Restore` for an object and `buckets.restore` through Google's generated JSON API client for a bucket, which the official client cannot list or restore. Managed folders come from `managedFolders.list`; Create managed folder is `managedFolders.insert` under the page's prefix and Delete managed folder is `managedFolders.delete` with `allowNonEmpty`, through Google's generated JSON API client, since the official client has neither; the delete's confirmation says the objects under it are kept and asks for its name, and a bucket without uniform bucket-level access is refused with the API's message (#828). The soft-deleted objects are the Objects tab's Show list choice Soft-deleted objects only, as Google's are (#1843). `TestConsoleStorageBucketSettings`, `TestConsoleStorageManagedFolders`; in headless Chrome, `TestStorageBucketSettingsThroughTheForm`, `TestStorageManagedFoldersThroughTheBrowser`. **Object versions (#853):** the Show list's Live and noncurrent objects (a Show versions checkbox before #1843) lists the same place from `objects.list` with `versions=true`, kept on for the viewer across pages; each row is named `name#generation`, as `gcloud storage` names one generation, with its generation, Live or Noncurrent since its `timeDeleted`, size and update time. A generation's page is addressed `_details/<bucket>/<generation>/<object>`, shows the object page's metadata for that generation with its State, and its crumbs lead to the object's page. **Restore as live version** is `objects.rewrite` through the official client's `Copier` with the generation as its source, against the live generation just read (or `ifGenerationMatch=0` when there is none), so the replaced version is kept as noncurrent on a versioned bucket; **Delete version** is `objects.delete` with the generation, confirmed by the object's name after a warning that the version is not kept as noncurrent. **Edit holds and custom time** is `objects.patch` of `temporaryHold`, `eventBasedHold` and `customTime` against the metageneration just read, on the live object or the generation shown; an emptied custom time is sent as null and refused with the API's message, since one cannot be removed. **Edit object retention** (mode None, Unlocked or Locked; retain-until; **Override unlocked retention**, which sends `overrideUnlockedRetention=true`) is offered only on a bucket made with object retention enabled, which the console cannot make: its Create form takes a name, so such a bucket comes from an SDK (`SetObjectRetention`) or gcloud. Submitting it asks for the object's name back, since a Locked configuration can only be extended. **Run lifecycle now** is CloudBurrow's own `POST /_cloudburrow/lifecycle`, not a Google Cloud call, which applies **every bucket's** lifecycle rules at once on the instance's clock; its label and form say so, and its dialog shows what the run did (versions deleted, storage classes changed, versions kept by a hold or retention, multipart uploads aborted). Every refusal is the API's message: a hold blocking a restore or delete, a shortened Unlocked retention without the override. Read back through the official client (`TestConsoleStorageObjectVersionsHoldsAndRetention`); in headless Chrome, `TestStorageObjectVersionsThroughTheBrowser`, which covers Show versions, Restore, Delete version, a generation's page, Edit holds and custom time and Run lifecycle now, but not Edit object retention, for want of a bucket the console can make with it. Settings (`/storage/settings`) names the service account `projects.serviceAccount.get` returns and lists `projects.hmacKeys.list`'s keys (access ID, service account, state, created, updated). **Create key** is `hmacKeys.create` for the service account typed; the key's **secret is shown once**, in the dialog that follows, with **Copy** and the note that it will not be shown again, as a secret value is: it is in the create response and nowhere else, not the operations ledger, Activity, the logs, the Request Log, the listing or any later response, and there is no key page to show it. Activate and Deactivate are `hmacKeys.update` against the etag just read. **Delete** (`hmacKeys.delete`) is offered only on an INACTIVE key, as the API requires, and asks for the access ID back; the route refuses it on an ACTIVE key. Read back through the official client (`TestConsoleStorageHMACKeys`); in headless Chrome, `TestStorageHMACKeySecretShownOnce`. Create bucket (#852) is `buckets.insert` with the options the form holds, and `enableObjectRetention=true` when object retention is chosen, the one setting that can be made only at creation; the storage classes offered are the four the backend keeps, and uniform bucket-level access is on by default, as the documented form has it (turned off, the bucket has fine-grained access, whose ACLs its Access control tab lists, adds and removes, stored and not enforced, #1233; `TestStorageConsoleACLs`, in headless Chrome `TestStorageACLsThroughTheBrowser`). A value the API refuses, such as a 5-second soft delete retention, is refused with its message and creates nothing. Each option is read back through the official client (`TestConsoleStorageCreateBucketOptions`); in headless Chrome, `TestStorageCreateBucketOptionsThroughTheForm` | **Create notification** is `BucketHandle.AddNotification` through the official client; its topic is a picker of the project's Pub/Sub topics, so it cannot name one that does not exist, with the event types (none checked is every type), an object name prefix, custom attributes one `key=value` per line and the payload format (`JSON_API_V1` or `NONE`). It is **offered only where it can succeed**: with no Pub/Sub on the instance the storage server answers `notifications.insert` with 501, and the tab says so in the server's own message, read from that refusal; a project with no topic has nothing to publish to, and the tab says to create one first. A value the API refuses (an eleventh custom attribute) is refused with its message on the form. **Delete** is `DeleteNotification`, confirmed by typing the notification's name, `notificationConfigs/<id>`. A configuration cannot be changed, as in the API; its page says to delete it and create another. The storage server delivers to the Pub/Sub emulator (#506). `TestConsoleStorageNotifications`; in headless Chrome, `TestStorageNotificationsThroughTheForm`. The XML API's bucket `PUT`, service `GET` and form `POST` (#1258) have no screens of their own: they are `buckets.insert`, `buckets.list` and `objects.insert` on the same server, so **Create**, the **Buckets** list and **Upload file** do what they do, and a bucket or object made through them shows on those pages. Create bucket's **Data encryption: Cloud KMS key** sets the bucket's default key, and new objects are encrypted with it; the bucket's **Configuration** shows its default key and which encryption types new objects may use, and an object's page its **Encryption** (the Cloud KMS key version, a customer-supplied key, or Google-managed) (#1938). |
| Pub/Sub | Four pages under one drawer row, **Topics**, **Subscriptions**, **Snapshots** and **Schemas**. Topics list, topic detail, subscriptions, and the topic's snapshots (`ListTopicSnapshots`, #787); on a topic: create subscription, publish message, pull and ack, pull without ack; on each of the topic's subscription rows: delete. Subscriptions list (subscription, topic, delivery type, ack deadline) from `ListSubscriptions`, subscription detail (delivery — push endpoint and its attributes, or the BigQuery, Cloud Storage or Bigtable target — dead-letter policy, retention, ordering, exactly-once, filter, expiration, retry policy, labels), and delete (#595); **Edit topic** on a topic's page and **Edit subscription** on a subscription's page (#786); on a subscription's page **Create snapshot**, **Detach from topic** (#1406), **Seek to snapshot** and **Seek to time**; Snapshots list (snapshot, topic, expiry) from `ListSnapshots`, snapshot detail (the subscriptions that can seek to it), **Edit snapshot** (labels and expiry, #1406), and delete (#787); a topic's schema settings on its page, Schemas list (schema, type, latest revision) from `ListSchemas`, schema detail (definition, and its revisions from `ListSchemaRevisions`), **Create schema**, and delete; on a schema's page **Commit revision**, **Test message** and **Delete schema** (#788); **Create topic** with its message retention and schema, on its own page, and **Create subscription** with its delivery, retention, retained acknowledged messages, message ordering, filter, retry and dead-letter settings (#852), exactly-once delivery and expiration period (#873); **Edit** on a topic's and a subscription's page opens Google's **Edit topic** and **Edit subscription** pages, ending in **Update**, and the Topics, Subscriptions and Snapshots lists' info panel has **Permissions** (topics and subscriptions; **Add principal** on every checked row) and **Labels** tabs (#1847) | The Subscriptions screen lists every subscription of the project, including one whose topic was deleted, which no topic page reaches. It has no create form: a subscription is created on its topic's page. **Pull without ack** is labelled as changing delivery attempts, because Pub/Sub has no peek; pulled messages are shown in the dialog and never recorded in Activity (#294). A subscription's **Delete**, on the topic page's row or on the Subscriptions screen, asks for the subscription's name back and calls `DeleteSubscription` through the official client (#595). The edit forms offer only what the pinned emulator's `UpdateTopic` and `UpdateSubscription` apply, measured against it (#786): a topic's message retention, labels and, since #1446, its schema and message encoding (an AVRO schema's binding the emulator's, a Protocol Buffer schema's CloudBurrow's front's; empty clears it); a subscription's push endpoint and attributes (pull↔push through `push_config`), ack deadline, retention, retain acknowledged messages, exactly-once delivery (since #880, on and off, refused with a push endpoint as Google refuses the pair), the expiration period (since #891, which CloudBurrow's front applies because the emulator refuses it: a duration or `never`, a period under a day or under the retention refused with the front's reason), retry policy, dead-letter policy and labels. **Labels are offered on both forms** (#949): the emulator refuses a labels update ("labels is not a known Topic field", and the same for a Subscription), and CloudBurrow's Pub/Sub front applies it instead, so the official client reads the new labels back (`TestConsolePubSubEditTopicAndSubscription`). The topic, filter and message ordering are shown disabled, each with the reason (the emulator refuses a filter change as "currently unsupported in the Pub/Sub Emulator"). A topic's retention, once set, cannot be emptied: the emulator would save 31 days. Edit: through the official client against the emulator, read back by it (`TestConsolePubSubEditTopicAndSubscription`); the subscription form is driven in headless Chrome by `TestPubSubEditSubscriptionThroughTheForm`, for exactly-once delivery by `TestPubSubEditSubscriptionExactlyOnceThroughTheForm`, and for the expiration period by `TestPubSubEditSubscriptionExpirationThroughTheForm`. Snapshots (#787) are created on a subscription's page, from its ID alone: **labels are not asked for**, because the emulator's `CreateSnapshot` accepts them and does not keep them. **Edit snapshot** (#1406) changes a snapshot's labels and expiry: the emulator's `UpdateSnapshot` answers `UNIMPLEMENTED`, and CloudBurrow's Pub/Sub front applies it instead, so the official client reads them back (`TestConsolePubSubSnapshotsAndSeek`). **Detach from topic** (#1406) on a subscription's page is destructive and confirmed: it says the subscription's messages are dropped and it receives nothing more; the front carries it out, as the emulator refuses `DetachSubscription`, and since #1446 a detached subscription's page offers **Edit subscription**, which changes the configuration the front keeps for it, still detached (`TestConsolePubSubDetachAndProtobufSchema`). **Seek to snapshot** is offered only when the subscription's topic has a snapshot, prefilled with it, since a seek to another topic's snapshot is refused; **Seek to time** takes an RFC 3339 time. Both are labelled as changing which messages are redelivered, and submitting either asks for the subscription's name back before anything is sent. A snapshot's **Delete** asks for its name back. None of the three is offered on a subscription whose topic was deleted or that was detached. **Advance clock (CloudBurrow extension)** on a subscription's page (#1040) is not a Pub/Sub API: it calls the front's `cloudburrow.pubsub.v1.EmulatorClock/Advance` with a duration, after a confirmation that asks for the subscription's name back and says the clock is the whole instance's, ages every subscription of every project and never moves back, and answers with the front's new clock and the subscriptions that expired (`TestConsolePubSubAdvanceClock`; in headless Chrome, `TestPubSubAdvanceClockThroughTheForm`). Through the official client against the emulator, read back by it (`TestConsolePubSubSnapshotsAndSeek`); in headless Chrome, `TestPubSubSnapshotSeekAndDeleteThroughTheForms`. Schemas (#788) go through the official `apiv1.SchemaClient`. **Create schema** offers a type, AVRO or PROTOCOL_BUFFER (#1406): the emulator refuses Protocol Buffer schemas as `UNIMPLEMENTED` ("Protocol buffer support not implemented in emulator"), and CloudBurrow's Pub/Sub front serves them instead (`TestConsolePubSubDetachAndProtobufSchema`). **Create schema** and **Commit revision** ask `ValidateSchema` first, so a definition it refuses is shown on the form with its message and nothing is created or committed. The only revision of a schema cannot be deleted: the emulator answers "Cannot delete last revision. Please use DeleteSchema.", and the page says so. A topic whose schema was deleted shows the `_deleted-schema_` the emulator reports, saying so. Read back by the official client (`TestConsolePubSubSchemas`); in headless Chrome, `TestPubSubSchemaCreateShowsTheValidationError`. Create topic and Create subscription (#852) offer only what the pinned emulator keeps and acts on, measured through the official client: a topic's retention and its schema, which then refuses a message that does not conform; a subscription's retention, retained acknowledged messages, message ordering, filter, which delivers only the matching messages, retry policy and dead-letter policy. **Exactly-once delivery and the expiration period are offered since #873.** The emulator stores an expiration policy and never acts on it, so CloudBurrow's Pub/Sub front (`internal/pubsubfront`) enforces it: the field defaults to Google's 31d, takes a duration or `never`, and a period under a day, or under the message retention, is refused with the API's reason; a subscription idle for its period is deleted (`TestPubSubSubscriptionExpiresWhenIdle`). Exactly-once delivery the emulator implements itself (`TestPubSubExactlyOnceDelivery`); with a push endpoint, which Google does not support with it and the emulator accepts, the form refuses it. A malformed filter is answered UNKNOWN "Application error processing RPC", which the official client retries until its deadline, so the console sends the create once and says the filter was refused; an out-of-range value is refused with the emulator's own message. `TestConsolePubSubCreateOptions`; in headless Chrome, `TestPubSubCreateOptionsThroughTheForms` |
| Cloud Tasks | Queues list, queue detail with configuration, tasks list, task detail; create queue, pause, resume, purge, delete; **Edit queue** and **Create task** on a queue's page, **Delete task** on a task's (#784) | Edit queue changes what `UpdateQueue` applies here: rate limits (max dispatches per second, max concurrent dispatches; max burst size shown, output only) and retry parameters (max attempts, min and max backoff, max doublings, max retry duration). Create task takes a **Target**: HTTP (URL, method, headers, body, optional schedule time and task ID, and an **Auth header**: none, an OIDC token with its service account and optional audience, or an OAuth token with its service account and optional scope, #1243), or App Engine (method, relative URI, service, version, headers, body, #1447), through `CreateTask`; a task's page shows its token, or its App Engine request with the host it is sent to and the last attempt's `NOT_FOUND` reason, since no App Engine runs on an instance. Edit queue also holds the queue's `app_engine_routing_override` (service, version, instance), shown with its host on the queue's Configuration; emptying the three clears it (#1447, `TestConsoleTasksEditQueueAppEngineRouting`). The Logs tab's Enable logs and Disable logs set the queue's logging (sampling ratio 1.0 or 0) through `UpdateQueue` (#1848). Both go through the in-process service's own API and are read back with the official client (`TestConsoleTasksEditQueueAndCreateTask`); the edit form is driven in headless Chrome by `TestTasksEditQueueThroughTheForm` |
| Cloud Run | Four pages under one drawer row, **Services**, **Jobs** (#785), **Worker pools** (#1300) and **Instances** (#1299). Worker pools list, a pool's page with its revisions, configuration and Permissions tab, **Deploy worker pool**, **Edit worker pool** (a new revision), **Scale** (the revision kept: the scaling mode, and the instance count, or the minimum, maximum and CPU utilization target of an automatic pool, #1788; Deploy and Edit offer the same settings) and **Delete**; instances list, an instance's page (with its URL, #1348), **Create instance** (which waits until the instance is ready, as every worker pool and instance operation does since #1348), **Stop**, **Start** and **Delete**, through the adapter's `WorkerPools`, `Revisions` and `Instances` API (`TestConsoleRunWorkerPoolDeployEditScaleDelete`, `TestConsoleRunInstanceCreateStopStartDelete`). Services list, service detail, revision history, revision detail, deploy, the **Containers**, **Networking**, **Security** and **Scaling** tabs, each ending in **View diff & redeploy** and its review's **Deploy changes** (#1852), **Edit and deploy new revision** (#595), **Delete** in the ⋮ menu of a revision that serves no traffic (#785, #1852), **Manage traffic** (splits, pinned revisions and LATEST, sent as an update mask of `traffic`; the Traffic section shows each tag and its URL; #1224, #1260), **Manage revision tags** (#1852); on the deploy and edit forms the service account, files from Secret Manager and in-memory volumes (#1297), NFS volumes (#1448), Cloud Storage volumes (#1474) and **Use HTTP/2 end-to-end**, which names the port h2c (#1475), and the settings stored without effect: execution environment, VPC connector and egress, encryption key and session affinity, each with help saying nothing acts on it locally (#1346). Jobs list, job detail (executions, configuration), **Deploy container** (opening Create job, ending in Create, #1933), **View and edit job configuration** (ending in **Update**, #1852), **Execute**, **Execute with overrides** (arguments, variables, task count and timeout for that execution only, #852), **Delete**; execution detail (status, task counts, **Tasks**, conditions, the failure's message, Logs), **Cancel** while running, **Delete** when finished (#785); task detail (status, index, retries, exit code, failure, conditions, containers, the task's own Logs; #1223) | Jobs go through the adapter's `Jobs` and `Executions` API, as the SDK does. The job form holds what the adapter maps (image, command, arguments quoted as a shell would, variables, variables from Secret Manager, labels, task count, parallelism, retries, task timeout, CPU and memory); the execution environment, VPC connector and egress and encryption key are offered as stored settings (#1346; `TestConsoleRunStoredSettings`); service accounts, volumes, binary authorization and triggers are named as not offered, and a job's **Permissions** tab reads and writes its stored policy through the adapter's `Jobs` `GetIamPolicy` and `SetIamPolicy` (`TestConsolePermissionsOnARunServiceAndJob`). An execution's Tasks and a task's page are read through the adapter's `Tasks` API (#1223); in headless Chrome, `TestCloudRunExecutionTasksInTheBrowser`. Cancel is performed without a confirmation, as a form's Cancel discards without asking (#783); a job's Delete is confirmed by the dialog's button alone, as Google's is, and the other deletes ask for the name back (#1931). An execution's Logs tab is its task pods' output read with `kubectl logs`: the Cloud Run API has no log method. Manage traffic is Google's per-revision dialog (#1852): a percent for the latest healthy revision and for each revision, prefilled from the service, keeping its tags, which Manage revision tags on each revision changes; a split that does not sum to 100, or that the API refuses, is shown on the form with its message (the API's own form, one `REVISION|LATEST PERCENT [TAG]` per line, is still accepted as the `traffic` field) (`TestRunManageTrafficSendsATrafficMask`; in headless Chrome, `TestCloudRunManageTrafficThroughTheBrowser`). The service account, files and volumes are read back and kept by an edit (`TestConsoleRunVolumesAndServiceAccount`; NFS volumes, `/mount/path=server:/export[:ro]`, `TestConsoleRunNFSVolume`; Cloud Storage volumes, `/mount/path=bucket[:ro][;option...]`, `TestRunGCSVolumeFieldRoundTrip`; HTTP/2, `TestRunHTTP2FieldNamesThePortH2C`). A revision's Delete is absent on a serving or tagged revision and on a service CloudBurrow did not create, which the API refuses.  The edit is the deploy form prefilled from the serving revision, with the service name shown and refused. It deploys through `UpdateService` and waits for the new revision; a failed rollout is reported with the adapter's message and the serving revision keeps serving. Annotations, probes and the working directory are read back and kept. Labels and variables from Secret Manager are on the deploy, edit and job forms (#852), one `NAME=secret` or `NAME=secret:version` per line, sent as the `secretKeyRef` the adapter resolves; the edit forms are prefilled with them, the secret named as the adapter recorded it rather than as the Kubernetes Secret it resolved to, so one removed on the form is removed. A variable from a secret the instance has no record of is named in the note and the form cannot be saved while it is there. Execute with overrides is RunJob's overrides, and leaves the job as it was. Through the official clients: `TestConsoleRunServiceLabelsAndSecrets`, `TestConsoleRunJobLabelsSecretsAndOverrides`; in headless Chrome, `TestCloudRunJobExecuteWithOverridesThroughTheForm`. Not offered for a multi-container service, or one whose entrypoint or arguments contain spaces, which the form's space-separated fields cannot hold. The label is not Google's: see §1 |
| Cloud Run v1 (Knative) API (#1293) | The **Services** page and its service and revision detail already show the services, revisions and traffic v1 lists as services, revisions, routes and configurations; a v1-only resource, the domain mapping, is the **Domain mappings** tab on a service's page, with **Map a domain** (domain, certificate mode) and **Remove domain mapping** (#1450) | v1 is read from the same Knative objects through the same v2 mapping the console's pages use, and writes through the same v2 server. The Domain mappings tab lists each domain mapped to the service with its `Ready`, `CertificateProvisioned` and `DomainRoutable` conditions, their reasons, and the DNS records Google documents; it says the instance's gateway routes a mapped domain (by Host header), that no public DNS is served, and that no certificate is issued, so `AUTOMATIC` ends `Ready` False (`CertificateNotIssued`) and `NONE` becomes Ready (`TestConsoleRunDomainMappings`, `TestDomainRowSaysWhyNotReady`). A job's `delayExecution` is v1-only too: the Jobs forms use v2, which has no field for it, so a job's page has **Set delayed execution**, a checkbox read and replaced through the v1 API at the region's host, as `gcloud beta run jobs update --[no-]delay-execution` does (`TestConsoleRunJobSetDelayedExecution`). A route and a configuration are a service's traffic and template, shown on its page. `gcloud run` in the console's terminal reaches v1 through the `run` override (`services list` only there: `--region` derives a host the pod cannot resolve) |
| Kubernetes | Google's Kubernetes Engine menu (#1857): Kubernetes clusters (the instance's one cluster, its Details, Nodes, Storage, Observability and Logs tabs, Connect), Workloads (with **Deploy**, row checkboxes and one **Delete** for the checked workloads, on each workload **Edit replicas**, **Autoscale**, **Expose**, **Rolling update**, **Delete** and the YAML tab's **Edit**, and its Overview tab's CPU, Memory and Disk charts, #2070), Secrets & ConfigMaps, Gateways, Services & Ingress, Storage (Persistent volume claims, Storage classes), Object Browser (filtered with an API group and kind checkbox tree, #2070); Pods, Jobs, Nodes and Events keep their pages, reached from those | CloudBurrow's own kind cluster, read through its Kubernetes API. **Not project-scoped:** a Kubernetes object belongs to a namespace. Without `gke` in `--services`, Create cluster, Edit, Upgrade and Delete are drawn and refused with the reason; with it, the Kubernetes Engine row below. Deploy is `kubectl create deployment`; a workload's actions are `kubectl scale`, `autoscale`, `expose`, a merge patch of the update strategy with `set image`, `delete` and `replace` (#1857, `TestGKEWorkloadActionsFollowGoogle`, `TestKubernetesEnginePagesWorkloadActionsThroughTheBrowser`), refused on Kubernetes system objects; the list's Delete is `kubectl delete` of each checked workload, refused whole when a system object is checked (#2070, `TestGKEWorkloadsListDeletesTheChecked`, `TestKubernetesEnginePagesWorkloadsBulkDeleteThroughTheBrowser`). The Overview charts are the kubelet's readings of the managed pods, summed (`TestGKEWorkloadOverviewUtilization`); nothing else writes to the cluster |
| Kubernetes Engine | With `--services gke` (#1913), Kubernetes clusters lists the project's Kubernetes Engine clusters beside the instance's own; **Create cluster** (Autopilot or Standard: name, regional or zonal location, release channel, version, network, default-pool size and machine type, more node pools with **Add node pool**, a client certificate), a served cluster's page with Google's tabs (Details; Nodes, holding **Node pools** and the **Nodes** read from the cluster with CPU requested and allocatable CPU, memory and storage; Storage, its persistent volumes; Observability; Logs; and Operations), **Connect** (`gcloud container clusters get-credentials`), **Edit** (release channel, labels), **Upgrade** (Upgrade control plane, also from the Version's **Upgrade available**) and **Delete**; the Node pools tab's **Add node pool** and each pool's **Resize**, **Edit**, **Upgrade** and **Delete**, and the **Operations** tab with **Cancel** (#1925) (`TestGKEServedClustersOnTheConsole`, `TestGKENodePoolsAndOperationsOnTheConsole`, `TestGKEServedClusterTabsAndUpgrades`, `TestGKEServedClusterThroughTheBrowser` in the served shard) | Each cluster is a kind cluster of its own. Upgrade is disabled with its reason while no newer version is served (one per local kind node image), and a node pool's while it runs the control plane's version; with a newer version served, the API refuses moving a cluster across kind node images, "not available locally" (#2069). An Autopilot cluster's node pools are refused with the reason, as Autopilot manages its nodes. The instance's own cluster still refuses Edit, Upgrade and Delete |
| VPC network | With `--services compute` (#1936), Google's VPC network menu: **VPC networks** (subnet count, MTU, mode, dynamic routing mode, firewall rules), **Create VPC network** (custom or automatic subnet mode, a first subnet, MTU, routing mode), a network's page with its **Subnets**, **Firewalls** and **Routes** tabs, **Edit**, **Switch to custom mode** and **Delete**; **Add subnet** (with secondary ranges), and on a subnet **Expand IP range**, **Edit secondary IPv4 ranges**, **Private Google Access** and **Delete**; **Firewall** with **Create firewall rule**, a rule's page with **Edit**, and **Delete**; **Routes** with **Create route** and **Delete**; **IP addresses** with **Reserve external static IP address**, **Reserve internal static IP address**, **Allocate IP range for private services access**, **Edit labels** and **Release** (`TestConsoleVPCNetworksAndSubnets`, `TestConsoleVPCFirewallsRoutesAndAddresses`, `TestVPCNetworkThroughTheBrowser`); and Network services' **Private Service Connect** (`/net-services/psc/list/consumers`, #2141): the Connected endpoints and **Connect endpoint** to a published service such as a Cloud SQL instance's service attachment, ending in Add endpoint (`TestConsolePSCEndpoints`, `TestCloudSQLPSCEndpointThroughTheBrowser`) | Networks are records other services check against; no traffic crosses them, and firewall rules and routes are not enforced. Every networking operation completes at once, so there is no Operations page. VM instances, disks and images are on Compute Engine's pages |
| Compute Engine | With `--services compute` (#1963, #1965), under **Compute**, Google's menu: **VM instances** (status, zone, machine type, internal and external IP, Connect; each row's **SSH**, answering with the `gcloud compute ssh` and `ssh` commands for the forwarded port, and its menu, **Start / Resume**, **Stop**, **Suspend**, **Reset** as the status allows, **Delete**); **Create instance** on Google's *Create an instance* page (name, labels, region and zone, machine type, boot disk image, type and size, network, subnetwork, external IPv4 address, network tags, service account and access scopes, startup script, metadata, deletion protection); an instance's page with its **Details**, **Storage**, **Networking** and **Serial port 1 (console)** tabs, **Edit** (labels, network tags, machine type while stopped, deletion protection, metadata), **Attach existing disk** and **Detach disk**; **Disks** (**Create disk** blank, from an image or a snapshot; **Create snapshot**, **Edit size**, **Edit labels**, **Delete**); **Snapshots** (**Create snapshot**, **Create disk**, **Edit labels**, **Delete**); **Images** (the public images served, the refused image projects with their reasons); **Machine types**; **Metadata** (the project's metadata, **Edit**) (`TestConsoleComputeVMs`, `TestConsoleComputeProjectMetadata`, `TestComputeEngineThroughTheBrowser`, `TestComputeEngineScreensMeetWCAG`) | A VM is a systemd container: SSH is the command for the forwarded port, not Google's in-browser SSH window; no Screenshot, Observability or OS info tabs; instance templates, instance groups, custom images, machine images, reservations and sole-tenant nodes are not served; firewall rules are not enforced between VMs |
| Secret Manager | Secrets list (with an Expires column and a **filter box**, #1228), secret detail, version detail, enable/disable/destroy, create, add version, show value; **Edit secret**; on Create and Edit secret the **expiry** (#1225), the **Pub/Sub notification topics** (#1259), and the **rotation** and **destruction delay** (#1278); **Advance clock (CloudBurrow extension)** on a secret's page (#1278); **customer-managed encryption keys** on Create secret, shown on the secret's page and changed or removed by **Change encryption keys**, and each version's key versions on its page (#1453); round 3 of #1850: the **Secret type** (Preview), Edit secret as a page ending in **Update secret**, the Versions tab's checkboxes with Disable, Enable and Destroy over the checked versions, View secret value in a dialog, and Create regional secret's Region or Multi-region on a **Regional secrets** tab | A value is shown only on request, and the request is recorded in Activity. Create secret offers the **replication policy** (#857): Automatic, or User-managed with its replica locations, kept and returned as given (`TestSecretManagerUserManagedReplicasRoundTrip`) and shown on the secret's page; it is fixed once the secret exists, so Edit does not offer it. The expiry is an expire time (RFC 3339) or a TTL from now, not both; in Edit both empty clears it. The secret's page shows it (Expires, and Expiration in Configuration, "Never" when unset). The list's filter box takes Secret Manager's own filter syntax, evaluated by the same code as `ListSecrets` and kept in the URL; a filter the API refuses is shown under the box with its message, with no rows (`TestConsoleSecretExpiry`, `TestConsoleSecretListFilter`; in headless Chrome, `TestSecretManagerExpiryThroughTheForms`, `TestSecretManagerListFilterThroughTheBox`). The topics are up to 10 `projects/PROJECT/topics/TOPIC`, one per line, each of which must exist on this instance's Pub/Sub; they are published to when the secret or a version of it changes, and shown on the secret's page (`TestConsoleSecretTopics`). The rotation is a next rotation time and a rotation period, and the destruction delay is the `version_destroy_ttl`, both shown on the secret's page; on a secret with a delay the version's Destroy is **Destroy (delayed)** and says when, the version's page shows its scheduled destroy time, and **Enable** or **Keep disabled** cancels it. Advance clock moves Secret Manager's clock and says it is not a Google API (`TestConsoleSecretRotationAndDestroyDelay`). The encryption keys are Cloud KMS CryptoKeys of this instance: one global key for automatic replication, or a key per replica in its location; a version is encrypted with them when it is added, its page shows the key version of each replica (Replication status), and Show value fails while a key version is disabled; Change encryption keys applies to new versions only, and clearing it removes them (`TestConsoleSecretCMEK`) |
| Cloud KMS | Key rings (every location of the project), ring detail with keys, key detail with versions, version detail; create ring and key (the key with its labels, destroy scheduled duration and rotation schedule, #852), delete an empty ring (#1595), add version, enable/disable/schedule destruction/restore, make primary, encrypt and decrypt (#593); **Edit key** on a key's page (#794) ; **EKM connections** (`kms-ekm`): a project's connections, Create EKM connection, a connection's page, Edit and Set as default; **Autokey** (`kms-autokey`): the configurations a project resolves through, Configure (Configuration source, Configure for project or folder or Inherit, #1851), its key handles, Create key handle (#1452); Create key as a page of its own, the key's Overview, Versions, Permissions and Logs tabs, and Google's confirmations of Rotate, Make primary, Disable, Enable, Restore and Destroy (Schedule Destruction) (#1851) | Create key and Create import job offer **Protection level** SOFTWARE or HSM; the forms say an HSM key is not in a hardware security module and has no attestation, and Create key offers EXTERNAL, EXTERNAL_VPC and HSM_SINGLE_TENANT too since #1469, saying the key URI or key path is stored and never dialled (#1452; `TestConsoleKMSCreateKeyAtHSM`, `TestConsoleKMSExternalKey`). The EKM connections page says nothing dials the EKM (`TestConsoleKMSEkmConnections`); the Autokey page shows the effective key project (`TestConsoleKMSAutokey`). Create key ring's **Location** is chosen from what `Locations.ListLocations` lists, `global` by default (#1291; `TestKMSCreateFormOffersListLocations`). Create key offers each implemented purpose and algorithm, and an ENABLED version's page offers its purpose's operations: Sign, Verify, Get public key, Encrypt with public key, Decrypt, MAC sign and verify, Raw encrypt and decrypt, Decapsulate; a ring's page offers **Generate random bytes** (#1212; `TestConsoleKMSVersionCryptoActions`, in headless Chrome `TestKMSSignAndVerifyThroughTheForm`). Verify and Encrypt with public key run standard crypto against the public key in the console, as a client would: Cloud KMS has neither method. Rings cannot be deleted, as in Cloud KMS; **Delete key** appears once a key has no versions and **Delete version** on a DESTROYED version that was not imported (`TestConsoleKMSDeleteIsOfferedWhereAllowed`). A ring's page has an **Import jobs** tab, with **Create import job** (import method, protection level) and an import job's page showing its wrapping public key, and a key's page offers **Import version**: key material as a file or base64, already wrapped or plain and wrapped in the console under the job's public key; a ring's **Retired resources** tab lists its deleted keys, each with its own page (#1219; `TestConsoleKMSImportJobsAndImportVersion`, `TestConsoleKMSImportJobsAndRetiredResources`, in headless Chrome `TestKMSImportJobAndVersionThroughTheForm`). **No key material is shown**: the provider calls only the API an SDK calls, which never returns it. A decrypted plaintext is shown in the dialog and recorded nowhere. Edit key changes the labels and the automatic rotation schedule (**Rotation period**, as `30d` or a Go duration, and **Next rotation time**, RFC 3339; #816), the fields `UpdateCryptoKey` applies here, sent with an update mask naming exactly the fields changed; the name is shown and fixed. The form does not check the period's range: one below the 24-hour minimum goes to `UpdateCryptoKey` and its refusal is shown. The service rotates the key at the next rotation time (a new version made primary) and moves it on by the period. Through the in-process service's own `UpdateCryptoKey`, read back with the official client (`TestConsoleKMSEditKey`, `TestKMSEditKeyRotationThroughUpdateCryptoKey`, the mask by `TestKMSEditKeyMaskNamesOnlyWhatChanged`); the form is driven in headless Chrome by `TestKMSEditKeyLabelsThroughTheForm` and `TestKMSEditKeyRotationThroughTheForm`. Create key (#852) sends the destroy scheduled duration, 30 days unless changed and fixed once the key exists, and the rotation period and next rotation time to `CreateCryptoKey`, whose refusal of a value out of range is shown with its message (`TestConsoleKMSCreateKeyOptions`; in headless Chrome, `TestKMSCreateKeyOptionsThroughTheForm`) |
| Cloud Scheduler | Jobs list with schedule, time zone, target, last run and result, next run; job detail; create (HTTP, Pub/Sub and App Engine targets), pause, resume, run now, delete (#593); **Edit job** on a job's page (#795) | Create job also takes an App Engine target (relative URI, service, version, body; #1447), and a job's page shows it with its host; every attempt fails `NOT_FOUND`, since no App Engine runs on an instance. Edit job is offered for such a job: relative URI, method, headers, body, routing (service, version, instance) and attempt deadline, through `UpdateJob` (`TestConsoleSchedulerEditAppEngineJob`). Create job and Edit job take an HTTP target's **Auth header**, an OIDC or OAuth token with its service account and its audience or scope (#1243), shown on the job's page and kept by an edit that does not change it (`TestConsoleTaskAndJobTokens`). Edit job changes what `UpdateJob` applies: description, schedule, time zone, the target's own fields (HTTP: URL, method, headers, body, attempt deadline; Pub/Sub: topic, message body, attributes; App Engine: as above) and the retry config. The name, region and target type are shown and fixed. An `Authorization` header, or one naming a token, secret or API key, is not put in the form and is kept. Not offered for a job whose body or message data is not UTF-8 text, which a text field cannot hold. Through the in-process service's own `UpdateJob`, read back with the official client (`TestConsoleSchedulerEditJob`); the form is driven in headless Chrome by `TestSchedulerEditJobThroughTheForm`. Edit job is a page, as Create job is, holding Create job's three steps (#1849, #2062). Each attempt writes `AttemptStarted` and `AttemptFinished` to the instance's Cloud Logging under `cloud_scheduler_job`, listed on the job's **Logs** tab and opened in the Logs Explorer by the row menu's **View logs** (`TestConsoleSchedulerLogsAndEditSteps`, `TestSchedulerLogsAndEditStepsThroughThePage`) |
| Permissions (IAM policy) | A **Permissions** tab on a Cloud Tasks queue's page, a Secret Manager secret's, a Cloud KMS key ring's and key's, a Pub/Sub topic's and subscription's (#1367), a Cloud Storage bucket's, and a Cloud Storage managed folder's, which is the page its row in the bucket browser opens, marked **Type: Managed folder** (#847): one row per principal and role; **Grant access** (principals and a role); **Remove principal** on each row (#793) | **CloudBurrow stores IAM policies but does not enforce them** ([ADR-0006](adr/0006-iam-policy-surface.md)): every caller can still do everything. The tab says so first, in the same words on every service, set by the console server so no provider can leave it out, with a link to the service's compatibility row. Every change reads the policy with the service's own `GetIamPolicy` and writes it with its `SetIamPolicy` carrying the etag the page read, so a policy changed since is refused with the service's own message (Cloud Tasks, Secret Manager and Cloud KMS `ABORTED`; Cloud Storage `412`) rather than overwritten. The principal and role are free text, because every one of these services stores any principal as typed and any role that is not empty, checking neither against Google's formats or roles; the help says so, and gives a role Google defines for the service as an example, not a list. A grant to no principal is refused before it is sent (a service drops an empty binding silently); an empty role is sent and refused with the service's message. IAM Conditions are not offered: the services refuse them `UNIMPLEMENTED`. Remove principal asks for the principal to be typed back; Cancel on Grant access discards without asking (#783). A managed folder's policy is read and written with `storage.managedFolders.getIamPolicy` and `setIamPolicy` on Google's generated JSON API client (the official client has no managed folder call), and is its own: a grant there leaves the bucket's alone. Not on a task, a secret version, a key version, a plain folder or an object, which have no policy here (object IAM is `UNIMPLEMENTED`); a change sent for one is refused. **Test permissions** (#1242), beside Grant access on every Permissions tab, sends the typed permissions to the service's own `TestIamPermissions` and shows each Held or Not held as it answers; it changes nothing (unit `TestPermissionsTest`; in headless Chrome `TestTestPermissionsThroughTheTab`). Read back with the official clients (`TestConsolePermissionsOnAQueue`, `TestConsolePermissionsOnASecret`, `TestConsolePermissionsOnAKeyRingAndKey`, `TestConsolePermissionsOnABucket`, `TestConsolePermissionsOnAManagedFolder`); in headless Chrome, `TestTasksPermissionsThroughTheTab` and `TestStorageManagedFolderPermissionsThroughTheTab` |
| Resource Manager | IAM & Admin's, as Google's console has it (#1865): the drawer has no Resource Manager row. **Manage resources** (`/cloud-resource-manager`) is Google's tree table of the organization, its folders and their projects (Name, ID, Status, Labels, Tags), with **Create project** (the New project page) and **Create folder** (the Create folder page, which comes back to Manage resources, #1932); **Move** and **Delete** for the selected row (a project is shut down: DELETE_REQUESTED for 30 days); **Resources pending deletion** with each one's deletion date and **Restore**; and the info panel (**Show info panel**, or a row's Permissions, Labels or Tags): the row's IAM policy with **Add principal** and **Remove**, a project's labels with **Edit labels**, and its tags (Google's Tags panel, below) (`TestManageResourcesStudio`, `TestManageResourcesTreeThroughThePage`). **Settings** has **Shut down** (`TestSettingsShutDown`, `TestIAMSettingsShutDown`). **Create tag key** is Google's page: Tag key, Tag key description, For use with network firewall (`GCE_FIREWALL` and its Network) and **Tag values**, **Predefined values** with an **Add value** row each (Tag value, Tag value description) made with the key, or **Dynamic values** with **Add value constraints** and a validation regular expression, the key's `allowedValuesRegex` (`.*` without constraints, #2023), ending in **Create tag key** (`TestTagKeyCreateForm`, `TestTagKeyCreateFormValuesAndPage`, `TestTagKeyDynamicValuesInTheConsole`, `TestDynamicTagValuesThroughTheBrowser`). A dynamic key's page shows its validation regular expression instead of values, with **Edit** for it, and a typed value, checked against it, is attached from the Tags panel (the Value a text box for such a key) or a project's or folder's **Bind dynamic tag** (#2023). With a folder or the organization chosen in the toolbar, **IAM**, **Organization Policies** and **Tags** are that resource's (#2023, §4.7). A key's row has **Actions** with **View details** and **Manage access** (its Permissions tab), and its page **Edit** next to Description, an edit panel ending in **Save** (`TestTagKeyPagesAsGooglesThroughTheBrowser`). The info panel's **Tags** is Google's: **Select scope** (the organization or a project, **Open**), **Add tag** with a **Key** and then a **Value**, **Delete item**, **Save** and **Confirm** (`TestManageResourcesTreeThroughThePage`). The Projects, Resource hierarchy and Tags lists keep their addresses, out of the menu, for a project's, a folder's, the organization's and a tag key's pages. Four pages: **Projects**, **Resource hierarchy** and **Tags**, and **IAM** under IAM & Admin (#1368): the selected project's IAM policy by principal, with **Grant access**, **Remove principal** and **Test permissions** on its Permissions tab, through Projects `GetIamPolicy`, `SetIamPolicy` and `TestIamPermissions`, stored, never enforced and inherited by nothing (ADR-0006). Project list, project detail, labels editable, create (with its **Parent**, the organization or a folder), delete, **Restore** (Google's word, #1865) and **Purge project now** while DELETE_REQUESTED (#1216, #1537); Delete is Google's shut down, the project ID typed back, then **Shut down** (#1865); the project's page has Details, Organization policies, Tags and Liens, with the local registry's caveats in the Details tab's note, not a Scope tab of its own (#1865, `TestProjectPageShutDownAndRestore`); **Move project** and the Parent on its page (#1294); the **Organization policies** section, **Set organization policy** and **Clear organization policy** (#1296); **Tags** on a project's page, bound and inherited, **Bind tag** and **Unbind tag**; **Liens**, **Add lien** and **Remove lien** (#1295). Resource hierarchy: the local organization and its folders with each one's path, folder detail with its folders and projects, **Create folder** (a page, #1932), **Rename folder**, **Move folder**, **Delete** and **Undelete folder**, and Tags on a folder's page (#1294); a **Permissions** tab on a folder's page (Folders `GetIamPolicy`, `SetIamPolicy` and `TestIamPermissions`, #1552), and the organization's own page, linked from the list, with what `GetOrganization` answers, the folders and projects directly under it, its **Organization policies** and a **Permissions** tab through Organizations `GetIamPolicy`, `SetIamPolicy` and `TestIamPermissions`, as Google's console offers IAM on the folder or organization picked in the resource selector (#1554, `TestConsoleOrganizationPage`, `TestResourceHierarchyOrganizationPageAndPermissions`); a **Log router** tab on a folder's and the organization's page, their log sinks, each opening on the Log router with that parent chosen (#1572). Tags: tag keys under the organization or a project, a key's page with its values, **Create tag key**, **Create tag value**, **Edit** next to Description (#1865) and **Delete** on each (#1295), and a **Permissions** tab on a key's and a value's page, stored and not enforced (#1486, `TestConsoleTagPermissions`); on a folder's page **Enable app management** and **Disable app management** (#1519), and on a folder's and the organization's pages **Organization policies** with **Set** and **Clear organization policy** (#1534, #1554, `TestResourceHierarchyAppManagementAndOrgPoliciesThroughThePage`, #1591); **Purge project now** on a project pending deletion (#1537, `TestResourceManagerPurgeProjectThroughThePage`) | A local registry, the one the Resource Manager v1 and v3 APIs serve: one fixed organization, its folders, tags, liens and project org policies. Org policies and tags are stored, never enforced (ADR-0006); a lien restricting `resourcemanager.projects.delete` makes deleting its project fail, as on Google, and a key with values or a bound value is not deleted. No IAM policy or billing: each returns UNIMPLEMENTED, and the project's page says so. **Undelete project** restores a deleted project whole; once purged (30 days on, or at once with **Purge project now**, #1537) it goes with what every service keeps under it (`TestConsoleProjectOrgPolicies`, `TestConsoleResourceHierarchy`, `TestConsoleTagsAndLiens`) |
| Firestore / Datastore | Collections and kinds, documents and entities, per-field detail, query builder; **Start collection**, **Add document**, and **Add field**, **Edit field**, **Delete field** and **Delete document** on their pages; **Create entity** on the Datastore screen and a kind's page, **Add property**, **Edit property**, **Delete property** and **Delete entity** (#796). Subcollections: a document's **Collections** tab (`ListCollectionIds`), each subcollection's page with the same reads and writes as a top-level collection, and **Start collection** on a document's page. Namespaces: the Datastore screen is Google's Entities page (#1846), the entities of one kind chosen with **Namespace** and **Kind** pickers over the table, checked rows deleted together with **Delete**; a **Namespaces** page lists the namespaces and each one's page its kinds, and **Create entity** takes a namespace. A kind's query tab is Google's **Query by kind** (built with **Add query clause**: WHERE or ORDER BY, property, operator, value type, value) and **Query by GQL** tabs. The Indexes page's **Entries** and **Size** are computed from the kind's entities by Google's storage size rules (<https://cloud.google.com/datastore/docs/concepts/storage-size>). The legacy **Datastore Admin** page is not offered: Google replaced it (backups by managed exports and imports, which the Datastore page's Export and Import are; bulk delete by a Dataflow template), and only a project that enabled it before can still reach it (<https://cloud.google.com/datastore/docs/console/datastore-admin-console>). Ancestors: an entity's **Children** tab, a child entity's page with its key path and parent, and **Create child entity** on an entity's page (#854). Firestore databases (#1331): a **Firestore databases** screen lists `(default)` and every created database, with **Create database** and **Delete database**, and a database's page shows its settings and its **Indexes** and **Field overrides** tabs, read through the Firestore Admin API the Firestore pod's call front serves; **Edit database** (concurrency mode, point-in-time recovery, App Engine integration, delete protection), **Create index** and **Add field override** on a database's page, **Delete index** on an index's row, and **Edit field override** and **Delete field override** on a field's row (#1343); a **Backup schedules** tab, **Create backup schedule** (daily or weekly, retention in days) on a database's page and **Edit retention** and **Delete backup schedule** on a schedule's row (#1461, `TestConsoleFirestoreBackupSchedules`), the tab saying a schedule takes its first backup when created; **Export documents** (a gs:// destination in the instance's Cloud Storage), **Import documents**, **Bulk delete documents** (optional collection IDs) and **Clone database** on a database's page (a whole minute in the source's version window, #1942), the **Disaster recovery** tab showing point-in-time recovery's status, retention period and earliest version time, and its **Edit** holding **Enable point-in-time recovery** beside the backup schedules, as Google's page does (#1942; Google's console offers no read at a past time, so none is offered), and a **Backups** tab with **Restore** and **Delete backup** on a backup's row (#1500, `TestConsoleFirestoreTransferAndBackups`; in headless Chrome `TestFirestoreBulkDeleteAndBackupsThroughTheBrowser`); Create database's **Edition** (Standard or Enterprise), and on an Enterprise edition database's page **Run pipeline** (a collection, collection group or database input, a where, a sort and a limit, with optional explain stats, its results listed) and Google's **Security** tab of its users (#1845, round 3: each user's role on the database) with **Add User** (Username and a role, Cloud Datastore Viewer, User or Owner, granted on that database alone by a conditional IAM binding on the project, then **Add user**), **Disable**/**Enable**, **Reset password** (the generated password shown once, with the MongoDB connection string it authenticates with) and **Delete**, and its base **Connection string**, which the Data panel's **View more** shows as **Connect using MongoDB tools** (Firestore with MongoDB compatibility, #1703, `TestFirestoreMongoDB`, `TestFirestoreMongoSecurityAndConnectThroughThePage`), which a Standard database's page does not offer, as Google serves both on Enterprise databases alone (#1500, `TestConsoleFirestorePipelineAndUserCreds`, `TestFirestorePipelineAndUserCredsThroughThePage`) | Indexes and field overrides are stored and returned READY and change nothing: the emulator answers any query without an index and deletes no expired document, and both tabs say so. Every database, index and field override change goes through the official `firestore/apiv1/admin` client, as the screen reads, and a refusal is the Admin API's own message. Edit database carries the etag the form was drawn from, so a database changed since is refused `ABORTED`. Create index takes its fields one per line, each ascending, descending or array-contains; a vector index is not offered. A field override's form offers a TTL policy and the single-field indexes Google's console offers (ascending, descending and array-contains, at collection and collection group scope); a field whose index settings it cannot express is offered Delete field override only, and Add field override is refused on a field that has one. `TestConsoleFirestoreDatabaseEditsIndexesAndFieldOverrides` (unit and compat) reads every change back with the admin client; in headless Chrome `TestFirestoreDatabaseEditAndIndexFormsThroughTheBrowser`. A collection or kind is not a first-class resource, so none is created or deleted as such: Start collection and Create entity make its first document or entity, and it goes with its last. Every write goes through the official `firestore` or `datastore` client against the emulator, and a refusal shows the emulator's or the client's own message (a taken ID is `ALREADY_EXISTS`, a latitude of 200 `INVALID_ARGUMENT`). Values are typed as Google's consoles type them — Firestore: string, number (with a decimal point a double, without one an integer), boolean, null, timestamp, geopoint, reference, bytes (as base64, #995), map, array; Datastore: string, integer, float, boolean, timestamp, key, geopoint, array, embedded entity, null, each with **Exclude from indexes** — and a map, array or embedded entity is written as JSON whose values are strings, numbers, booleans, null, maps and arrays. A field is edited and deleted on its own page, prefilled with its stored type, so saving it unchanged writes the same type back (`TestEditFormsRoundTripTheStoredType`); a value that form cannot hold (a map or array holding bytes, a timestamp, a geopoint or a reference) is shown without an edit of the whole and can be deleted. A Datastore key value is shown and prefilled as `Kind/name=…` or `Kind/id=…` pairs, with `__namespace__/{namespace}/` first in a namespace other than the default, so a key to the name `id=7` (`Order/name=id=7`) is not the numeric ID 7 (`Order/id=7`) and saving it unchanged writes it back as it was (#887). A key value in another project or database is shown with where it is, such as `Order/id=7 (project another-project)`, and offered no edit; Add, Edit and Delete property write the entity back through the v1 API, so they leave it, and every value they do not touch, as it was (#893). A key inside an array or an embedded entity is shown as `key(Order/name=id=7)` (#894). A kind's entities and the query builder's results are read with `RunQuery` on the same v1 API, so a key value and an embedded entity's own key are shown with where they are in the listing's Properties cell as on the entity's page (#904). A Firestore map or array field's **Elements** tab lists every value inside it, at any depth, as `meta.items[2]` or `meta.items[2]["odd name"]`, each with its own page, with **Edit value** (prefilled; a reference into another project or database, and a map or array, are offered none; a top-level field holding such a reference is offered no **Edit field**, and the route refuses one posted by hand, #1050, `TestConsoleFirestoreEditFieldRefusesAForeignReference`), **Add value** on a map (a new key) or array (at an index, or appended) and **Remove value**; each is read with `BatchGetDocuments` and written with one `Commit` masked to the changed value's path as far as it runs through maps, preconditioned on the document's update time and on a digest of the field as drawn, so nothing else in the document is written and a field changed meanwhile is refused (#995, `TestConsoleFirestoreEditValuesInsideMapsAndArrays`; in headless Chrome `TestFirestoreEditAddRemoveAndBytesValuesThroughTheBrowser`). A Datastore array or embedded entity property's **Elements** tab lists every value inside it, at any depth, each opening its own page, and **Edit value** on one — typed as Edit property is, prefilled — writes back that value only, through the v1 API in a transaction, keeping its index flag, with every other value in the property and the entity written back exactly as it was read; a blob and a key in another project or database are listed and offered no Edit value (#905, `TestConsoleDatastoreEditValueInsideArraysAndEntities`; in headless Chrome `TestDatastoreEditValueInsideAnArrayThroughTheBrowser`). A subcollection's page is addressed by its path in one segment (`/firestore/users%2Falice%2Forders`), so its documents and fields keep the collection, document, field levels, and its breadcrumb leads back through every document and collection above it. A Datastore page in a namespace other than the default is addressed under `__namespace__/{namespace}`, a segment no kind can take (Datastore reserves kind names that begin and end with two underscores); entities are listed in a **Name/ID** column, as on Google's console, which renders a name `name=…` and a numeric ID `id=…`, so a root entity named `id=7` (`name=id=7`) and the one whose numeric ID is 7 (`id=7`) read differently where they are listed (#885); a key path — a child's heading, an entity's Key path, the Parent column — uses the same rendering, `Customer/name=alice/Order/id=7`, with a slash or percent sign in a name escaped (`%2F`, `%25`). An entity's page is addressed by its key encoded as Google's console encodes it in its own entity URLs — the URL-safe base64 of the key, the official client's `Key.Encode` — and headed by its Name/ID, or a child by its key path, so every entity has an address of its own: a root entity named like a child's key path (`Customer/alice/Order/x` in kind `Order`) and that child both open, and a delete asks for the key as the page is headed (#875). A kind's entities and the query builder's results have a **Parent** column — *none (root entity)*, or the parent's key path — as Google's console lists an entity's parent, so that root and that child are told apart where they are listed (#882), as their Name/ID cells, `name=Customer/alice/Order/x` and `name=x`, now also tell them (#885). A link in the older form — a name, `id=N` or a key path — still opens what it did; that form cannot tell a name from an ID, so `/datastore/Order/id=7` opens the numeric ID 7, as it always did, and the entity named `id=7` is opened by its row, which links to its encoded key (#885). A segment that is both is read as an encoded key only when it is the canonical encoding (byte for byte what `Key.Encode` writes, or that with the page's own project named) of a complete key with the page's namespace and kind and the default database, and as the older form otherwise, so another project's, namespace's or kind's key is a name here as it was before (#882); the one older link this changes is a root entity whose name is itself such an encoding, and its row opens it by its own encoded key (unit `TestDatastoreEncodedKeyPrecedence`). An entity's Children tab reads its descendants with a kindless ancestor query, bounded at 1,000, and lists the direct children among them. A subcollection is not deleted with its document, in Firestore or here; a document that does not exist but holds subcollections is listed in italics, its Fields cell reading *no fields — has subcollections*, because a collection is read with `ListDocuments` and `show_missing` (the client's `DocumentRefs`) rather than a query, which does not return it; its page is its Collections tab, and Add field on it creates it (#875). The Documents count on the Firestore screen and on a document's Collections tab is of that same list, so it includes such a document, and each list's note says so (#882). Every delete asks for the name back and returns to the page above.
