A Firestore field edit carries the document's update time as a precondition and a Datastore change is a transaction, so a change made meanwhile is refused rather than overwritten. `TestConsoleFirestoreDocumentCreateEditDelete`, `TestConsoleDatastoreEntityCreateEditDelete`, `TestConsoleFirestoreSubcollections`, `TestConsoleDatastoreNamespacesAndChildren`, `TestConsoleFirestoreListsMissingDocumentsWithSubcollections`, `TestConsoleDatastoreOpensARootEntityNamedLikeAKeyPath`, `TestConsoleFirestoreCountsMissingDocuments`, `TestConsoleDatastoreRowsNameTheirParent` and `TestConsoleDatastoreNameIDTellsANameFromAnID` (emulators shard) read every write back through the official clients; in headless Chrome, in the emulators shard's browser step, `TestFirestoreStartASubcollectionOnADocumentPage`, `TestDatastoreNamespaceAndChildEntityThroughTheBrowser`, `TestFirestoreMissingDocumentIsListedInItalics`, `TestDatastoreRootEntityNamedLikeAKeyPathOpens`, `TestFirestoreCollectionCountIncludesMissingDocuments`, `TestDatastoreKindListShowsEachRowsParent` and `TestDatastoreNameIDColumnTellsANameFromAnID` |
| Bigtable | Tables, rows, per-cell detail, column families, row-range reader, create and delete tables, in the instance chosen in the screen's **Instance** picker (#1360); **Add column family** and **Write cell** on a table's page, **Edit GC policy** and **Delete column family** on a family's row in its Schema tab, **Write cell** and **Delete row** on a row's page, **Delete cells** on a cell's row (#797); a **Permissions** tab on a table's page, stored and not enforced (#1486, `TestConsoleBigtableTablePermissions`); **SQL** in the table's query form, and **Create backup**, **Restore backup to a new table**, **Create authorized view**, **Snapshot table** and **Create schema bundle** on a table's page (#1499); the Bigtable instances screen's instance page has **Backups**, **Logical views**, **Materialized views**, **Snapshots**, **Authorized views** and **Schema bundles** tabs with their row actions, and a cluster's **Edit memory layer** (#1499, `TestConsoleBigtableSQLSnapshotsBundlesAndViews`); **Edit change stream** on a table's page (its retention, 0 to disable) and the table's **Change stream** section of the last hour's changes (#1523, `TestBigtableChangeStreamThroughThePage`, #1591) | By default the instance `cloudburrow`, whose tables the emulator serves without it being created. Since #1360 the screen's **Instance** picker offers it and every instance `ListInstances` lists; the choice is carried in the URL as `?instance=`, as the project is, and the default leaves it out, so every existing link still opens `cloudburrow`. Every request the screen and its table and row pages make carries it, so the tables, rows, column families, row-range reader and every table, family and cell action address the chosen instance; an instance that is not listed (mistyped, or deleted) is refused with its name rather than served empty, since the emulator would serve any name. An instance's page on the instances screen has a **Tables** tab whose rows open each table on the Bigtable screen with that instance chosen. `TestConsoleBigtableInstancePicker` (unit, against the front in process; and compat, read back through the official clients on the created instance) and `TestBigtableInstancePickerThroughTheBrowser` (picks the instance, creates a table in it, deletes its rows and follows the Tables tab in headless Chrome). Since #1331 the instance admin API is served (by CloudBurrow's Bigtable front) and the screen's note lists the project's created instances, with display name, state and cluster count (`TestConsoleBigtableListsInstances`). Since #1358 the **Bigtable instances** screen (`/bigtable-instances`) lists them with type, state, cluster count and labels, and offers **Create instance** (ID, display name, type, labels and a first cluster: ID, zone, storage type, nodes or autoscaling) and **Delete instance**, confirmed by its ID, which deletes its tables; an instance's page offers **Edit instance** (display name, type, labels), **Add cluster**, **Create app profile** and **Delete instance**, with a **Clusters** tab (**Edit nodes**: a fixed size, or autoscaling limits and CPU target; **Delete cluster**, refused with the API's message for the last cluster or one an app profile routes to), an **App profiles** tab (**Edit app profile**: description, multi- or single-cluster routing, single-row transactions; **Delete app profile**) and a **Permissions** tab on the instance's IAM policy, stored and not enforced. Every change goes through the official `InstanceAdminClient` (`TestConsoleBigtableInstancesScreen` against the front in process; `TestConsoleBigtableInstanceAdmin` against an instance, reading each back with the client; `TestBigtableInstanceAdminThroughTheBrowser` creates an instance, edits a cluster's nodes and deletes it in headless Chrome). Clusters and nodes are stored, not acted on: the one emulator serves every table. A table created with its column families is listed by the official admin client, and a console delete removes it (`TestConsoleBigtableCreateAndDeleteTable`, #699). Families change through the official admin client (`ModifyColumnFamilies`) and cells through the data client's `Apply` (`MutateRow`) against the emulator, and a refusal shows the emulator's own message (a family that exists, a write to one that does not). A GC policy is what Google's console offers: a maximum number of versions, a maximum age in whole days, hours or minutes, or both, deleted when either or only when both are exceeded; Edit GC policy is prefilled from the stored policy, and a policy the form cannot express (nested, or an age that is not whole minutes) is shown with no edit (`TestGCPolicyFormRoundTripsWhatItCanHold`). Write cell is offered only on a table that has a family, since a write names one; a timestamp is RFC 3339 at millisecond precision, and a finer one is refused before it is sent, because Bigtable refuses it. Every delete asks for the name back — the family, the `family:qualifier` column or the row key — and Delete row returns to the table; a delete of a row or column that holds nothing is refused rather than reported done. `TestConsoleBigtableFamiliesAndRowWrites` (emulators shard) reads every change back through the official clients' `TableInfo` and `ReadRow`. Since #1198 a table's page also offers **Delete rows by prefix** and **Delete all rows** (`DropRowRange`), each confirmed by the table's name typed back, and **Edit deletion protection** (`UpdateTable`), prefilled from the table, and while it is on Delete table is refused with the emulator's message; a cell offers **Increment** and **Append value** (`ReadModifyWriteRow`); Delete row and Delete cells are one `CheckAndMutateRow` each, refused when the row or column was emptied since the page read it. `TestConsoleBigtableTableAndCellActions` reads each back through the official clients, and the browser tests `TestBigtableDeleteRowsByPrefixAndAllRowsThroughTheBrowser`, `TestBigtableDeletionProtectionRefusesDeleteTableThroughTheBrowser`, `TestBigtableIncrementAndAppendValueThroughTheBrowser` and `TestBigtableConditionalDeleteRowAndCellsThroughTheBrowser` drive them in headless Chrome (emulators shard). **Not driven in a browser:** Add column family, Edit GC policy, Delete column family and Write cell; the unit tests `TestBigtableFamilyAndRowActionsThroughTheOfficialClients` and `TestBigtableActionsAreOfferedWhereTheyApply`, against `bttest`, cover them and where they are offered. Its calls, read through its front, are in the Request Log and charted on **Monitoring** (request rate, errors, latency and the emulator container's CPU and memory, #1267; `TestBigtableCallsReachMetricsAndTheRequestLog`) |
| Request Log (Operations) | Every API call CloudBurrow served: time, service, method, resource, canonical code, duration. Filters by service, code and project, carried in the URL. Live over `/api/stream?stream=requests` (#291). | **Not a Google Cloud console screen.** It has no GCP reference, so no parity is claimed: it is modelled on LocalCloud's request logs and LocalStack's App Inspector. Cloud Tasks, Secret Manager, Cloud Run, Cloud KMS, Cloud Scheduler, Cloud Logging, Resource Manager and Cloud Storage are observable, because CloudBurrow serves them itself; storage calls are scraped from the in-cluster server with the bucket and object they were for (#518). Which services are observed comes from the observers `up` wires, the same set the metrics count (#683). Memorystore's admin API is observed too. Pub/Sub's and Bigtable's calls are read from their fronts (#1266, #1267), and Spanner's, Firestore's and Datastore's from the call front in each emulator's pod, a pod's calls as well as the host's (#1288; `TestPubSubCallsReachMetricsAndTheRequestLog`, `TestBigtableCallsReachMetricsAndTheRequestLog`, `TestSpannerCallsReachMetricsAndTheRequestLog`, `TestAPodsFirestoreCallsReachMetricsAndTheRequestLog`). Any other enabled service, with no call observer, is listed as *requests not observable (forwarded to the service's own server without being read)*, never as an empty list. No payload or query string is recorded, and the page copies named fields only. |
| Connect and About (Management tools) | What `cloudburrow env` exports for this running instance, in each format it prints (shell, plain, JSON, Terraform, docker-compose, Kubernetes), each with **Copy**, and the variables as a table; Go, Python and Node.js client snippets for the variables exported; the `eval "$(cloudburrow gcloud-setup)"`, `gcloud-teardown` and `cloudburrow terraform` commands to copy, each with what it writes on the host; **About**, version, commit and build date as `cloudburrow version` prints them, also in Settings and utilities; **Download bundle**, the `cloudburrow diagnose` bundle (#802). Reached from the toolbar's Connect control, where the console this mirrors keeps its help menu, and from the navigation | **Not a Google Cloud console screen**, so no parity is claimed. Every value comes from the functions the commands use, so the page cannot drift from them (`TestConnectVariablesAreEnvJSON`). No credential is shown: `MYSQL_PASSWORD` is named and withheld, the ADC fixture is shown by path only, and the admin token is never held. The console runs no command and writes no gcloud or Terraform configuration. The download needs the admin token, pasted once per tab, because the bundle carries the admin API's events and a workload can reach the console on Docker Desktop; the admin API decides (`TestConsoleDiagnoseNeedsTheAdminToken`). Below 600px the toolbar control gives way to the Settings and utilities link. `TestConsoleConnectIsTheInstancesEnv`, `TestConsoleDiagnoseBundleHoldsNoCredentials`, `TestConnectPageShowsTheEnvironmentAndDownloadsTheBundle` |
| Fault injection (Operations) | The rules `/admin/faults` holds (service, method glob, project, probability, code or HTTP status, latency, remaining of count, seed, injected); **Add rule** with exactly those fields, offering only the services the instance interposes and the codes and HTTP statuses the API maps; **Delete** per rule, confirmed by its id; **Clear all**, confirmed; the services whose rules are refused, each with the admin API's own reason; **Recent faults** from the recorder's `fault` events, refreshed every two seconds (#800) | **Not a Google Cloud console screen**, so no parity is claimed. It needs the instance's admin token, pasted once per tab, because a workload can reach the console on Docker Desktop and the console adds no token of its own ([networking.md](networking.md#the-console-and-the-admin-token)). Every action is the admin API's, called in process with that token, and a refusal shows its message. **Cancel** on the form discards without asking; Escape asks. `TestConsoleFaultsActThroughTheAdminAPI`, `TestConsoleFaultRuleFailsTheSDKCall`, `TestFaultRuleAddedAndDeletedThroughTheForm` |
| Spanner | Instances, databases, tables, columns and indexes, DDL, query editor (read-only by default; **Read-write** runs one DML statement, #798), create (the instance is created when absent), **Delete database** on a database's page, **Delete instance** on an instance's page, **Edit instance** (display name and processing units, prefilled) on an instance's page and **Drop protection** on a database's page (#1461, `TestConsoleSpannerEditInstance`); a **Permissions** tab on an instance's and a database's page, stored and not enforced (#1486, `TestConsoleSpannerPermissions`); the Instances list's Info panel **Labels** tab, opened by a row's checkbox, and **Upgrade** beside an instance's edition (#1859, `TestSpannerInfoPanelLabelsAndUpgradeThroughThePage`) | Node count is accepted and has no effect locally. The database admin client reads back a console-created database and its first table, and each drop leaves `NOT_FOUND` (`TestConsoleSpannerCreateAndDropDatabase`, #699). The editor's **Read-write (writes data)** switch, offered on a database's and its tables' pages and never remembered across visits, runs one `INSERT`, `UPDATE` or `DELETE` in a read-write transaction after a confirmation naming the database, and reports the rows it changed; read-only mode refuses DML before sending it, and neither mode runs DDL or several statements (`ExecuteBatchDml` is not verified). Read back by the official client (`TestConsoleSpannerDML`); not checked in a browser |
| Cloud SQL for PostgreSQL | Databases, tables, columns and indexes, schemas, views, functions, users, server settings, live activity, SQL editor (read-only by default; an opt-in, confirmed **Read-write** mode runs the statement as the application's user in one transaction, #995); create database (`CREATE DATABASE`) and drop database (`DROP DATABASE`); **Add user** and **Delete user** on a database's page (#1301) | A real PostgreSQL, which the Cloud SQL Admin API serves as the instance `postgres` (#1301): Add user and Delete user run what `users.insert` and `users.delete` run, through the same code, and each sees the other's users (`TestConsoleCloudSQLUsersMatchTheAdminAPI`); no database flags. A user's password is stored, not checked: the server trusts local connections. Dropping the database the server was initialised with is refused. A console-created database accepts a pgx connection, a console drop removes it from `pg_database`, and the refusal carries the provider's message (`TestConsoleCloudSQLCreateAndDropDatabase`, #699). Read-write asks first, naming the database and showing the statement, and answers with PostgreSQL's command tag and rows affected or its own error; a failed statement is rolled back (`TestConsoleCloudSQLReadWriteEditor`, read back with pgx; in headless Chrome `TestCloudSQLReadWriteEditorsThroughThePage`) |
| BigQuery | Datasets, tables, a table's schema (nested fields by dotted path), details and first rows (Preview, read with `tabledata.list`), and a query editor on every dataset and table page, read-only by default (#698) with an opt-in, confirmed **Read-write** mode (#994); **Create dataset** and **Delete** on the list, **Create table** (with a schema editor, or a view with its query, #994) and **Delete dataset** on a dataset's page, **Insert rows** (`tabledata.insertAll`, with **Skip invalid rows** and **Ignore unknown values**, #994), **Edit table** (description and labels, `tables.patch`, #994) and **Delete table** on a table's page (#854); a materialized view's page shows its definition, with its last refresh time, and offers **Refresh**, which moves it, **Edit table** and **Delete table**, and **Create table** makes one (#1390); its query, and the query editor's scripts, read current data after a change to a base table, and a script may create, alter and drop one (#1415); a view's page offers **Edit table** and **Delete table**; **Load from Cloud Storage** and **Load from a file** (#999) on a dataset's and a table's page (with **Source data partitioning**, hive partitioning AUTO, STRINGS or CUSTOM from a source URI prefix, from Cloud Storage only, and **JSON extension** GEOJSON, #1547, `TestBigQueryHiveAndGeoJSONLoadsThroughTheForm`, #1591), **Export to Cloud Storage** on a table's page, and **Job history** with each job's page (a query job's statement type and DML row counts, #1041), **Delete job** and, on a job that is not done, **Cancel job** (#993); a table's **Row access policies** tab with **Create row access policy** (or replace one), **Delete policy** on each row and **Delete all row access policies**, and a **Permissions** tab on a table's and a routine's page that shows the API's refusal of their IAM methods (#1218); a table's and a view's **Analysis rules** in its Details tab (#1580); a dataset's **Models** section of the models `CREATE MODEL` trains in Read-write mode, a model's page (type, features, label, training runs, evaluation metrics, weights; an imported ONNX model's inputs, outputs and the URI it was imported from, #1711, `TestBigQueryONNXModelPage`), **Edit model** and **Delete model** (#1465, `TestBigQueryModelsSectionAndPage`; in headless Chrome, `TestBigQueryModelsThroughThePage`, #1554) | Through the official `cloud.google.com/go/bigquery` client against the forwarded REST port. A dataset is deleted with its tables, and both deletes ask for the name back. The emulator is more permissive than BigQuery in ways that damage data (measured, #854), and the validating front (`internal/bigqueryfront`, #861) in the port the console uses refuses all of it as BigQuery does, so the console no longer repeats those checks (#874): an invalid ID, a field name, a duplicate dataset or column, and every row's values (each type, REPEATED arrays, RECORD objects, REQUIRED values, unknown fields) are refused by the API, in its words, and a refused insert writes nothing. The forms still carry BigQuery's ID and field-name patterns, and the schema editor refuses a name, a case-only duplicate at one level or a RECORD with no fields before anything is sent. The field-name pattern is BigQuery's flexible column names, which the front accepts since #861 and #881: letters, numbers, marks, underscores, dashes, spaces and `& % = + : ' < > # |`, at most 300 characters, so `first name` is a field name (#994). The schema editor offers every scalar type, **RECORD with its nested fields listed under it, to 15 levels** (#874), and the three modes; a RECORD inside a REPEATED RECORD, or a REPEATED RECORD inside a RECORD, is written and read back since CloudBurrow builds the emulator with goccy/googlesqlite#76 (#1061); it was refused as not implemented before. A GEOGRAPHY value is sent as written, unchecked, as the emulator stores it. At most 500 rows per insert. The query editor reads by default: it runs one `SELECT` or `WITH … SELECT`, checked before it is sent, because BigQuery has no read-only transaction and the emulator's dry run reports every statement as `SELECT` (measured); unqualified names resolve in the dataset on screen; at most 200 rows are shown and a query stops after 15 s. The editor was read-only (#698) because a console that let one Run button decide from the text whether it wrote would be one misread statement from an unintended write; Spanner Studio's opt-in mode (#798) answered that, and BigQuery's is the same (#994): **Read-write** is a switch nobody's visit starts on, its Run button reads **Run statement**, and every run asks first, naming the project and default dataset and showing the statement. It sends the statement with `jobs.query` (never `jobs.insert`, which leaves a job-named dataset behind, #698): DDL — `CREATE SCHEMA`, `CREATE TABLE` with columns or `AS SELECT`, `CREATE VIEW`, `CREATE OR REPLACE`, `IF NOT EXISTS`, `DROP` — and scripts with `DECLARE` and `SET`, and answers that the statement ran, with its job. What the front answers 501 (`ALTER TABLE … SET DATA TYPE`, control-flow blocks, …) is shown in its words. **DML** (`INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `MERGE`, `TRUNCATE TABLE`) as a statement of its own is sent through `jobs.query` since #1024, and the answer says what it changed from the rows the front reports (the emulator alone changed the rows but reported no affected row count and failed a `MERGE` from a subquery, measured, #1008); since #1567 an `UPDATE` with a `FROM` clause of its own, which the front runs (#1027, #1459), is sent as it is, and so is DML inside a script or a `BEGIN` block, which the front carries out a statement at a time (#1353, #1459), answered with the script's job and each DML statement's rows, read from its child jobs, as Google's console lists a script's statements (`TestConsoleBigQueryReadWriteEditorRunsDML`, `TestBigQueryDMLAnswerSaysWhatChanged`, `TestBigQueryWriteReportSendsJobsQuery`). **Edit table** changes what `ALTER TABLE` changes (#1227) in one `tables.patch` of what changed: the description (a blank one clears it), friendly name, labels (a removed one is deleted, the others kept, as BigQuery merges a patch's labels and the front does since #1009, `TestBigQueryTablePatchLabelsAndDescription`), expiration, fields added at the schema's end, column descriptions, and REQUIRED columns relaxed to NULLABLE (`TestBigQueryEditTableSchema`); the emulator alone showed an added column in `tables.get` and never wrote it (measured, #1013), and the front adds it to the table since #1010 and #1036. **Create table** with the **VIEW** type sends `tables.insert` with the view's query as GoogleSQL; a view with a schema, or a table with a view query, is refused (`TestConsoleBigQueryReadWriteEditorRunsDDLAndScripts`, `TestConsoleBigQueryEditTableViewsAndInsertOptions`, `TestTheBigQueryReadWriteEditorScreensStatements`, `TestBigQueryWriteReportSendsJobsQuery`, `TestBigQueryCreateViewAndEditTable`, `TestBigQueryInsertRowsOptions`, `TestBigQueryFieldNamesAreFlexible`; in headless Chrome, `TestBigQueryReadWriteEditorThroughTheForms` and `TestBigQueryFlexibleNamesInsertOptionsEditTableAndViewThroughTheForms`). **Every project** (#1764): BigQuery serves every ACTIVE project of Resource Manager, and each page reads and writes the project chosen in the toolbar (`TestBigQueryServesEveryResourceManagerProject` browses another project's datasets and tables); a project BigQuery does not serve shows a prompt naming Resource Manager rather than an error or an empty table (`TestConsoleBigQueryDatasetsSchemaAndQuery`, `TestConsoleBigQueryDatasetTableAndRowWrites`, `TestBigQueryWritesGoToTheChosenProject`, `TestBigQueryConsoleShowsTheAPIsRefusals`; in headless Chrome, in the emulators shard's browser step, `TestBigQueryCreateTableInsertRowsAndDeleteThroughTheForms` and `TestBigQueryRecordColumnsThroughTheSchemaEditor`). **Loads, exports and Job history** (#993): **Load from Cloud Storage** on a dataset's and a table's page and **Export to Cloud Storage** on a table's page offer only what the validating front serves and CloudBurrow's compat tests exercise (docs/compatibility.md, BigQuery): a load's `gs://` URIs, CSV, JSON, Parquet, (since #1457) Avro with Use Avro logical types, or (since #1713) ORC, WRITE_APPEND or WRITE_TRUNCATE, a schema or auto-detect, and the CSV options of #944, #945 and #952; an export to one or more URIs (More destination URIs) as CSV, JSON, Avro or Parquet, with the compressions each format allows (GZIP, DEFLATE, SNAPPY, ZSTD), Avro logical types, a delimiter and the header row. A load's WRITE_EMPTY is not offered (`TestBigQueryLoadFormBecomesTheLoadsSource`); an ORC load from the form is `TestConsoleBigQueryLoadORCThroughTheForm`. A combination the front refuses is sent and refused in the API's words; each job that runs is answered with a link to its page. **Job history** is BigQuery's second page, `/bigquery-jobs` (not under `/bigquery/`, where it would shadow a dataset named `jobs`): every job of the served project from `jobs.list`, and each job's page from `jobs.get` with its state, times, `errorResult`, what it was asked to do, the load statistics it reports, its `status.errors` and its configuration as the API gave it. **Delete job** asks for the job's ID back. **Cancel job** is offered only on a job that is not DONE, and every job the emulator and the front run is done when it is answered, so none offers it (`TestConsoleBigQueryLoadExportAndJobHistory`; in headless Chrome, `TestBigQueryLoadExportAndJobHistoryThroughTheForms`). **Load from a file** (#999), on a dataset's and a table's page, is Load from Cloud Storage's form with a file control where the URIs are; the file and the action go together to the console's action upload route (`POST /api/actions/bigquery/upload`), under the upload limit in Settings, and are streamed to the front through the official Go client (`ReaderSource`: multipart up to 4 MiB, resumable past it), answered with the job (`TestConsoleBigQueryLoadFromAFile`; in headless Chrome, `TestBigQueryLoadFromAFileThroughTheForm`). A query job's page shows its statement type and, for DML, its affected, inserted, updated and deleted rows, each only when the job reported it (#1041, `TestConsoleBigQueryJobPageShowsDMLRows`) **Row access policies and Permissions** (#1218): the tab lists a table's policies from `rowAccessPolicies.list` through the generated client, and says CloudBurrow takes every caller to be its credentials' account, `cloudburrow-local@PROJECT.iam.gserviceaccount.com`, so that a policy's rows are those a query returns here (docs/compatibility.md, Row-level security); Preview of a table with policies shows the front's refusal of `tabledata.list`. The Permissions tabs are not the Permissions tab of #793: BigQuery stores no IAM policies here (ADR-0006), so they show the API's 501 in its words and offer no Grant access (`TestConsoleBigQueryRowAccessPolicies`, `TestBigQueryRowAccessPoliciesTab`). **Analysis rules** (#1580): a table's or view's `privacy_policy`, which the Go client has no field for, is read from `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TABLE_OPTIONS` and shown as an Analysis rules group of the Details tab, one line per rule (Aggregation threshold, Differential privacy, Join restriction) with its settings by name. Google's console offers no form for analysis rules (the analysis rules page sets them only with the DDL's `privacy_policy` option; that its console shows them in Details is UNVERIFIED (docs re-read 2026-10-17: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/analysis-rules>)), so they are read-only here and the group's **Edit analysis rules** line names the statement that changes them, `ALTER VIEW` (or `ALTER TABLE`) `… SET OPTIONS (privacy_policy = …)`, run in the query editor's Read-write mode, which the front then enforces (`TestAnalysisRuleProps`; in headless Chrome, `TestBigQueryAnalysisRulesOnTheTablePage`) **Data governance tags** (#1456): a column's `dataGovernanceTagsInfo`, which the Go client's `FieldSchema` has no field for, is read from `tables.get` over REST into the generated client's `Table` and shown in a Data governance tags column of the Schema tab (`key: value`), only when a column has some. Google's console shows them and has no form to bind or unbind them ("You can use the Google Cloud console to view data governance tags on columns, but not to bind or unbind them", docs re-read 2026-10-17: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/tags#data-governance-tags>), so they are read-only here and the tab's note names `ALTER TABLE … ALTER COLUMN … SET OPTIONS (data_governance_tags = …)` in Read-write mode, or `tables.patch` (`TestGovernanceTagsByField`; in headless Chrome, `TestBigQueryDataGovernanceTagsOnTheSchemaTab`); an **object table** (#1783) is listed as `EXTERNAL` on its dataset's page and its page shows the fixed schema, the connection, the Cloud Storage URIs, the metadata cache mode and max staleness, and a Preview read by a query (`tabledata.list` does not read an external table), with **Refresh metadata cache** (`CALL BQ.REFRESH_EXTERNAL_METADATA_CACHE`) for a `MANUAL` cache and no write actions (it is read-only); a dataset's **Create object table** makes one by `tables.insert` of an `objectMetadata` `externalDataConfiguration` (in headless Chrome, `TestBigQueryObjectTablePage`) |
| Memorystore | The Instances page (#1302): every instance (location, version, endpoint, tier, capacity, AUTH, state), read through the admin API's `ListInstances`; an instance's page, with its AUTH string; **Create instance** (ID, display name, region, capacity, AUTH, labels, Redis configs) through `CreateInstance`; **Edit** (display name, capacity, labels, Redis configs) through `UpdateInstance`; **Delete** through `DeleteInstance`, each waiting for its operation; **Export** and **Import** (confirmed) on a created instance's page, to and from a gs:// RDB file, through `ExportInstance` and `ImportInstance`, and Edit's **Persistence** (RDB snapshot period) through `persistence_config` (#1384); **Flush**, confirmed, runs FLUSHALL on the instance `up` runs ; **Upgrade** to a later version, and Create instance's **Version** (#1386); Create instance's **Tier**, **Enable read replicas** and **Replicas**, a Standard tier instance's read endpoint, zones and nodes, **Failover** (confirmed, with the data protection mode) through `FailoverInstance`, and Edit's **Replicas** or **Enable read replicas** (#1385); Create instance's **Enable in-transit encryption**, and an instance's **In-transit encryption** and **Server CA** tab (#1413); Create instance's **Customer-managed encryption key (CMEK)**, an instance's **Encryption** and, while its key cannot be used, **Suspension reasons**, and **Rotate server CA** (confirmed) on an instance with in-transit encryption, Memorystore's rotation on a support request (#1463). The **Memorystore Redis Cluster** (`/memorystore-clusters`) and **Memorystore for Valkey** (`/memorystore-valkey`) pages (#1519): clusters and instances listed, **Create** (shards, replicas, node type, PSC network, configs, **Enable in-transit encryption**, **IAM authentication** (the token checked since #1614: a page with it names the connect role to grant on the IAM page, and a cluster's **Edit** turns it on or off), **Token authentication**, an ACL policy, persistence, a CMEK key, **Restore from backup**, and for Valkey the engine version, the mode and **Secondary of**), **Edit** and **Delete**; a page's In-transit encryption and Authorization modes and, with in-transit encryption, its **Server CA** tab (#1549, `TestMemorystoreClusterServerCATabsThroughThePage`, #1591); **Token auth** and **Backups** tabs with **Add token auth user**, **Add auth token**, **Back up now**, **Export backup** and their deletes, and a cluster's **ACL policies** tab with **Create**, **Update**, **Delete** and **Set ACL policy** (#1549, `TestMemorystoreTokenAuthBackupsAndACLThroughThePage`); a Valkey secondary's replication, **Detach**, **Start migration** and **Finish migration** (#1557); Create instance's **Advanced network options** (Connection, IP range), **Edit** as a page of its own saved with **Save**, and every page's values in the create pages' words (#1862) | No maintenance, which is never scheduled. The instance `memorystore` (the server `up` runs) has no Edit or Delete. Flush is the server's, not an admin API method |
| APIs & Services | Google's menu (#1866): **Enabled APIs & services**, the Traffic, Errors and Median latency charts over the project's enabled APIs with Requests, Errors and Latency, the project's own calls over Google's time ranges (1 hour to 30 days); each API's page (Overview with Traffic by response code, Metrics with Google's default graphs and **Select Graphs**, Quotas & System Limits, Credentials) with **Disable API**, confirmed; the **Library**'s search and categories, each API with **Enable** or **Manage**; **Quotas & System Limits** with **Edit quotas** over the Cloud Quotas API, **Domain verification** over the Site Verification API, **Google Auth Platform** (Branding, Audience, Clients, Data Access; #2030, `TestQuotasThroughThePage`, `TestDomainVerificationThroughThePage`, `TestGoogleAuthPlatformThroughThePages`); **Credentials** with **Create credentials** (**API key**, **OAuth client ID**), the project's **API Keys** (each key's page with its name, application and API restrictions, **Show key**, **Rotate key** and **Delete**, and **Restore deleted credentials**, #1915) and the project's service accounts (`TestConsoleAPIsStudio`, `TestConsoleAPIsPerProjectHistory`, `TestServiceUsageDisableAndEnableThroughThePage`, `TestAPIsMetricsPerProjectOverATimeRange`, `TestAPIsCredentialsAndUnservedPages`, `TestConsoleAPIKeys`, `TestAPIKeysThroughTheCredentialsPage`); the **Service Usage operations** page lists a project's operations, each done, with **Delete** (confirmed) (#1530, `TestConsoleServiceUsageOperations`, `TestServiceUsageOperationsThroughThePage`) | Only the APIs this instance serves; a disabled API's calls fail with SERVICE_DISABLED (#1914). Counts start when the instance started, and a call that named no project counts in the default project. Quotas, domain verification and Google Auth Platform since #2030; Page usage agreements says "Not available locally" |
| Cloud SQL for MySQL | Cloud SQL's **MySQL** page (`/cloudsql-mysql`, beside **PostgreSQL** under one Cloud SQL product, #868): the one server, named with its version in the list's note; its databases (character set, collation, tables, size), tables, columns and indexes, views, routines, users, server settings, live activity, SQL editor (read-only by default; an opt-in, confirmed **Read-write** mode runs one statement as the application's user, #995); create database (`CREATE DATABASE`, granted to the application's user) and drop database (`DROP DATABASE`); **Add user** (name, password, host, `%` by default) and **Delete user** on a database's page (#1301) | A real MySQL, which the Cloud SQL Admin API serves as the instance `mysql` (#1301): Add user and Delete user run what `users.insert` and `users.delete` run, and a console-made user logs in with its password (`TestConsoleCloudSQLUsersMatchTheAdminAPI`); no database flags. MySQL's system databases are neither listed nor opened, and dropping the database the server was initialised with is refused. The editor runs each statement as `cloudburrow_console`, an account the screen creates with only `SELECT` and `SHOW VIEW` on the database queried, inside `START TRANSACTION READ ONLY`: the transaction alone does not stop DDL in MySQL, which commits implicitly before it, so MySQL refuses writes and DDL by privilege (error 1142) (`TestConsoleCloudSQLMySQLSchemaAndReadOnlyQuery`, `TestConsoleCloudSQLMySQLCreateAndDropDatabase`; in headless Chrome, in the emulators shard's browser step, `TestCloudSQLMySQLCreateQueryAndDropThroughThePage`). Read-write runs as `cloudburrow`, the application's user, with its privileges and MySQL's autocommit, never as `cloudburrow_console`, which is not widened: afterwards the read-only mode still refuses a write with 1142; the answer is MySQL's rows-affected count or its own error (`TestConsoleCloudSQLMySQLReadWriteEditor`, read back with `go-sql-driver/mysql`; in headless Chrome `TestCloudSQLReadWriteEditorsThroughThePage`) |
| Cloud SQL instances | Cloud SQL's **Instances** page (`/cloudsql-instances`, #1330): every instance the Admin API serves, the servers `up` runs and the ones created through it, with state, connection name, version, tier, address and deletion protection; **Create instance** (ID, database version from the images the instance runs, PostgreSQL 9.6 to 18 and MySQL 8.0 and 8.4 (#1462), tier, MySQL root password, zonal availability, deletion protection, point-in-time recovery), **Delete**, and **Restart** on a created instance; each instance's page with its settings, **Edit instance** on a created one (tier, labels, database flags, deletion protection, zonal availability, automated backups and their start time, retained automated backups, point-in-time recovery and its transaction log retention days), a **Backups** tab with **Create backup**, **Restore** and **Delete backup**, and **Clone**; on an instance with point-in-time recovery, its earliest and latest recovery times and **Point-in-time restore**; **Create read replica** on a primary's page, and **Promote**, **Stop replication** and **Start replication** on a replica's, which shows its primary as the primary's page shows its replicas; on a REGIONAL instance, its zone and secondary zone and **Failover** (#1408); **Export** and **Import** with Parallel, Threads and masterData, **Upgrade** with Include replicas, and **Clone to binary log position** on a MySQL instance with binary logging (#1421); **Run SQL (Data API)**, **Pre-check upgrade**, **Truncate log** on MySQL, **Delete with final backup**, and **Edit**, **Restore** and **Delete project backup** over the project-level Backups API (#1462, `TestConsoleCloudSQLDataAPIAndProjectBackups`) | Through the Admin API itself, so the page shows what gcloud and Terraform see: a created instance is a server of its own, listed PENDING_CREATE until it serves. Delete is refused, in the API's words, by deletion protection and for the instances `up` runs (`postgres`, `mysql`). Edit instance sends one `instances.patch` with every setting the form holds; the instances `up` runs offer no Edit. A backup is a dump of the server's databases kept on the host, and Restore replaces every database of the instance with the backup's, confirmed first; Clone copies the instance's databases as they are now; Point-in-time restore makes a new instance holding them as they were at the time given, recovered from the engine's archived WAL or binary log (docs/cloudsql.md). A read replica is a server replicating from its primary by the engine's own replication (docs/cloudsql.md); Promote is confirmed first, since it ends replication for good. Zonal availability is `settings.availabilityType`: REGIONAL adds a standby replicated synchronously, ZONAL removes it; Failover is `instances.failover` with the instance's settingsVersion, confirmed first, and the connection name stays (docs/cloudsql.md) (`TestCloudSQLConsoleHighAvailability`, `TestConsoleCloudSQLInstancesCreateRestartAndDelete`, `TestConsoleCloudSQLInstanceEditAndBackups`, `TestConsoleCloudSQLReadReplica`, `TestConsoleCloudSQLPointInTimeRestore`) Stop and Start are confirmed as Google's, "Stop database instance?" and "Start database instance?" with nothing typed; the create page's Customize groups are Google's (Encryption, Connections, Security and Query insights each their own), and Enable password policies' options are in Google's Set password policy dialog, ending in Save, on Create and Edit (#1860, `TestCloudSQLConsoleRound3`, `TestCloudSQLConsoleRound3DialogsAndCreateGroups`). |
| Backup and DR | Google's menu (#1844): **Overview** (protection summary, the last 24 hours' jobs, and the page's own **Find protected resources**, **Show trial**, **Subscribe to trial**, **End trial** and **Check Assured Workloads**, #1536); **Vaulted backups** (each protected Cloud SQL instance, with **Filter table** > **Resource type**; **Schedule backup**; each row's menu with **Create on-demand backup**, **Change backup plan** and **Remove backup plan**; a resource's page with its backups, **Create on-demand backup**, **Change backup plan**, **Remove backup plan**, **Restore** over the source or an existing instance, or to a new instance (#1922), through Cloud SQL's `instances.restoreBackup` with `backupdrBackup`, **Point-in-time restore** through `instances.pointInTimeRestore`, and a backup's **Delete**, disabled with the reason inside its enforced retention, #1844); **Backup plans** (**Create Backup plan**, a plan's page with its rules, resources and revisions, **Edit** (Google's Edit backup plan page, ending in **Save Changes**), **Delete**); **Backup plan associations**; **Backup vaults** (**Create a backup vault**, a vault's page with Google's details, **Edit minimum enforced retention**, **Edit vault**, **Delete**); **Jobs** (backup and restore jobs with Current failures and Google's time range list, System Jobs, from the job logs, #1924); **Reports** "Not available locally" (`TestConsoleBackupDRGooglePages`, `TestBackupDRScheduleAndRestoreThroughThePages`, `TestBackupDRRestoreToANewInstanceAndJobsThroughThePages`, `TestBackupDRConsoleRound2ThroughThePages`) | Cloud SQL instances, and Compute Engine VMs and disks (#2075: Resource Type Compute Engine and Disk in Create Backup plan, Schedule backup and Filter table; a VM's or disk's page restores to a new VM or disk, `TestBackupDRComputeRestoresThroughThePages`); other workload types (AlloyDB, Filestore) and management servers are not served (docs/backupdr.md); Reports: Google's are Looker Studio over a BigQuery sink of the job logs, which no local instance runs (#1924) |
| Certificate Authority Service | The Certificate Authority Service page (`/security/cas`, #1834): a project's CA pools in every region (tier, CAs, labels), read through the service in process; **Create pool** (ending in Done, #1933) (region, tier, maximum certificate lifetime, allowed request types, publishing of CA certificates and CRLs, a requester-specified not-before time, identity constraints with a CEL expression, passthrough extensions, a customer-managed encryption key, labels); a pool's page with its **Certificate authorities**, its **Certificates**, its issuance policy and its **CA certificates** (`FetchCaCerts`), **Create CA** (a key algorithm or a Cloud KMS key version, a publishing bucket, a subject or a subject RDN sequence), **Request certificate from a CSR**, **Request certificate from a config** (each with a subject mode and a requested not-before time), **Edit labels**, **Delete pool** and a **Permissions** tab; a CA's page (state, subject, key algorithm, validity, PEM chain) with **Enable**, **Disable**, **Delete** (with or without the grace period), **Undelete** and **Edit labels**, each in the states that allow it; a certificate's page (subject, SANs, serial, validity, revocation, PEM, chain) with **Revoke** and **Edit labels** (`TestConsolePrivateCAPoolsCAsAndCertificates`, `TestPrivateCAThroughThePage`); **Create CA** of type SUBORDINATE, signed and activated by an issuing CA of this instance, or awaiting activation with its **CSR** shown and **Activate** offered for a third-party issuer; a CA's **Revocation lists** and current CRL, with **Edit CRL labels**; the **Certificate templates** page (`/security/cas/templates`) with **Create template** and **Edit template** (each with a CEL expression and passthrough extensions), **Delete template** and a **Permissions** tab, and a template on **Request certificate** (`TestConsolePrivateCASubordinatesTemplatesAndCRLs`) | A DevOps pool stores no certificates, so its Certificates section says so. A CA's key is the instance's own unless a Cloud KMS key version is named, and a CMEK pool needs the instance to serve Cloud KMS (docs/privateca.md) |
| Cloud Logging | Entries written through the Logging API, in the Logs Explorer beside pod logs (source `logging/<log>`); the Logs Explorer's scope can be a folder or organization (`folders/{n}`, `organizations/{n}`), showing that parent's own entries, as Google's refines its scope (#1395, `TestGcloudLoggingFolderAndOrganizationSinks`, `TestParentScopeShowsThatParentsOwnEntries`); a **Log name** picker listing the log names `ListLogs` answers for the project, folder or organization in scope (or the log view's parent), as Google's Log name filter does, picking one filtering to that log (#1554, `TestConsoleLogNamePicker`, `TestLogsExplorerLogNamePicker`); **Delete log** on such an entry of the selected project (#799); **Log-based metrics**, **Log router** (sinks) and **Exclusions** under Operations, each with list, detail, create, edit and delete (#1226) | Log storage reads a project's, folder's, organization's or billing account's buckets through its Parent picker (#1411), and the Log router a folder's or organization's sinks through the same picker, created and edited with **Include children**, **Intercept children** (its documented rules, include children and a project destination, are CreateSink's refusals shown on the form), the sink service and exclusion filters; a folder's and the organization's pages on Resource hierarchy have a **Log router** tab listing their sinks and a Log router link (#1572, `TestConsoleFolderAndOrganizationSinks`, `TestLogRouterFolderSinksThroughTheBrowser`), and the Logs Explorer reads through a log view (#1410). A metric's page counts the stored entries its filter matches over the last hour and day and says it produces no time series (Cloud Monitoring is not emulated); the sinks screen shows each sink's writer identity and delivery status (entries delivered, the last delivery, entries waiting for the hourly Cloud Storage file, the last error, #1404, and the entries a BigQuery sink wrote to its dataset's `export_errors` table, #1451, `TestConsoleSinkShowsExportErrors`); each change is the in-process MetricsServiceV2 or ConfigServiceV2 call, its refusal shown on the form (`TestConsoleLoggingMetrics`, `TestConsoleLoggingSinksAndExclusions`, `TestLoggingAdminMetricsThroughTheBrowser`). See the Logs row. Delete log is `DeleteLog` on the in-process service, confirmed by typing the log ID; Cancel discards without asking. It is not offered on pod logs or CloudBurrow's own request log, which the Logging API does not hold, and the page says so (`TestConsoleDeleteLogThroughDeleteLog`, `TestConsoleDeleteLogIsSeenByTheOfficialClient`) |
| Monitoring | Node CPU, memory, pods, network rate, filesystem; per-pod CPU and memory; per-product log rate; time-range control; API request rate, error rate and p50/p95 latency per measured service, Pub/Sub, Bigtable, Spanner, Firestore and Datastore among them (#1266, #1267, #1288), and each in-cluster emulator's container CPU and memory (#1267) | In memory only; a restart clears it. Spanner's, Firestore's and Datastore's calls are counted by the call front in each emulator's pod, so a pod's are as well as the host's (#1288; `TestFirestoreOnMonitoringChartsItsCallsAndContainer`, `TestSpannerOnMonitoringChartsItsCalls`) |
| Cloud Monitoring | Two pages beside **Monitoring** under its drawer row (#1939), over the Monitoring API v3 this instance serves in process: **Metrics explorer** (`/monitoring/metrics-explorer`) in the layout of Google's builder: **Select a metric** (a resource type from `ListMonitoredResourceDescriptors`, then a metric of it from `ListMetricDescriptors`), **Filter** (terms on `metric.labels.*` and `resource.labels.*`), **Aggregation** (the aligner and its alignment period, the reducer and the labels it groups by) and the time range (5 minutes to 1 week), drawn as a line chart of every series with the table of the series, their labels and latest value below, each Apply one `ListTimeSeries`; beside **Builder** its **PromQL** tab (#1991), Google's code editor: a PromQL query over the same series (Google's quoted metric names, as `{"custom.googleapis.com/shop/sales"}`, or the legacy `custom_googleapis_com:shop_sales`) and its minimum interval, **Run query** charting the range query over the selected time range with the table of the result series (`TestConsoleMonitoringExplorerPromQL`, `TestMonitoringPromQLThroughThePage`); **Metrics management** (`/monitoring/metrics-management`): the project's metric descriptors (kind, value type, unit, labels, description), **Create metric** for a user-defined `custom.googleapis.com` metric (GAUGE or CUMULATIVE, BOOL, INT64, DOUBLE, STRING or DISTRIBUTION, unit, labels) and **Delete metric** for a user-defined one (`TestConsoleMonitoringExplorerAndMetrics`, `TestMonitoringThroughThePage`) | A distribution is charted by its mean, as a line rather than Google's heatmap; a STRING metric is listed and not charted |
| Cloud Monitoring alerting, uptime checks and dashboards | Pages under Monitoring (#1964), over the same in-process API: **Alerting** (`/monitoring/alerting`): policies with their enabled state, severity, conditions, channels and open incidents, **Create policy** (one metric-threshold, metric-absence, log-match or **PromQL** condition, the PromQL one with its query, evaluation interval and retest window, its notification channels and **Repeat notifications** every 30 to 1440 minutes, #1991), a policy's page with its conditions, repeat notifications, incidents, snoozes and JSON, **Enable**, **Disable**, **Repeat notifications**, **Snooze** and **Delete policy**; **Incidents** (`/monitoring/alerting/incidents`): each incident's state, policy, condition, resource, start, end and summary, its page with the notifications sent for it, **Acknowledge** and **Close incident**; **Notification channels** (`/monitoring/alerting/channels`): **Create channel** (webhooks, Pub/Sub, email, SMS, Slack, PagerDuty, Google Chat), a channel's page with its recent deliveries, **Enable**, **Disable**, **Send verification code**, **Verify**, **Get verification code**, **Delete channel**; **Uptime checks** (`/monitoring/uptime`): each check's target, frequency, last result and latency, **Create uptime check** (HTTP, HTTPS or TCP), a check's results and latency chart, **Edit**, **Delete uptime check**; **Dashboards** (`/monitoring/dashboards`): **Create dashboard** from its JSON, a dashboard's page with a line chart per XY chart widget over the last hour and its JSON, **Edit JSON**, **Delete dashboard**; **Groups** (`/monitoring/groups`) with members and subgroups, **Services** (`/monitoring/services`) with **Create SLO** and **Delete SLO**, and on a service's page each SLO's status, compliance, error budget remaining and 1-hour burn rate with its **Service level indicator** and **Error budget** charts (#1991), and **Snoozes** (`/monitoring/snoozes`) with **Create snooze** and **End snooze** (`TestConsoleMonitoringAlertingChannelsAndIncidents`, `TestConsoleMonitoringUptimeDashboardsGroupsServices`, `TestMonitoringAlertingThroughThePage`, `TestMonitoringUptimeAndDashboardsThroughThePage`, `TestMonitoringAlertingUptimeAndDashboardsScreensMeetWCAG`, `TestConsoleMonitoringPromQLPolicyAndRenotify`, `TestConsoleMonitoringSLOCompliance`, `TestMonitoringPromQLAlertAndRenotifyThroughThePage`, `TestMonitoringSLOComplianceThroughThePage`, `TestMonitoringPromQLSLOAndRenotifyScreensMeetWCAG`) | Create policy builds one condition; a policy of several conditions is created through the API and shown and toggled here. Webhooks reach only local URLs and email, SMS and the third-party channels are recorded, never sent, so a verification code is shown in the channel's deliveries. A dashboard draws its XY chart widgets as line charts and lists its other widgets. Repeat notifications go to every channel of the policy at one interval; a per-channel strategy is set through the API and shown here. An SLO with a basicSli has no values: its automatic metrics are not produced locally |
| Parameter Manager | With `--services parametermanager` (#2054), the **Parameter Manager** page in Secret Manager's menu (`/secrets-parameters`): a project's parameters in every location (location, format, encryption, labels, created); **Create parameter** (name, location, format Unformatted, YAML or JSON, Google-managed or Cloud KMS key, labels); a parameter's page with its **Versions** and **Overview** (its resource name, format, encryption, principal, labels), **New version** (payload, disabled), **Edit** (labels and key) and **Delete** (refused while it has versions); a version's page with its **Payload**, its **Rendered payload** (each `__REF__` replaced by the Secret Manager version, read as the parameter's principal) and **Overview**, **Disable** or **Enable**, and **Delete** (`TestConsoleParameterManager`, `TestParameterManagerThroughThePage`); the **Parameter templates** page, its Templates tab (`/secrets-parameters/templates`, #2063): a project's templates in every location (location, format, labels, created); **Create template** (name, location, YAML or JSON, labels); a template's page with its **Versions** and **Overview**, **New version** (payload, disabled), **Edit** (labels) and **Delete** (with its versions, as Google's "Deleting a template also deletes all of its template versions", #2143); a version's page with its **Payload** and **Overview**, **Render**, Google's Render template version page (a JSON or YAML parameter, then its enabled version; Render opens a page with the payload and the rendered value), **Disable** or **Enable**, and **Delete** (`TestConsoleParameterTemplates`, `TestParameterTemplatesThroughThePage`). The Parameter Manager API's **Quotas & System Limits** tab lists its read, write and render quotas with Edit quota for the project (`TestConsoleAPIQuotas`, `TestRequestQuotasThroughThePage`); laid out as Google's in the round of 2026-11-22 (§4.7, Parameter Manager) | The payload is edited in a text area, without Google's JSON and YAML editor (its Secret picker, Ctrl+Space, is served); tags are set at creation through the API only, as Google's tags page documents them (REST) |
| Workflows | With `--services workflows` (#1986), the **Workflows** page: a project's workflows in every region (state, revision, last updated, description, labels); **Create workflow** with Google's fields in its order (name, region, description, service account, **Encryption**: Google-managed or a **Cloud KMS key** chosen with Browse from the region's keys, **Call log level**, **Execution history** Basic or Detailed, **Environment variables**, labels), the **Triggers** section (#2011: **Add new trigger** for an **Eventarc** trigger, Google's trigger name, event type and its filters and service account, made through Eventarc's API with the workflow its destination; or a **Cloud Scheduler** trigger, Google's name, region, frequency, timezone, workflow's argument, workflow's call log level and service account, made as the Cloud Scheduler job Google makes, posting to `workflowexecutions.googleapis.com`, which this instance's scheduler sends to its own Workflow Executions; a row that would be refused makes nothing) and its **workflow editor**: the source, YAML or JSON, with line numbers and its syntax highlighted (keys, strings, `${…}` expressions, numbers, comments), the **workflow graph** beside it redrawn as it is typed (Start, each step with its kind, the next-step arrows, switch branches and `next` jumps labelled with their condition, `for`, `parallel`, `try` and nested `steps` as collapsible blocks, return and raise to End; zoom in, zoom out, reset), a source that does not parse naming its line (#2004); a workflow's page with its **Details** (call log level, execution history, encryption, environment variables), **Executions**, **Source** (highlighted, read only, beside its graph), **Revisions**, **Triggers** (its Eventarc triggers and Cloud Scheduler jobs, #2011) and **Logs** (its executions' entries) tabs, **Execute** (input as JSON, call log level, **Execution history level**: Basic or Detailed over the workflow's, #2011), **Edit** (the Create fields, Add new trigger among them; name and region shown and fixed; a changed source deploys a new revision) and **Delete**; an execution's page with Google's **Summary** (execution state, duration, revision, times, call log level, execution history level, current step, **Input** and **Output**, the result or the error's context and payload), **Steps** (the **Visualization** panel, expandable, of the revision that ran with each step marked succeeded ✓, failed ! or in progress, over the step entries: step, routine, type, state, times, exception; and Google's **step detail panel** (#2011): the chosen step entry's details, a for loop's iteration, its **Variables** with Detailed history, and its **Parent**, **Children**, **Previous** and **Next** entries, each a link that moves the panel to that entry) and **Logs** (the execution's Cloud Logging entries: start and end, `sys.log` and call logs, newest first), and **Cancel** while it runs (`TestConsoleWorkflows`, `TestConsoleWorkflowsTriggersHistoryAndSteps`, `TestWorkflowsThroughTheBrowser`, `TestWorkflowsScreensMeetWCAG`) | Add new trigger is two buttons (Eventarc, Cloud Scheduler) adding a row each in the form, not a menu opening a side pane; an Eventarc trigger is in this instance's one Eventarc location and offers Google sources only; the step detail panel chooses its entry from a Step entry list above the table rather than by clicking a table row; the graph is CloudBurrow's own drawing of the steps, not Google's layout pixel for pixel |
| Logs | Live stream, severity timeline, filters in the URL, per-resource tab on every detail page | Severity is **inferred** from the line; container logs carry none |
| Local AI | **Local models** page (#1306): each catalogued model's download state and size; **Download** (a pinned, ungated model only, its exact size confirmed, every file checked against its pinned SHA-256), **Cancel**, **Load** and **Unload** (the embedding runtime only), **Delete** the local copy | See §6. The Model Garden screen lists catalogued models and opens one for its provenance. A model with no pin, or a gated one, is listed and never downloaded; the generation model is the container `-local-ai-model` starts, shown as loaded with no unload |
| Document AI and Speech-to-Text | **Document AI** (#1930): Overview, Processor gallery (Google's cards under category headings; Enterprise Document OCR and Layout Parser with Create processor; every other type says *Not available locally* and why), My processors (Create processor, Enable processor, Disable processor, Delete) and a processor's page (Overview; Manage versions with Default version, Deploy, Undeploy and Delete; Upload test document with Google's process options (version, pages, symbols, layout config), opening the analysis page (#2084) that draws the layout's boxes over the page images beside the text and rows, with the layout parser's Chunks tab; Batch process from and to Cloud Storage). **Speech-to-Text** (#1930): Transcriptions (Google's New transcription: Audio configuration with its Workspace, a Cloud Storage bucket chosen or made with New workspace, a Local upload for Recognize or a Cloud storage file for BatchRecognize, then Transcription options, recognizer, Spoken language and Model; the audio, transcript and metadata written to the workspace and listed from it; Transcription details playing the stored audio, #2084), Recognizers (Create recognizer, a recognizer's page, Edit, Transcribe, Delete, Undelete), Custom classes and Phrase sets (Create, a page, Edit, Delete, Undelete) | Served by the local AI endpoint (#1799), which `up` starts with a local model or with Cloud Run; the pages are listed only when it runs. See §6 |
| Vertex AI custom prediction | **Online prediction** page (#869), where Cloud Run is enabled | Pick a deployed custom prediction container (a Cloud Run service with `AIP_*` variables), edit JSON instances and parameters, send, and see the container's status and body verbatim; **Explain** with a Sampled Shapley path count and feature baselines, and the attributions per feature (#1454). Vertex's `Endpoint`, the explanation methods that need gradients or an Example index, and batch prediction are not served, and the page names them; see [playground.md](playground.md) §8 |
| Instance (Management tools; also Settings and utilities) | **Save state**, the archive `cloudburrow state save` writes, as a download, with the manifest's list of the services it captures and those it does not, each with its reason; **Load state**, an uploaded archive of up to 5 GiB (its own limit, not the upload limit in Settings, so an archive Save state wrote loads back; #1250), confirmed by typing the instance's name and naming the services the archive replaces; **Reset**, every service or those chosen, optionally in one project, with **Reseed**, confirmed by typing the scope (the project, else the services, else `all`); **Seed**, an uploaded seed document, with **If not exists** (#801) | **Not a Google Cloud console screen**, so no parity is claimed: these are `cloudburrow state`, `reset` and `seed`. Only what `GET /admin/instance` says the admin API accepts is offered: a component that cannot be reset by project is disabled while a project is named, and Reseed is disabled with a project or without a startup seed. Every action is the admin API's, called in process with the admin token, pasted once per tab, because a workload can reach the console on Docker Desktop and the console adds no token of its own ([networking.md](networking.md#the-console-and-the-admin-token)); a refusal shows its message. Each action is in the operations ledger with its outcome, never the archive or the document. **Cancel** on a confirmation closes it and sends nothing. `TestConsoleInstanceActsThroughTheAdminAPI`, `TestConsoleStateSaveResetLoadRestores`, `TestConsoleLoadsTheArchiveItSavedOverTheUploadLimit`, `TestStateLoadIsNotHeldToTheUploadLimit`, `TestInstanceResetConfirmedByTypingTheScope` |

**An unsupported cloud feature is rendered as an explicit unavailable state**, never as a
working-looking control and never as a plausible number. Concretely, the following are
forbidden on any operational screen:

- A button that does nothing, or that opens a form which cannot succeed.
- A metric, chart, cost figure or count that is not read from live local state.
- A placeholder row, a lorem-ipsum resource, or a spinner that never resolves.
- A status badge whose value is not derived from the resource it describes.

An unavailable feature is shown disabled, with one sentence saying why and a link to the
matrix row that records it.

---

## 4. Screen-by-screen checklist

Each item is checkable against the references in §1. Items are **structural**; see §1 for why
none of them state pixel metrics.

### 4.1 Application shell — every screen

- [x] A **toolbar** across the top, the region the appearance documentation names.
- [x] Product name **CloudBurrow** at the left of the toolbar, with a navigation-menu trigger beside it.
- [x] A **project selector** in the toolbar showing the current project. It opens the **registry** rather than only projects that hold resources: scanning could not show a project with nothing in it and could not offer to make one, so the console could never answer "which projects are there". It also validates the selection — a `?project=` naming a project that is not registered used to leave every per-project screen reporting an error apiece with nothing saying the project was the problem.
- [x] A **search** input in the toolbar. It matches resource names, every column each list screen shows, product names and log entries, and shows results as you type.
- [x] A **notifications** control showing in-progress and recent operations.
- [x] A **Settings and utilities** control (`more_vert`), carrying at minimum the theme choice.
- [x] A persistent, non-dismissible **`LOCAL`** indicator (§2).
- [x] A **navigation menu** listing only the services in §3, each linking to its list screen. Kubernetes Engine, Agent Platform (formerly Vertex AI), Pub/Sub and Cloud Run are one drawer row each with their pages inside, because a bare "Services" or "Jobs" row sitting beside Cloud Run is ambiguous with Cloud Run's own.
- [x] **Light / Dark / Same as device** themes, with **no page reload on change** — the documented behaviour.
- [x] The current service and screen are visibly marked in the navigation.
- [x] An **Activate terminal** control in the toolbar, Cloud Shell's place: a drawer at the foot of the page that can be resized from its grip (by pointer or arrow keys), minimised to its header and closed, and that reopens to the same shell until the shell ends. The drawer has **tabs**, as Cloud Shell's does (#834): **+** opens another shell in the same pod (files and tools are shared, as Cloud Shell's tabs share one VM), each tab is its own session with its own output and idle timeout, a tab is renamed by double-click or F2 and closed by its x or Delete (ending only its shell, with no confirmation), and tabs are chosen by click, the arrow keys in the tab strip, or Alt+PageUp / Alt+PageDown from inside the terminal. A reload or a closed drawer keeps every tab with its recent output; a console keeps at most 8 tabs and says so when asked for a ninth; a project switch is announced in every tab. The shell runs in a pod in the instance's cluster, never on this machine, scoped to the toolbar's project, and the drawer says when the project changes. When no shell can be had the drawer says why, never a blank terminal. What differs from Cloud Shell — no Google sign-in, no home directory beyond the pod, no Terraform, a read-only kubectl — is in [compatibility.md](compatibility.md#console).

### 4.2 Resource list screens

- [x] Page title matching the documented console page name: **Buckets**, **Topics**, **Subscriptions**, **Queues**, **Services**, **Secrets**, **Workloads**.
- [x] A primary **create** action labelled as the documentation labels it — **Create**, **Create topic**, **Create queue**, **Deploy container** — from `Creator.CreateForm`, so the label comes from the provider that will perform it. The `add_box` icon is **not** used: the icons here are CloudBurrow's own line drawings (§1), and CloudBurrow does not ship Material Symbols.
- [x] A table with a header row, sortable by any column, with a filter input above it.
- [x] Pagination, with the page size selectable — and, where a provider can continue a read, a control that fetches the rows past the backend's own bound rather than a note saying they exist.
- [x] Row selection, and a delete action that is disabled until something is selected.
- [x] A refresh control, plus a bounded automatic poll so a list does not go quietly stale.

### 4.3 The four states every data screen must have

Not optional, and each is a separate check because each is separately easy to get wrong:

- [x] **Loading** — a skeleton or progress indicator, never an empty table that looks like "none".
- [x] **Empty** — says the collection is empty and offers the create action **where there is one**. It used to say "Create one here" on every empty screen, including the read-only ones, naming a button that was not there and could not be. The listing's own note is kept on the empty path, which is the one path where it is the only thing on the page.
- [x] **Error** — states what failed and offers retry. Never a silent empty table, which is the failure mode that makes a developer debug their own code.
- [x] **Operating** — an in-flight create or delete shows progress, and the affected row shows it too.

### 4.4 Detail screens

- [x] Resource name as the page title, with one breadcrumb node per level, every one above the last a working link.
- [x] Tabs where the console documents tabs — Cloud Run's **Containers, Networking, Security** grouping is the documented example. The mechanism ships: `Driller` returns named sections, the strip carries `tablist`/`tab`/`tabpanel` semantics with arrow-key movement, and the selected tab is in the URL. Cloud SQL databases use it (**Tables** / **Schemas**); the other four drillable products declare one section each and correctly render no strip. Cloud Run's own grouping is a create-form grouping, and that one is `Section` on `Field` — see the Container section on `/run/create`.
- [x] Only fields CloudBurrow actually stores. A field the backend does not hold is absent, not blank. `TestNoDetailShowsABlankProperty` builds every screen from the same registry a running instance serves (`consoleProviders`), backs each with its in-process server or official fake (the built-in Cloud Storage, pstest, bttest, the Cloud Tasks, Secret Manager, KMS, Scheduler and Resource Manager services, and a fixture `kubectl` for Cloud Run and the cluster screens), seeds the sparsest resources each API accepts, opens every row and every openable row beneath it, and fails on any property whose value is empty or whitespace, naming the screen, the resource path and the label. A new screen is walked without being listed; the six whose backend is an emulator or database container (Firestore, Datastore, Spanner, BigQuery, and Cloud SQL for PostgreSQL and for MySQL) are exempted by name with the reason. An unset field is left out, or worded for what unset means ("Not scheduled yet", "None: no revision is ready"), as unset scaling settings are; a label, header or attribute held with an empty value says "(empty value)" rather than vanishing. The server also drops any blank property before serving a page (`TestTheServerDropsBlankProperties`), which is the backstop for the six exempted screens.

### 4.5 Create forms

Fields and labels follow the documented forms, restricted to what CloudBurrow supports:

- [x] **Bucket**: Bucket name; Labels; under **Choose a storage class for your data**, the default storage class (STANDARD, NEARLINE, COLDLINE or ARCHIVE); under **Choose how to control access to objects**, uniform bucket-level access, on by default, fine-grained access absent because there are no ACLs; under **Choose how to protect object data**, object versioning, the soft delete retention (7 days by default) and object retention, which can be enabled only at creation (#852). With more than three fields the form is its own page. `TestConsoleStorageCreateBucketOptions`; in headless Chrome, `TestStorageCreateBucketOptionsThroughTheForm`. Location is **absent** rather than fixed-and-shown: the backend has one location and a control that offered a choice it ignores would be a control that does nothing. The field's help says so.
- [x] **Topic**: Topic ID; **Add a default subscription** checkbox, defaulted on — a topic with no subscription drops every message published to it; the **Message retention duration**; the **Schema**, an ID of the project's or a full name, and its **Message encoding**, JSON or BINARY (#852). The emulator enforces the schema on publish. **Labels** (#962), one `key=value` per line, checked in the browser against Google's label rules, line by line, before anything is sent. `TestConsolePubSubCreateOptions`, `TestPubSubFormsHoldLabelsToGoogleRules`; in headless Chrome, `TestPubSubCreateOptionsThroughTheForms` and `TestPubSubLabelsAreCheckedOnTheForms`.
- [x] **Subscription**: not a form on the Subscriptions screen, which offers no create action. A subscription is created from its topic's page (**Create subscription**: Subscription ID; under **Delivery**, push endpoint, acknowledgement deadline, message retention duration, retain acknowledged messages, message ordering and filter; under **Retry policy** and **Dead lettering**, the same fields as Edit subscription, #852), because a subscription belongs to a topic and a form that asked for the topic again would be one more place to name the wrong one. Under **Delivery** too, **Exactly-once delivery** and the **Expiration period** (#873), 31d by default, `never`, or a duration of at least a day and no shorter than the retention, each with its help; exactly-once with a push endpoint is refused on the form. Under **Labels**, the subscription's labels (#962), held to Google's rules as Create topic's are. `TestConsolePubSubCreateOptions`, `TestPubSubFormsHoldLabelsToGoogleRules`; in headless Chrome, `TestPubSubCreateOptionsThroughTheForms`.
- [x] **Snapshot** (#787): not a form on the Snapshots screen, which offers no create action. **Create snapshot** is on a subscription's page: Snapshot ID only. Labels are not asked for: the emulator accepts them and does not keep them, and the field's help says so. **Edit snapshot** on the snapshot's page (#1406) takes its labels and expiry, applied by CloudBurrow's Pub/Sub front since the emulator's `UpdateSnapshot` is `UNIMPLEMENTED`. `TestConsolePubSubSnapshotsAndSeek`; in headless Chrome, `TestPubSubSnapshotSeekAndDeleteThroughTheForms`.
- [x] **Seek** (#787): **Seek to snapshot** (Snapshot ID, prefilled with one of the topic's snapshots, which its help lists) and **Seek to time** (an RFC 3339 time, prefilled with now) on a subscription's page, each labelled as changing which messages are redelivered. Submitting either asks for the subscription's name to be typed back, naming what the seek changes; Cancel there leaves the form open and sends nothing, and Cancel on the form discards it without asking (#783).
- [x] **Detach subscription** (#1406): **Detach from topic** on a subscription's page, destructive, its confirmation saying the subscription's retained messages are dropped, it receives nothing published afterwards and it is kept detached until deleted. `DetachSubscription` is carried out by CloudBurrow's Pub/Sub front, since the emulator refuses it. A detached subscription's page offers Edit subscription (#1446), which changes its kept configuration, and of the actions only Advance clock: a snapshot of it, or a seek to one, is refused `FAILED_PRECONDITION`. `TestConsolePubSubDetachAndProtobufSchema`.
- [x] **Schema** (#788): **Create schema** on the Schemas screen: Schema ID, a definition and a type, AVRO or PROTOCOL_BUFFER (#1406; the emulator refuses Protocol Buffer schemas, and CloudBurrow's Pub/Sub front serves them). The definition is checked with `ValidateSchema` before `CreateSchema`, so a malformed one is refused on the open form with the API's message ("InvalidArgument: Could not parse schema definition") and nothing is created. **Commit revision** on a schema's page is prefilled with the latest definition and validated the same way; **Test message** takes an encoding (JSON, or BINARY in base64) and a message and shows `ValidateMessage`'s verdict. Cancel discards without asking (#783). `TestConsolePubSubSchemas`; in headless Chrome, `TestPubSubSchemaCreateShowsTheValidationError`.
- [x] **Topic edit** (#786): **Edit topic** on the topic's page, the **Topic ID** shown disabled and the **Message retention duration** prefilled, the one field the emulator's `UpdateTopic` applies. **Labels** are edited too (#949), applied by CloudBurrow's front in place of the emulator's refusal, and held to Google's label rules on the form (#962, `TestPubSubFormsHoldLabelsToGoogleRules`; in headless Chrome, `TestPubSubLabelsAreCheckedOnTheForms`). A retention once set is required, because emptying it would save 31 days. A value the emulator refuses, such as `5m`, is refused on the form with its message. **Schema** and **Message encoding** (#1446) bind the topic to a schema, AVRO or PROTOCOL_BUFFER, or, emptied, clear it. `TestConsolePubSubEditTopicAndSubscription`; the schema, `TestConsolePubSubDetachAndProtobufSchema`.
- [x] **Subscription edit** (#786): **Edit subscription** on the subscription's page, prefilled from it, under **Delivery**, **Retry policy**, **Dead lettering** and **Not editable**. An empty push endpoint is a pull subscription, so giving one switches it to push and emptying it switches back; a BigQuery, Cloud Storage or Bigtable subscription is not offered the push fields. Only the fields that changed are sent, in one `UpdateSubscription`. **Exactly-once delivery** is a checkbox under **Delivery** (#880): the emulator applies a change to it and acts on it (`TestPubSubExactlyOnceCanBeChanged`), and with a push endpoint it is refused on the form. The ID, topic, filter, expiration and message ordering are shown disabled with the reason, and a request that changes one is refused. **Labels** are edited under **Labels** (#949), held to Google's label rules (#962). `TestConsolePubSubEditTopicAndSubscription` reads each change back with the official client; in headless Chrome, `TestPubSubEditSubscriptionThroughTheForm` and `TestPubSubEditSubscriptionExactlyOnceThroughTheForm`.
- [x] **Queue**: Queue name; Region, with its help saying CloudBurrow places nothing geographically.
- [x] **Queue edit** (#784): **Edit queue** on the queue's page, under **Rate limits** and **Retry parameters**, prefilled from the queue; **Max burst size** is shown disabled, because the API derives it. A value the API refuses (a max attempts below -1, a rate above 500) is refused with `UpdateQueue`'s own message on the form. `TestConsoleTasksEditQueueAndCreateTask`; in headless Chrome, `TestTasksEditQueueThroughTheForm`.
- [x] **Bucket edit** (#789): **Edit bucket** on a bucket's page, prefilled from the bucket: **Location** shown disabled; **Labels** one `key=value` per line; **Default storage class**; under **Protection**, **Object versioning**, **Soft delete retention (seconds)**, **Pause hard deletion** (`softDeletePolicy.hardDeletePause`, #1702: the Configuration tab shows it as **Hard delete pause** with the time it took effect, and while it holds the Deleted objects tab says its objects are kept past their hard delete time; Edit bucket and Delete bucket show the API's refusals of #1715: the pause changed again within 24 hours, soft delete turned off while paused, the paused bucket deleted; `TestStorageHardDeletePauseThroughTheConsole`), **Retention period (seconds)** (its help says, once locked, that it can only be increased) and **Default event-based hold**; under **Lifecycle and CORS**, the lifecycle `rule` array and the `cors` array as JSON. A value the API refuses (a soft delete retention of 5 seconds, an unknown lifecycle condition, removing a locked policy) is refused with `buckets.patch`'s own message on the form. **Cancel** discards without asking (#783). **Lock retention policy** is a confirmed action, not a form. `TestConsoleStorageBucketSettings`; in headless Chrome, `TestStorageBucketSettingsThroughTheForm`. In §8, `buckets.update` is excluded, being the replace-everything form of the edit made here through `buckets.patch`.
- [x] **Object actions** (#790), on an object's row menu and its page, each prefilled from the object: **Copy** (destination bucket, default this one; destination name; Replace), **Move or rename** (new name; Replace), **Edit metadata** (Content-Type, Cache-Control, Content-Disposition, custom metadata and object contexts (#1345), each one `key=value` per line), **Edit storage class**; and **Compose** in a bucket's or folder's action bar, enabled once objects are checked, with the checked names prefilled one per line (destination name, Content-Type, Replace). **Replace the destination if it exists** is off by default: an existing destination is refused with the API's `412` message on the form, and checked, submitting asks for the destination's name to be typed back first, whose Cancel sends nothing. Cancel on the form itself discards without asking (#783). `TestConsoleStorageObjectOperations`; in headless Chrome, `TestStorageObjectMetadataAndCopyThroughTheForms`. In §8, `rewrite`, `move`, `compose` and `patch` are this surface; `objects.copy` and `objects.update` are excluded, being the one-request and replace-everything forms of edits made here through `rewrite` and `patch`.
- [x] **Object versions, holds and retention** (#853): **Show versions** beside Upload file on a bucket's and folder's object list; each generation's row opens its own page and offers **Restore as live version** (noncurrent only) and **Delete version**, whose confirmation says it is not kept as noncurrent and asks for the object's name, and whose Cancel sends nothing. **Edit holds and custom time** on an object's page and a generation's, prefilled from it (**Temporary hold**, **Event-based hold** under **Holds**; **Custom time** RFC 3339). **Edit object retention** (Mode, Retain until, Override unlocked retention) only where the bucket has object retention; submitting it asks for the object's name back. **Run lifecycle now (CloudBurrow extension)** on a bucket's page, whose form says it is not a Google Cloud call and applies every bucket's rules, and whose dialog shows the run's counts.
