A refusal (removing a custom time, shortening an Unlocked retention without the override, a held object) is the API's message on the form. `TestConsoleStorageObjectVersionsHoldsAndRetention`; in headless Chrome, `TestStorageObjectVersionsThroughTheBrowser`.
- [x] **Notification** (#791): **Create notification** on a bucket's page: under **Destination**, **Pub/Sub topic**, a picker of the project's topics, and **Payload format** (`JSON_API_V1` or `NONE`); under **Event types**, a checkbox for each of `OBJECT_FINALIZE`, `OBJECT_METADATA_UPDATE`, `OBJECT_DELETE` and `OBJECT_ARCHIVE`, none checked meaning every type; under **Filter and attributes**, **Object name prefix** and **Custom attributes** one `key=value` per line. Not offered with no Pub/Sub, or in a project with no topic, and the Notifications tab says which. **Cancel** discards without asking (#783). `TestConsoleStorageNotifications` reads the notification back with the official client's `BucketHandle.Notifications` and pulls the message an upload then publishes with the official Pub/Sub client; in headless Chrome, `TestStorageNotificationsThroughTheForm`.
- [x] **Task** (#784): **Create task** on the queue's page: URL, HTTP method (default POST), headers, body, schedule time and task ID, the last two optional, and an Auth header, an OIDC or OAuth token (#1243). `TestConsoleTasksEditQueueAndCreateTask` reads the task back with the official client's `ListTasks` and receives the one due now at a test HTTP target.
- [x] **Document** (#796): **Start collection** on the Firestore screen (Collection ID, then the document's fields) and **Add document** on a collection's page: Document ID, optional, empty for an auto ID; a first field's name, type and value, optional. **Add field** on a document's page takes a name, type and value; **Edit field** on the field's page shows the name disabled and the type and value prefilled. The type is one of string, number, boolean, null, timestamp, geopoint, reference, bytes (base64, #995), map or array, refused on the form otherwise. `TestConsoleFirestoreDocumentCreateEditDelete` reads each back through the official `firestore` client with its type. A map or array field's page adds **Add value**, and each value on its **Elements** tab **Edit value** and **Remove value** (#995): `TestConsoleFirestoreEditValuesInsideMapsAndArrays` reads each change back through the official client, every other value as written.
- [x] **Entity** (#796): **Create entity** on the Datastore screen (Kind, then the entity's fields) and on a kind's page: Key identifier, optional — as the Name/ID column shows one, `name=alice` for a name and `id=123` for a numeric ID (a bare `alice` is a name too, so `name=id=7` is how the name `id=7` is written, #885), or empty for an allocated numeric ID; a first property's name, type, value and **Exclude from indexes**, optional. **Add property** and **Edit property** take the same, the edit with the name disabled and the rest prefilled. `TestConsoleDatastoreEntityCreateEditDelete` reads each back through the official `datastore` client with its type and index flag; `TestConsoleDatastoreNameIDTellsANameFromAnID` reads back the entity `name=id=7` makes. A **key** value is shown and prefilled in the same rendering (#887): `Kind/name=…` or `Kind/id=…` pairs, ancestors first, a slash or percent sign in a kind or name escaped (`%2F`, `%25`), and `__namespace__/{namespace}/` first for a key in a namespace other than the default (a bare `Customer/alice` is still read as a name). The older `Kind/name` form showed a key to the name `id=7` as `Order/id=7`, and saving it unchanged rewrote it to the numeric ID 7; now Edit property saved unchanged writes the same key back, for a name that looks like an ID, a name holding `/`, `%` or `=`, an ancestor and a namespace, and a key in a namespace is editable (`TestConsoleDatastoreKeyValuesRoundTripThroughEditProperty`, read back through the official client; unit `TestDatastoreKeyValuesRoundTripThroughEditProperty`; in headless Chrome, `TestDatastoreKeyValueEditPropertyIsANoOp`). A key naming an entity in **another project or database** (a key value's `PartitionId`, which the official Go client's `Key` does not hold) was read as this project's, and Add, Edit or Delete property, which wrote the whole entity back through the client, rewrote it to this project's default database without a word; the entity's pages now read it with `Lookup` and those three write it back with `Commit` in the same transaction, changing only the named property, and the key is shown as `Order/id=7 (project another-project)` or `(database another-db)` with no Edit property and a note saying why (#893). Inside an **array or embedded entity**, a key is shown as `key(Order/name=id=7)`, a timestamp, geopoint or blob as `timestamp(…)`, `geopoint(lat, lng)` or `blob(N bytes)`, and an embedded entity's own key as its `__key__`, where Go's `/Order,id=7` did not tell a name from an ID (#894). Since #904 a kind's entity listing and the query builder's results read the entities with `RunQuery` on the v1 API rather than the client, so a key in another project or database, and an embedded entity's own key there, are shown with where they are in the Properties cell too, the same rendering as on the entity's page (`TestConsoleDatastoreListingAndQueryShowWhereAKeyIs`, which also pages 205 entities by the query cursor, still the client's encoding; unit `TestDatastoreQueriesAreWrittenAsTheClientWritesThem`, `TestDatastoreEmbeddedEntityKeyKeepsItsPartition`). Since #905 an array or embedded entity property's page has an **Elements** tab listing every value inside it, at any depth, as `items[2]`, `line.when` or `line.__key__` (a name that is not a plain identifier as `line["odd name"]`), each with its own page; **Edit value**, on a row's menu and on that page, takes a type (string, integer, float, boolean, timestamp, key, geopoint or null; an embedded entity's key is a key) and a value, prefilled, and writes back that value only, keeping its index flag, and its meaning when the type is unchanged, so saving it unchanged writes back exactly what was stored; a blob, a key in another project or database, and a type Edit value does not write (an array or entity, which Edit property writes as a whole) are refused. `TestConsoleDatastoreEditValueInsideArraysAndEntities` writes the entity with the official client, adds another project's key through the v1 API, saves every Edit value unchanged and reads the entity back proto-equal through `Lookup`, then changes a key, a timestamp, a geopoint, a key in a nested array and an embedded entity's own key and reads every other value back unchanged through `Lookup` and the new ones as their types through the official client; unit `TestDatastoreElementsAreListedAndAddressed`, `TestDatastoreEditValueWritesOnlyThatValue`; in headless Chrome `TestDatastoreEditValueInsideAnArrayThroughTheBrowser`. Edit value, Add value, Remove value and **Exclude from indexes** carry a digest of the property as the page read it, in a hidden form field, and a change another writer made to that property since is refused in the same transaction with "changed since the page was loaded … reload the page" rather than reaching a value that has moved (#923, `TestConsoleDatastoreValueActionsRefuseAConcurrentChange`; unit `TestDatastoreValueActionsRefuseAChangedProperty`); Exclude from indexes, on an array or embedded entity property's page and on each array or embedded entity inside one, sets or clears the flag on every value inside it in one write, keeping every value and its order, and indexing is refused while a string or blob inside is over 1,500 bytes (#924, `TestConsoleDatastoreExcludeWholeArrayFromIndexes`; unit `TestDatastoreExcludeFromIndexesSetsEveryValue`; in headless Chrome, with #923, `TestDatastoreStaleValueActionIsRefusedAndWholeArrayExcludedThroughTheBrowser`). `TestConsoleDatastoreKeepsAKeyValuesProjectAndDatabase` writes the entity through the v1 API and reads it back through it and the official client; unit `TestDatastoreKeyValuesKeepTheirProjectAndDatabase`, `TestDatastoreFormValuesAreWrittenAsTheClientWritesThem`, `TestNestedDatastoreValuesRenderAsTheirType`; in headless Chrome `TestDatastoreNestedAndForeignKeysThroughTheBrowser`, in the emulators shard, whose browser step exports the Datastore and Firestore emulators' addresses (#895).
- [x] **Subcollection** (#854): **Start collection** on a document's page: the Start collection form, whose collection becomes a subcollection of that document. `TestConsoleFirestoreSubcollections` reads it back at its path through the official client; `TestFirestoreStartASubcollectionOnADocumentPage` drives it in headless Chrome.
- [x] **Namespace and child entity** (#854): **Create entity** on the Datastore screen takes an optional **Namespace**, with Datastore's pattern and its reserved names refused; a namespace's page offers Create entity with a Kind. **Create child entity** on an entity's page takes a Kind and the entity's fields, and refuses a parent that does not exist. `TestConsoleDatastoreNamespacesAndChildren` reads each back through the official client; `TestDatastoreNamespaceAndChildEntityThroughTheBrowser` drives the child in headless Chrome.
- [x] **Dataset** (#854): **Create dataset** on the BigQuery screen: Dataset ID (letters, numbers and underscores), Location, Description and Labels, one `key=value` per line. `TestConsoleBigQueryDatasetTableAndRowWrites` reads each back through the official client.
- [x] **Table** (#854, #994): **Create table** on a dataset's page: Table ID, **Table type** (TABLE, or VIEW with its **View query**, #994), Description and **Schema**, an editor of rows — each a field name, a type (STRING, BYTES, INTEGER, FLOAT, NUMERIC, BIGNUMERIC, BOOLEAN, TIMESTAMP, DATE, TIME, DATETIME, GEOGRAPHY, JSON, RECORD) and a mode (NULLABLE, REQUIRED, REPEATED) — with **Add field** and **Remove**. A RECORD row opens its nested fields under it, indented, with **Add nested field** (#874). A name outside BigQuery's flexible column-name rule (#994), two names at one level that differ only in case, or a RECORD with no fields mark the schema invalid on the form before anything is sent. `TestBigQueryCreateTableInsertRowsAndDeleteThroughTheForms` and `TestBigQueryRecordColumnsThroughTheSchemaEditor` drive it in headless Chrome.
- [x] **Rows** (#854): **Insert rows** on a table's page: one JSON object per line, or a JSON array of objects, its help naming the table's fields with their types and modes. A refusal names the row and the field. **Skip invalid rows** and **Ignore unknown values** (#994) set `insertAll`'s `skipInvalidRows` and `ignoreUnknownValues`; with the first, the answer lists each skipped row, numbered, with the API's reason, and how many were inserted.
- [x] **Edit table** (#994): on a table's and a view's page, prefilled from it: Description and Labels. Since #1025 and #1227 it also takes the friendly name, expiration, fields added at the schema's end, column descriptions and REQUIRED columns relaxed, and removing a label or clearing the description is sent and carried out (#1009, `TestBigQueryCreateViewAndEditTable`, `TestBigQueryEditTableSchema`). `TestConsoleBigQueryEditTableViewsAndInsertOptions` reads each back through the official client; `TestBigQueryFlexibleNamesInsertOptionsEditTableAndViewThroughTheForms` drives it in headless Chrome.
- [x] **Load and export** (#993): **Load from Cloud Storage** on a dataset's page (with a Table ID) and on a table's page: Source URIs, one `gs://` URI per line; File format (CSV, NEWLINE_DELIMITED_JSON, PARQUET); Write preference (WRITE_APPEND, WRITE_TRUNCATE); under **Schema**, Auto-detect and the schema editor, both optional; under **Advanced options**, Number of errors allowed and Ignore unknown values; under **CSV options**, Header rows to skip, Field delimiter, Quote character, Null marker, Null markers, Encoding (UTF-8, ISO-8859-1, UTF-16BE, UTF-16LE, UTF-32BE, UTF-32LE), Source column match (POSITION, NAME), Time zone, Date format, Datetime format, Time format and Timestamp format (#1325), Allow jagged rows, Allow quoted newlines and Preserve ASCII control characters, each a CSV option the front serves, refused on a load of another format; since #1457 Field delimiter and Quote character take any one ISO-8859-1 character with that encoding (a quote of several characters is its first), Source column match NAME reads the names from the last header row skipped, and Auto-detect with Header rows to skip reads the header as BigQuery documents (generic `string_field_0` names when there is none), each sent as the form holds it and served by the front (`TestBigQueryCSVDelimiterAndQuoteBytes`, `TestBigQueryCSVNameMatchAfterSkippedRows`, `TestBigQueryCSVAutodetectHeaderRows`). **Export to Cloud Storage** on a table's page: Destination URI, More destination URIs, Export format (CSV, NEWLINE_DELIMITED_JSON, AVRO, PARQUET), Compression (NONE, GZIP, DEFLATE, SNAPPY or ZSTD, as each format allows), Use Avro logical types, Field delimiter and Print header. Each answers with its job, linked to its page in Job history, or with the API's refusal on the form. `TestConsoleBigQueryLoadExportAndJobHistory` reads each back through the official client; `TestBigQueryLoadExportAndJobHistoryThroughTheForms` drives both forms and Job history in headless Chrome.
- [x] **Edit model** (#1465): on a BigQuery ML model's page, prefilled from it: Description and Friendly name, sent in one `models.patch`; **Delete model** on the page and in the Models section's row menu asks for the model's name back (`models.delete`). `TestBigQueryModelsSectionAndPage` reads each through the official client; `TestBigQueryModelsThroughThePage` trains a model in the Read-write editor and drives the Models section, Edit model and Delete model in headless Chrome (#1554).
- [x] **Service**: Service name; labels; container image; environment variables; variables from Secret Manager, one `NAME=secret` or `NAME=secret:version` per line (#852); and every other field the adapter maps — port, entrypoint, arguments, CPU and memory limits, min and max instances, requests per instance, request timeout. Region is **absent**: the adapter takes one location from configuration, and `TestDeployFormOffersOnlyWhatTheAdapterMaps` asserts the form covers what `ToKnative` writes and nothing `Unsupported` refuses.
- [x] **Service edit** (#595): the Service form's fields, prefilled from the revision that is serving — not the latest template, which after a failed rollout is the configuration that failed. **Service name** is shown disabled, and a request that changes it is refused with the field's own help text. Labels and variables from Secret Manager are prefilled and replaced (#852): `TestConsoleRunServiceLabelsAndSecrets`, `TestRunEditSetsLabelsAndSecretBackedVariables`. `TestConsoleRunEditAndDeployNewRevision` changes a variable from the console and reads the new revision through the official client. In headless Chrome, `TestCloudRunEditFormIsPrefilledAndGivesFocusBack` opens the dialog from the service's page and reads the disabled name and the image and variable prefilled from the serving revision (#700).
- [x] **Job edit** (#795): **Edit job** on the job's page and in each row's menu opens the **Edit job** page (#2062: the job's address with `?edit=1`, a back arrow to the job or the list it was opened from, Save then Cancel at the bottom), the Create job form's fields prefilled from the job, under **Target** and **Retries**. **Name**, **Region** and **Target type** are shown disabled, and a request that changes one is refused; only the fields of the job's own target type are offered, plus the method, headers and attempt deadline of an HTTP target and the attributes of a Pub/Sub one, which Create job does not ask for. An invalid cron expression or time zone is refused with `UpdateJob`'s own message on the page. `TestConsoleSchedulerEditJob` reads the change back with the official client, and the next run on the new schedule; in headless Chrome, `TestSchedulerEditJobThroughTheForm`.
- [x] **Key edit** (#794): **Edit key** on a Cloud KMS key's page, the key's labels prefilled, one `key=value` per line, and **Key name** shown disabled; a request that changes it is refused. Labels and, since #816, the rotation period and next rotation time are offered, the fields `UpdateCryptoKey` applies here; purpose, protection level, algorithm and destroy scheduled duration are fixed, which the dialog's note says. The update mask names exactly the fields changed. A label key `UpdateCryptoKey` refuses (one with a capital), and a rotation period below its 24-hour minimum, are refused with its own message on the form. `TestConsoleKMSEditKey` reads the labels and the schedule back with the official client's `GetCryptoKey`; in headless Chrome, `TestKMSEditKeyLabelsThroughTheForm` and `TestKMSEditKeyRotationThroughTheForm`.
- [x] **Key** (#852): **Create key** on a key ring's page: Key name; Labels; **Destroy scheduled duration**, 30d by default, from 24 hours to 120 days, which cannot be changed once the key exists; under **Automatic rotation**, the rotation period and next rotation time Edit key also offers. `TestConsoleKMSCreateKeyOptions`; in headless Chrome, `TestKMSCreateKeyOptionsThroughTheForm`.
- [x] **Secret** (#852, #857): **Create secret** is Name, the first value, labels and annotations, and the **replication policy**: Automatic (the default) or User-managed with its locations, comma-separated. User-managed with no location, or a location with Automatic, is refused rather than saved without it (`TestSecretCreateKeepsTheReplicationPolicy`, `TestConsoleSecretReplicationPolicy`, `TestSecretManagerCreateReplicationPolicyThroughTheForm`). **Expiration** (#1225): Expire time or TTL, on Create secret and Edit secret; both set is refused (`TestConsoleSecretExpiry`, `TestSecretManagerExpiryThroughTheForms`). **Notifications** (#1259): Pub/Sub topics, one per line, on Create secret and Edit secret (`TestConsoleSecretTopics`). **Rotation** and **destruction delay** (#1278): next rotation time, rotation period and the delay, on Create secret and Edit secret (`TestConsoleSecretRotationAndDestroyDelay`).
- [x] **Execute with overrides** (#852): on a Cloud Run job's page, beside **Execute**: container arguments quoted as a shell would, environment variables added or replaced, number of tasks and task timeout, each for that execution only; a form with no override is refused, since that is Execute. Labels and variables from Secret Manager are on Create job and Edit job. `TestConsoleRunJobLabelsSecretsAndOverrides`; in headless Chrome, `TestCloudRunJobExecuteWithOverridesThroughTheForm`.
- [x] **Spanner DML** (#798): not a form but the query editor's **Read-write (writes data)** mode on a database's page, next to **Read-only**, the default after every navigation. **Run DML** opens a confirmation that names the database and shows the statement; its Cancel discards without asking (#783) and sends nothing. The result is the rows affected; a refusal is the console's reason (DML in read-only mode, DDL, several statements, a `SELECT` in read-write, over 16 KiB) or Spanner's own message (a constraint violation). `TestConsoleSpannerDML`; unit `TestSpannerStatementClassifier`. No browser test: the emulators shard has no browser step.
- [x] **Cloud SQL writes** (#995): the PostgreSQL and MySQL editors' **Read-write (writes data)** mode on a database's page and its tables', next to **Read-only**, the default after every navigation, as Spanner's. **Run statement** opens a confirmation that names the database and the engine (`Write to database cloudburrow on the local PostgreSQL server?`) and shows the statement; Cancel sends nothing. The statement runs as `cloudburrow`, the application's user — in one transaction on PostgreSQL, one statement with autocommit on MySQL, never as MySQL's read-only `cloudburrow_console` — and the answer is the database's own: PostgreSQL's command tag with rows affected, MySQL's rows-affected count, or the error. `TestConsoleCloudSQLReadWriteEditor`, `TestConsoleCloudSQLMySQLReadWriteEditor`; unit `TestCloudSQLReadWriteModeIsOfferedWhereTheEditorIs`, `TestCloudSQLWritesRefuseBeforeReachingTheServer`, `TestPostgresWriteReportIsPostgreSQLsCommandTag`; in headless Chrome `TestCloudSQLReadWriteEditorsThroughThePage`.
- [x] **Column family** (#797): **Add column family** on a table's page: Column family ID, then under **Garbage collection** Maximum versions, Maximum age (`7d`, `12h`, `30m`) and **Only when both limits are exceeded**, each optional — neither limit keeps cells for ever. **Edit GC policy** on the family's row in the Schema tab takes the same three, prefilled. `TestConsoleBigtableFamiliesAndRowWrites` reads each policy back with the official admin client's `TableInfo`.
- [x] **Cell** (#797): **Write cell** on a table's page (Row key, Column family prefilled with the first, Column qualifier, Value, Timestamp) and on a row's page (the same without the row key). The timestamp is optional, empty for now. `TestConsoleBigtableFamiliesAndRowWrites` reads each cell back with the official client's `ReadRow`, with its value and timestamp.
- [x] **Grant access** (#793): on a resource's **Permissions** tab, **New principals** (one or more, separated by commas or new lines) and **Role**, both required, both free text with help saying the service checks neither, over the not-enforced note. Saved, the principals are added to the role's binding in one `SetIamPolicy` with the etag the tab read; a stale one is refused on the form with the service's message. **Remove principal** on a row asks for the principal back. The service's own `GetIamPolicy`, through the official clients, reads each change: `TestConsolePermissionsOnAQueue`, `TestConsolePermissionsOnASecret`, `TestConsolePermissionsOnAKeyRingAndKey`, `TestConsolePermissionsOnABucket`; in headless Chrome, `TestTasksPermissionsThroughTheTab`.
- [x] Validation inline and before submission, with the same constraint the API enforces.
- [x] A submission failure shows the API's own message, not a generic one.

### 4.6 Operations and notifications

- [x] A long-running operation appears in the notifications control.
- [x] Its terminal state — success or failure — is shown, with the failure's cause.
- [x] Nothing reports success before the API says so.

---

### 4.7 The shared layout (#1842)

Round 1 of the console-parity work (#1868). Google's console is described from its public
documentation only (the BigQuery web UI page, Create a bucket, Create a topic, Create a queue, the
Logs Explorer interface); the layout below is what every category screen (#1843–#1866) builds on.

- [x] **A product's own left-hand menu.** Inside a product, `#product-nav` sits on the left of the
  page: the product's mark and name, then its pages one per row, the current one marked
  (`aria-current="page"`). It is drawn on the product's list screens, its resources' pages and its
  create pages. Its control ("Collapse left pane" / "Expand left pane", named "Collapse
  <product> navigation menu") folds it to a rail, remembered across screens. A narrow window shows
  it as a strip above the page. It lists only the pages this instance serves, and Agent Platform's
  pages that run locally (Studio, Embeddings, Local models, Online prediction) while their backend is
  off, as Google's menu always lists them: each draws its off state (`listedWhenOff`). It replaces the
  horizontal link bar above the page.
- [x] **One drawer row per product.** Firestore (Data, Databases), Bigtable (Instances, Tables), Key
  Management (Key management, Autokey, EKM connections, KMS Controls, Key inventory, Single-tenant HSM), Memorystore (Redis, Redis
  Cluster, Valkey), Cloud Logging (Logs Explorer with the admin pages), APIs & Services (Enabled
  APIs & services, Library, Credentials, Operations) and IAM & Admin (IAM, Roles, Groups, Service accounts, Audit Logs)
  are each one product with their pages in its menu. Every address is unchanged, so no redirect is
  needed; a pin made on a page that is now inside a product points at the product.
- [x] **The title line carries the actions.** A list's Create (with its plus), Refresh and the
  info-panel toggle (an icon, "Show info panel" / "Hide info panel") are on the title's line; the
  bar under it keeps the product's list actions and the bulk Delete. A resource page has a back
  arrow, its Edit / Delete / actions beside the title, and is titled "<Kind> details" with its name
  under the title where its route declares `kind` (Bucket, Topic, Subscription, Queue, Job, Secret,
  Key ring so far); a deeper page (an object, a document) keeps its own name as the title.
- [x] **The filter bar.** Every list table's filter is the shared `filterBar`: focusing it lists
  the table's properties; choosing one and typing a value, then Enter, makes a chip
  ("Location: us-east1"); chips are joined by AND, plain text matches any column, and each chip has
  a remove control, with "Clear filters". **View options** beside it turns columns off and on,
  remembered per service.
- [x] **Status first, as an icon**, with its text for assistive technology and in the tooltip; the
  row menu's column has no header text.
- [x] **Create flows are full pages.** The create page has a back arrow; a form whose fields declare
  sections is laid out as numbered sections ("1 Get started", "2 Choose a storage class for your
  data", …), each with Continue, which folds the finished section to its title and opens the next;
  Create then Cancel at the bottom. Create a bucket and Create topic use it; Create queue and
  Create key ring, dialogs before, are pages now (`CreateOnPage`). Other forms of three fields or
  fewer are still dialogs until their category converts them.
- [x] **A project is always selected.** The picker has no "All projects" entry; with no default
  project the first registered one is chosen.
- [x] **Emulator notes are a dismissible banner** above the table instead of a paragraph, dismissed
  for the tab.
- [x] **Details in the page header** (round 2). A resource's properties are a row of its page header
  (`.resource-summary`, label over value) above the tab strip, as Google's bucket page carries
  Location and Storage class beside its title, not a "Details" card between the header and the
  tabs; such a header scrolls with the page rather than staying pinned over the tab's table. A
  resource whose page is only its details (a build trigger, a private worker pool) shows them on a
  Details tab, the first tab Google's pages put them on. Every
  detail route declares `kind`, so every top-level resource page is "<Kind> details"
  with its name under the title; the data browsers (Firestore Data, Datastore), whose pages are a
  collection or entity path rather than a resource, keep the path as their title, and Firestore's
  breadcrumb starts at "Firestore" (`crumb`).
- [x] **A Logs tab only where Google's page has one**: Cloud Run services, jobs and worker pools,
  functions, and GKE workloads, pods, Services and Jobs (route `logs: true`; "Click the Logs tab to
  get the request and container logs for all revisions of this service",
  https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/logging). It reads the instance's log store, which holds
  what clients write to Cloud Logging when the instance serves it (`/api/logs` answers
  `cloudLogging`), says so when it is not served, and links to the Logs Explorer with the filter
  applied. A bucket or a topic has no Logs tab, as on Google's pages (a secret's has, #1850); their lines stay in
  the Logs Explorer. A provider's own Logs tab (a Cloud Run task's) is kept. The Query tab stays
  where the service has a query editor.
- [x] **Search.** The toolbar search reads "Search (/) for resources, pages and products" and `/`
  focuses it; its dropdown lists products, the pages of every served product matched by page or
  product name ("deleted" finds Deleted buckets, "log" Cloud Logging's pages), and resources, with
  "All results" for the results page. Google's search also finds documentation, which a local
  console does not hold.
- [x] **Help and keyboard shortcuts.** The toolbar's Help (question mark) menu holds Keyboard
  shortcuts, Connect and All products; `?` opens the same shortcut list. Below 600px Help gives
  up its place in the bar and Settings and utilities lists Keyboard shortcuts and Connect.
- [x] **Select a resource and New project.** The picker is Google's resource selector, "Select a
  resource" (#1865): a search over projects and folders, Recent, Starred and All (recents and stars
  are kept in this browser; each project row has a star), Name and ID in two columns, New project
  and Manage resources (`/cloud-resource-manager`). All is the organization, its folders and the
  projects under each, as a tree. A project row selects the project. A folder or the organization
  scopes the pages that work at its level, IAM, Organization Policies and Tags, as Google's does
  (#2023): the address carries `?folder=N` or `?organizationId=N`, as console.cloud.google.com's,
  the toolbar names the folder or organization, and the page reads and writes it (IAM through the
  Folders' or Organizations' `GetIamPolicy` and `SetIamPolicy`, with the bindings above it inherited,
  and its Deny tab; Organization Policies and Edit policy the folder's or organization's policies;
  Tags the organization's keys, Create tag key making them there). From any other page it opens the
  folder's or organization's own page, the project kept; a page that works per project only drops
  the choice, so the toolbar always names what the page reads, and choosing a project replaces it
  (`TestConsoleScopesToAFolderAndTheOrganization`, `TestResourceSelectorScopesIAMOrgPoliciesAndTags`).
  New project opens the New project page at `/projectcreate`: Project name first, the Project ID
  made from it ("It cannot be changed later", Edit), and Location (No organization, the organization
  or a folder); Create makes it through Resource Manager's create and selects it, and its back
  link is Manage resources. The Projects list, at its address, keeps its Create project dialog.
- [x] **Welcome.** Home is "Welcome", "You're working in <project>": Project info (name, number, ID,
  Go to project settings), Resources (the counts of the project's buckets, topics, secrets, queues
  and the other served products' resources), Quick access to the served products, APIs (the
  enabled APIs and services, linking to the APIs overview), and Activity
  (View all activity, Request Log); the instance's own state (Instance, Cluster, Endpoints,
  utilisation, components, services) follows under "Instance". Google's Recommendations card has
  no counterpart: CloudBurrow serves no Recommender API.

#### For category workers: the shared components (`internal/console/assets/console.js`)

| Component | Use |
|---|---|
| ROUTES `product`, `productTitle`, `navTitle`, `kind` | Give every page of a product the same `product` key and `productTitle`; the menu and the drawer follow. `navTitle` names a page in the menu when its title is the product's name; `kind` titles its resources' pages "<Kind> details". |
| `pageHeader(title, subtitle, { back, backLabel, actions })` | A screen's header: back arrow, h1 and the title-line slot. `headerActions(header)` returns the slot of a header already drawn. |
| `createButton(label, onclick)`, `refreshButton(run)` | The title line's Create (with its plus) and Refresh (busy while `run` runs). |
| `filterBar({ noun, columns, onChange })` | `.node` (the bar), `.chips` (the chip row), `.matches(get)`, `.active()`, `.text()`, `.clear()`; `renderTableInto` already uses it for every list. |
| `viewOptions({ service, columns, onChange })` | The column chooser; `.hidden()` is the set turned off. |
| `Scope.ViewOptions` | The list's scope chosen in its View options dialog, as radio buttons over the column choices, applied by Save, instead of a picker over the list: Google's Buckets page holds "Soft deleted buckets only" there (#1843). |
| `Section.ShownIn`, `Section.ShowAs`, `Section.Show` | A section drawn by another tab's Show list instead of a tab of its own, under the label `ShowAs`; `Show` names the host tab's own rows ("Live objects only" when empty). A bucket's soft-deleted objects are its Objects tab's "Soft-deleted objects only", an object's soft-deleted generations its Version history's; an address naming the section opens the host tab with it chosen (#1843). |
| `statusIcon(status)` | A status cell's icon, colour from `stateOf`, text kept for assistive technology. |
| `noteBanner(text)` | An emulation note as a dismissible banner. |
| ROUTES `logs`, `crumb` | `logs: true` gives a resource page the shared Logs tab (only where Google's page has one); `crumb` names a breadcrumb's first link where the page title would not name the product. |
| `createSteps(fields.nodes)` | Numbered sections with Continue, from the form's `Section`s; `renderCreatePage` uses it, so a provider gets it by declaring sections and `CreateOnPage() bool { return true }`. |
| `CreateTitle() string` (`console.CreateTitler`) | The create page's title when it differs from its button: "Create a bucket", ending in Create (#1843). |
| `Section.Actions` | Actions drawn at the top of one tab (`.section-actions`) instead of beside the title, such as the Lifecycle tab's Add a rule; `DetailActions` must offer them too. |
| A placeholder `Section{ID: "permissions", Kind: KindPermissions}` | Puts the IAM Permissions tab where the provider lists it (third on a bucket, as Google's is) instead of last; dropped when the resource has no policy. |
| `Field.Tab` | Inside a `Section`, puts the field on one tab of that section's tab strip (`.form-tabs`), as Create service holds Container(s), Variables & Secrets, Volumes, Networking and Security; a refused field's tab is shown (#1852). |
| `CreateSubmit() string` (`console.CreateSubmitter`) | The create page's submit label when it differs from its button: Deploy container opens Create service, ending in Create (#1852). |
| A placeholder `Section{ID: "logs", Kind: KindLogs}` | Puts the Logs tab (the resource's local Cloud Logging entries) where the provider lists it, as a service's Metrics, SLOs, Logs, Revisions (#1852), instead of last. |
| `Field.OptionLabels`, `Field.DisabledOptions` | A select's or radio's options read as Google names them ("PostgreSQL 17" for POSTGRES_17), and a radio option drawn but not choosable (SQL Server) (#1860). |
| `Field.Links` | Documents a field is about, drawn as links under its help, each opening in a new tab: Microsoft's licence terms under Cloud SQL for SQL Server's licence checkbox (#1916). |
| `Field.VisibleWhen` | `{"engine": "MySQL"}`: the field is shown only while those fields hold those values; hidden, it is neither checked nor sent (#1860). |
| `Field.CheckedWhen` | `"security=IAM_AUTH\|TOKEN_AUTH"`: a checkbox checked and locked while another field holds one of those values, still sent, as Memorystore's Security holds Enable In-Transit Encryption (TLS) on (#1862). |
| `EditForm.Page` | The resource's Edit opens a page of its own (the resource's address with `?edit=1`: the form under the resource's crumbs and a back arrow, ending in Submit and Cancel, back to the resource's page on save) instead of a dialog, as Google's Edit opens the Edit Redis instance page (#1862). A list row's Edit opens the same page. |
| `Detail.SideNav` | The resource's sections drawn as a menu down the left of the page (a strip above it when narrow), as Google's Cloud SQL instance menu is (#1860). |
| `RowMenuOrder() []string` (`console.RowMenuOrderer`) | A list row's menu in Google's order: the row's action IDs, with `console.RowMenuEdit` and `console.RowMenuDelete` for its Edit and Delete, as the Log Router reads View sink details, Edit sink, Disable sink, Delete sink, Troubleshoot sink (#1863). |
| `Section.Tabs` | A menu page's own tabs, a tablist inside its panel (the tab chosen kept in the address as `subtab`), as Google's Cloud SQL Connections page has Summary, Networking and Security (#1860). |
| `Field.Panel` | Fields naming the same panel are drawn in a dialog of their own, titled by it, opened by a button of that label where the first of them would be, ending in Save (Cancel or Escape puts back what they held); they are still validated and submitted with the form, and the button is drawn while any of them is visible, as Google's Set password policy under Enable password policies (#1860). |
| `Field.Generate` | On a password field, a **Generate** button filling it with 20 random characters (`crypto.getRandomValues`); every password field also has **Show password** (`aria-pressed`): Cloud SQL's root password (#2141). |
| `Action.Title` | The action's dialog title where Google's is not its label: Cloud SQL's Create database opens "New database", Add user account "Add a user account to instance NAME" (#1860). |
| `Action.ConfirmTitle` | The confirmation's title where Google's names the kind rather than the resource, with `ConfirmStyle` `confirm` for nothing typed, `Delete` or `Disable` for that word, or `prefix10` for the name's first 10 characters: Cloud SQL's "Stop database instance?" and "Delete backup" (#1860), Storage Intelligence's Disable and Delete HMAC key (#2047); a form's confirmation honours them. |
| `Field.PickFrom` on a `"picklist"` field (`console.PicklistFieldType`) | Items chosen from a picker, as Google's Create role and Edit role hold a role's permissions: each chosen item with a checkbox (unchecked to take it out), and the `AddLabel` button opening a picker under the list (Service, Filter, a checkbox per item, Add), filled from the provider's Studio call `service:call`; the value is the checked items, one per line (#1864). |
| `Listing.TableTitle` | A heading over a list's table that also names it (`aria-labelledby`), where Google titles the table apart from the page: Audit Logs' "Data Access audit logs configuration" (#1864). |
| `Section.Edit` | A form drawn inside one tab and sent as the page's Edit is (the provider's `Editor`), as a service account's Details tab has its Name box and Save (#1864). |
| `CreateCommitStep() int` (`console.CreateCommitter`) | The step whose Continue reads Create and continue and makes the resource, the page's submit then finishing it with `_created` set: Create service account (#1864). |
| `Action.Panel` | An action's form drawn in the info panel beside the list instead of a dialog, its fields' Sections as the panel's tabs: a selection action opens as rows are checked (one row's own Panel action, with its values, for one row), a list action from its button, as Google's Audit Logs information panel and Set Default Configuration are (#2013). |
| `Action.Href` on a selection action | A selection action that is a link: the checked rows go in the address as its SelectionField's parameter, as Create custom role from selection opens Create custom role with `?roles=` (#2013). |
| `Field.SeedFrom` on a `"picklist"` field | The address parameter that seeds a picklist's chosen items, read through its PickFrom call with `{"seed": …}`, each listed checked: Create custom role from selection's permissions (#2013). |
| `Field.RemoveLabel` on a `"group"` field | The rows' remove button where Google's is not Remove: Audit Logs' exempted principals have Delete (#2013). |
| `Listing.InfoOnSelect`, `Resource.Info` (`console.InfoTab`) | A row's checkbox opens the info panel on it, and the panel's tabs beyond the row's columns (Details first): an `InfoTab`'s Properties, or its Empty text, and an optional `Form` (an Action edited in the tab, saved with its Submit, performed at the row's Target; `DetailActions` there must offer it, `Linked`), as Google's Spanner Instances list opens a checked instance's Labels tab (#1859). |
| `Section.Refs` (`console.RefPicker`), `Section.Blame`, `Section.Language` on any file | The code view of Secure Source Manager's web interface (#2071): a branch and tag selector over a tab, each ref opening its path; a `KindText` section with a `Language` other than `workflows` is a file's lines, numbered, with its syntax highlighted (comments, strings, numbers, keywords of the language), and `Blame` draws each run of lines' commit, author and summary beside them behind the Blame button (`?blame=1`). |
| `KindDiff`, `Section.Diff` (`console.DiffView`) | A pull request's Files changed drawn line by line (#2071): each file's hunks, old and new numbered, collapsible; the Whitespace menu (Show all whitespace changes, Ignore whitespace changes), Split view/Unified view, the diff options menu (Expand all files, Collapse all files), kept in the address; a plus icon beside each line opening a comment form whose Add single comment and Start review send the page's `CommentAction` and `StartAction` with `path`, `line` (negative on the old side) and `lineBody`; the code comments under their lines, pending ones marked Pending. |
| `Listing.Sorts` (`console.ListSort`), `Listing.Menus` (`console.ListMenu`) | A tab's Sort menu and filter menus over its rows' fields (a menu keeps the rows whose comma-separated field holds the choice; a `Toggle` is a pressed button, Assigned to me), kept in the address as `{tab}-sort` and `{tab}-{menu}`: Secure Source Manager's Issues and Pull requests tabs (#2071). |
| `Property.Action`, `Property.ActionText`, `Action.Linked` | A link beside a property's value that opens one of the page's actions (the page's address with `?action=`), as Google's Upgrade beside a Spanner instance's edition; a `Linked` action is offered by the page but drawn only where something links to it, never as a button beside the title (#1859). |
| `Action.Page`, `Action.Opens` | An action's form drawn as a page of its own at the resource page's address with `?action=<id>`, under its `Title` (or label) with a breadcrumb and a back arrow, ending in its `Submit` and Cancel, which return to the resource's page; a reload shows it again: Cloud KMS's Create key (#1851). `Opens` names the field whose value is the row it made, which the console opens when it is done: the Secure Source Manager web interface's Create repository (#1856). The resource-level counterpart of `EditForm.Page` (#1855). |
| `Field.OptionsBy`, `Field.OptionsFor` | A select listing only the options `OptionsFor` gives for the value of the field `OptionsBy` names, the current value kept where it is listed, else the first: Create key's Algorithm follows its Purpose (#1851). |
| `Listing.HiddenColumns` | Columns off in Column display options until the viewer turns them on (a viewer's own choice, once made, is kept): Key inventory's EKM via VPC connection (#1851). |
| `Detail.Panels`, `Resource.Panel` (`console.SidePanel`, `console.PanelTable`) | A side panel in the info panel's place, opened by a button beside the title (a page's `Panels`) or a link on a row (its `Panel`, the link reading its `Label`), holding the provider's Intro, preformatted `Text` with a Copy button (`Copy`), headed `Groups` and captioned `Tables`; Close or Escape hides it and returns focus to what opened it. It changes nothing. Artifact Registry's Setup Instructions and a vulnerability's View / View Fixed, Vulnerability details with its File location(s) table (#2066). |
| `Section.ShowLabel` | Names a tab's Show list where Google's is not Show: the Vulnerabilities tab's Layer bar, "Layer", whose choices are "All layers" (`Show`) and a section per layer `ShownIn` the tab (#2066). |
| `PropertyGroup.Bars` | Draws a group of counts as a horizontal bar graph, a list item per property naming its label and count beside its bar: the Dependencies tab's Most common licenses (#2066). |
| `Field.Browse` `"artifact-registry"` | A Browse button beside the field lists the project's standard repositories of the form's format in its location, and fills in the one chosen by its resource name: a remote repository's upstream (#2066). |

#### Cloud Storage (#1843)

- [x] Menu: Buckets, Observability, Storage Intelligence (its Configuration page), Batch operations, Inventory reports, Transfer data, Settings. Observability (Cloud Monitoring) is a service CloudBurrow does not run: its page is in Google's place and says "Not available locally" and why.
- [x] Batch operations (#1957): Storage Batch Operations jobs (Job ID, status, transformation, buckets, objects done of found, failed, dry run, created, completed); Create job (Name your job, Choose a source: bucket and object prefixes or a CSV manifest, Choose a transformation: delete objects, update object metadata, update object holds, update encryption key; dry run); each job's page with its progress, bucket operations and error summaries; Cancel on a running job, Delete.
- [x] Inventory reports (#1957): Storage Insights report configurations of the project's buckets; Create report configuration (Identify your report configuration, Choose metadata fields, file format CSV or Apache Parquet and destination, Scheduling options); each configuration's page with Report history (reportDetails), Edit and Delete; Advance clock (CloudBurrow extension). Google also reaches the list from a bucket's Inventory reports tab, which is not drawn yet.
- [x] Transfer data opens Storage Transfer Service (#1957): Transfer jobs (Create transfer job from a bucket or a POSIX file system, with Amazon S3, Azure and URL list drawn disabled, "Not available locally"; schedule, prefixes and age conditions, overwrite and delete options, Pub/Sub notifications; each job's Run history and Configuration; Start a run, Disable job, Enable job, Delete; a run's page with its counters and errors, Pause run, Resume run, Cancel run; Advance clock (CloudBurrow extension)) and Agent pools (Create pool with a bandwidth limit, Edit, Delete).
- [x] Buckets list: Created, Location type, Location, Default storage class, Last modified, Public access, Access control, Protection, Lifecycle, Hierarchical namespace, Labels; the row menu has Create restore job. View options is Google's dialog: Live buckets or Soft deleted buckets only as radio buttons over the column choices, applied by Save; Soft deleted buckets only lists the soft-deleted buckets with Restore and a page each; `/storage/deleted`, their page before, now opens it.
- [x] Create a bucket: a page of five steps (Get started; Choose where to store your data; Choose how to store your data; Choose how to control access to objects; Choose how to protect object data), with Google's defaults (US multi-region, Standard, public access prevention enforced, Uniform, soft delete 7 days), ending in Create. "Set a default class" or Autoclass, and Uniform or Fine-grained access control, are radio choices; hierarchical namespace holds the access control on Uniform. Autoclass is stored (docs/compatibility.md, "Bucket fields"): no object changes class by itself here.
- [x] Bucket page tabs: Objects, Configuration, Permissions, Protection, Lifecycle, Observability, Inventory reports, Operations, then CloudBurrow's other tabs. Configuration shows Autoclass, with Edit Autoclass; Permissions shows the access control with Switch to (Uniform or Fine-grained); Protection has Edit object versioning, Google's "Object versioning" dialog ending in Confirm, whose "Add recommended lifecycle rules to manage version costs" adds the max. versions and noncurrent-expiry limits as lifecycle rules, and Lock retention policy; Lifecycle lists the rules, Add a rule (an action, then object conditions) and Delete rule on each; Observability says "Not available locally" (Cloud Monitoring is not run); Inventory reports lists the Storage Insights inventory report configurations whose source is the bucket, each opening its page, with Create inventory report configuration (the bucket filled in).
- [x] Objects tab: Upload files, Upload folder, Create folder (an empty `name/` object on a flat bucket, a folder with hierarchical namespace), Create managed folder; Google's Show list: Live objects only, Live and noncurrent objects (every generation, kept for the next object list) and Soft-deleted objects only (the soft-deleted objects with Restore). There is no Deleted objects tab: `?tab=deleted` opens the Objects tab with Soft-deleted objects only chosen.
- [x] Object page: Overview with Public URL (Copy URL), Authenticated URL (Copy authenticated URL) and gsutil URI (Copy gsutil URI), then the object's metadata; Download, Edit metadata, Edit access (while the bucket is fine-grained) and Delete beside the title. The public URL is this instance's storage server, the authenticated one this console's download. A Version history tab lists every generation (at most 1,000, as Google's); Restore on a noncurrent one opens "Restore object version", ending in Confirm; its Show list's Soft-deleted objects only lists the object's soft-deleted generations, each with Restore and Confirm.
- [x] Create restore job: Google's pane, "Choose which soft-deleted objects to restore" (Filter by glob pattern, deleted after and before) and Restore options (allow overwrite, copy source ACL), ending in Create.
- [x] Settings: Project access (the project and its Cloud Storage service account) and Interoperability (Service account HMAC; Create a key for a service account, ending in Create key) tabs.
- [x] Storage Intelligence: the Configuration page, with the project's Overview (Status, Edition, Bucket filters) over each resource's configuration, Enable Storage Intelligence and Disable.
- [x] Kept on purpose: a bucket's own ACLs are edited on its Access control tab while access is fine-grained, which Google's console does not offer (only gcloud does); the UI parity rule (#782) puts every implemented feature in the console, so the tab stays, after Google's tabs.
- [x] Restore a soft-deleted bucket (#2047): Restore opens Google's pane, the tabs Specify bucket generation (every soft-deleted generation of the name, the row's chosen) and Specify objects to restore (Don't restore objects, Restore all objects, or Restore objects that match criteria: a deletion time range and, under Filter by glob pattern, globs), and Additional options (allow overwrite, copy source ACL), ending in Restore: `buckets.restore` of the generation, then, for objects chosen, one `objects.bulkRestore` on the restored bucket, as Google's console creates a bulk restore operation for them. Google's page does not list what Additional options holds; its options here are the bulk restore's.
- [x] Interoperability (#2047): "Access keys for service accounts" lists the service accounts with HMAC keys (their key and active key counts), each opening its keys. A key's state is a button: clicked on an Active key it asks first and ends in Deactivate; on an Inactive key it activates at once. Delete, on an inactive key only (the API refuses an active one), asks for the access ID's first 10 characters. Google's managing-hmackeys page words the confirmation backwards ("If you are changing the key's state from Inactive to Active, click Deactivate in the window that appears"); CloudBurrow asks on the change that stops requests, to Inactive.
- [x] Storage Intelligence (#2047): Enable Storage Intelligence opens Google's Configure Storage Intelligence dialog: the resource (the project, its folders or the organization), Continue, then bucket filters, by location (include or exclude, Add location) and by bucket name (include or exclude, a regular expression at a time with Add regular expression), ending in Enable this configuration (`Update…IntelligenceConfig`, edition Standard with the filter). Disable beside the Overview's Status, and on each row, asks for Disable typed; each row keeps Inherit from parent, Start trial and Edit filter.
- [x] Version history (#2047): the rows are checked for Delete, which asks for the word delete and deletes each checked generation (`objects.delete` with its generation); the rows' menus no longer carry Delete version. Show versions on a bucket's or folder's object list, and a generation's page, keep Delete version.

#### Pub/Sub (#1847)

Shared additions: `Field.ShowWhen` ("name" for a checked checkbox, "name=value" for a radio or select choice) shows a field only while its choice holds, and a hidden field is not validated; `Action.Href` makes an action a link (a topic row's Create subscription); an action's result rows may carry their own actions (a pulled message's Ack), sent to the page's path; a create page takes a field's starting value from its address (`/pubsub/subscriptions/create?topic=…`). Round 2: `CreateLayouter` (`CreateLayout() == "page"`) draws a create page as one page under its section headings instead of numbered steps (Pub/Sub's three; Cloud Storage and Cloud SQL keep steps); `CreateChecker` adds buttons that check the form without creating (`POST /api/resources/{service}/check`, a check with fields asking for them in a dialog); `Field.Unavailable` (a checkbox) and `Field.UnavailableOptions` (radio choices) draw a Google choice this instance cannot make disabled with its reason, and the provider refuses a request that makes it; `Detail.OpenTab` names the tab a page opens on when the address names none. Round 3: `EditForm.Layout` (`console.EditLayoutPage`) draws an edit as a page of its own at the resource's address with `?edit=1`, under its section headings, ending in its `Submit` ("Update"), Cancel going back to the resource; `InfoTab.Permissions` draws an info panel tab as the row's IAM policy, read with `POST /api/permissions/{service}` `{"Read":{}}`, with Add principal (granting on every checked row) and Remove principal on each.

- [x] Menu: Topics, Subscriptions, Snapshots, Schemas, in Google's order (Pub/Sub Lite is shut down).
- [x] Lists: Topics (Topic name, Topic ID, Encryption key, Retention, Schema, Labels; the row menu has Create subscription); Subscriptions (Subscription ID, Delivery type, Topic name, Ack deadline, Retention, Message ordering, Exactly once delivery, Expiration, Dead lettering) with Create subscription; Snapshots (Snapshot ID, Topic name, Expiration) with Create snapshot; Schemas (Schema ID, Schema type, Latest revision, Revision created).
- [x] Create topic, one page as Google's: Topic ID, Add a default subscription (checked), Use a schema (reveals the schema and its encoding), Enable ingestion (disabled: no ingestion runs locally), Enable message retention (reveals days, hours, minutes), Export data to BigQuery and Backup message data to Cloud Storage (each reveals its table or bucket and creates TOPIC-bigquery or TOPIC-storage, #1874), Transforms (disabled: no single message transforms run locally), Encryption (Google-owned and Google-managed encryption key, or Cloud KMS key revealing the key name, checked against the local Cloud KMS, #1874), Labels. Order from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/create-topic> (re-read 2026-11-19).
- [x] Create subscription, one page: Subscription ID, Pub/Sub topic, Delivery type (Pull, Push revealing the endpoint and attributes; Write to BigQuery revealing the table, schema configuration, Drop unknown fields, Write metadata and service account; Write to Cloud Storage revealing the bucket, file name prefix, suffix and datetime format, Text or Avro (Write metadata), and the batching limits, #1874), Filter, Retry policy (Retry immediately, or exponential backoff with its minimum and maximum), Dead lettering, Delivery properties (exactly once, ordering), Acknowledgement deadline (10 s), Lifetime options (7 days, Retain acknowledged messages), Expiration period (31 days of inactivity, or Never expire), Labels.
- [x] Topic page tabs: Subscriptions, Snapshots, Metrics (not available locally: Cloud Monitoring), Details, Messages (Publish message; Pull from one of its subscriptions, each message with an Ack; Pull and ack and Pull without ack kept), Ingestion (not available locally), Permissions; Create subscription beside the title opens the page with the topic.
- [x] Subscription page tabs: Metrics, Details, Messages (Pull, then Ack on each message), Snapshots, Permissions, opening on Details: Google's monitoring guide opens the subscription details page and then says "click the Metrics tab" (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/monitor-subscription>, re-read 2026-11-19); Edit, Create snapshot, Replay messages (Seek: To a previous time point or To a snapshot), Detach and Advance clock beside the title. Seek to snapshot and Seek to time are still served by the action route.
- [x] Create schema, one page: Schema ID, Schema type (Avro or Protocol Buffer), Schema definition, then Validate definition (ValidateSchema of the form's definition) and Test message (a window with Message encoding, Message body and Test: ValidateMessage against the definition on the form, given inline), neither creating anything, as <https://docs.cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/create-schemas> describes (re-read 2026-11-19). The verdicts' wording is CloudBurrow's: the docs quote none.
- [x] Exports and CMEK (#1874): the front delivers BigQuery and Cloud Storage subscriptions to the local BigQuery and Cloud Storage and checks topics' Cloud KMS keys against the local Cloud KMS, so Write to BigQuery, Write to Cloud Storage, Export data to BigQuery, Backup message data to Cloud Storage and Cloud KMS key are enabled (they were drawn disabled in #1847 round 2).
- [x] Info panel (round 3): checking rows on the Topics and Subscriptions lists opens the info panel with Details, Permissions and Labels; the Snapshots list's with Details and Labels (Google's console sets no snapshot policy). Permissions lists the checked row's principals with Remove principal on each, and Add principal grants the role on every checked row, each saved with its own etag ("To set roles for one or more topics, select the topics. If the info panel is hidden, click Show info panel. In the Permissions tab, click Add principal", <https://docs.cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/access-control>, read 2026-11-20). Labels saves the row's labels ("Select a resource and change to the LABELS tab", <https://docs.cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/labels>, read 2026-11-20). The checkbox also feeds the header Delete, with its confirmation window, as Google's "select a topic's checkbox, then Delete" (#1847 item 10).
- [x] Edit flows (round 3): a subscription's Edit opens the Edit subscription page, Create subscription's fields in its order, prefilled, ending in Update ("In the subscription details page, click Edit. For Delivery type, select the new delivery type ... Click Update", <https://docs.cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/manage-subscriptions>, read 2026-11-20); Subscription ID, Pub/Sub topic, Filter and message ordering are shown fixed with their reason. A topic's Edit opens Edit topic the same way, with Create topic's Use a schema and Enable message retention (days, hours, minutes); the encryption is shown fixed. Cancel goes back to the resource.
- [x] Delivery type on Edit (#2051): Edit subscription offers Pull, Push, Write to BigQuery and Write to Cloud Storage on a pull, push, BigQuery or Cloud Storage subscription, with Create subscription's fields (table, schema configuration, bucket, file name parts, file format, batching), prefilled on an export subscription; Update clears the old type's configuration and sets the new one's in one `UpdateSubscription` (push_config, bigquery_config, cloud_storage_config), as Google's gcloud steps do ("Clear the configuration for the current delivery type and set the configuration for the new type", <https://docs.cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/manage-subscriptions>, read 2026-11-20). The emulator refuses an update of bigquery_config and cloud_storage_config, so CloudBurrow's Pub/Sub front checks it as on create (the table or bucket exists and fits; one delivery at a time; no exactly-once delivery on an export) and applies it, and the export deliveries start and stop with it (`TestPubSubUpdateSubscriptionSwitchesDeliveryType`; in headless Chrome, `TestPubSubEditSubscriptionChangesDeliveryType`). A Bigtable subscription's delivery is shown fixed.
- [ ] Not yet: a 36-hour expiration period shows as 2 days and is kept unless the days are changed; list column order and the Snapshots and Schemas columns are from the console as remembered, the docs naming none (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-19: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/list-subscriptions> and <https://docs.cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/create-topic> name no columns); the subscription page's default tab is Details because the docs rule out Metrics without naming the tab (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-19: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/pubsub/docs/monitor-subscription>).

#### Cloud Logging (#1863)

Shared additions: `/api/logs` and `/api/stream` take `query` (the Logging query language, `internal/console/logquery.go`, refused with 400 or a `failure` event naming the term) and `from` / `to` (RFC 3339); every entry's JSON carries `logEntry`, its LogEntry form; the stream ends its backlog with a `ready` event (other readers ignore it). Round 2: `Field.ShowWhen` takes conditions joined by `&`, all of which must hold; a `group` field (`Field.Group`, `AddLabel`, `console.ParseGroup`) is a repeatable row of fields; `DeleteLabeler` and `RowEditor` name a row's Delete and add its Edit; `CreateLayoutNext` is the steps with Next; `CreateSubmitter` (from #1852) gives the create page its own submit label; `CreateCheck.Opens` opens the check's answer, a console address, in a new tab; an overflow menu item can be drawn disabled with its reason. Round 3: `console.RowMenuOrderer` orders a row's menu (the services' `rowMenuOrder`); a list row's `Target` sends its actions to `ActAt`, so a `ResultActor` row action (Troubleshoot sink) answers with rows.

- [x] Menu, Google's order: Logs Explorer, Log Analytics, Logs Dashboard, Log-based Metrics, Log Router, Logs Storage; then Saved queries (Google's Query library, linked from the Explorer's toolbar), Exclusions (part of a sink on Google's console, kept reachable), Settings, whose Log Scopes tab holds the log scopes (#2020).
- [x] Logs Explorer, from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/view/logs-explorer-interface> (re-read 2026-11-19): Query library and Share link beside the title; Refine scope (Project logs, a folder or organization, a log view; and All sources for the pod lines no project can be claimed for), the time-range picker (Last 5 minutes to Last 30 days, Last 1 hour by default, Custom after a zoom) and Stream logs / Stop stream; the query pane with Search all fields, the Resource, Log name and Severity menus, each writing its clause into the query editor, and Run query (Ctrl+Enter), the editor suggesting the field names the entries read carry and, after `=` or `:`, their values; the Timeline with Collapse timeline, a column's click zooming to its time, Jump to now, drag handles for the start and end of the range with Run, and a pin for each pinned entry (Scroll to log entry, Zoom to log entry, Unpin); round 3 (re-read 2026-11-20): Zoom out, Zoom in, Backward and Forward over the range, and each bar's pop-up with Scroll to time (the results brought to the bar's first entry) and Zoom to time; the Log fields pane (fields pinned to it first under Pinned, each with Unpin, then Resource type, Severity, Log name, Project ID, each value with its count, Clear), a value adding its clause; Query results, each entry (Severity, Timestamp, Resource, Summary) expanding to its LogEntry fields with Expand nested fields, Collapse nested fields, Copy link, Copy as JSON and, on each value, Show matching entries, Hide matching entries and Pin to Fields pane; Pin on each entry (darkened while pinned, with Same resource.type, Same resource.labels, Same trace and Show all); the results' Actions menu: Create metric and Create sink (their create page with the query as the filter), Create log alert (Cloud Monitoring's Create policy with a log match condition on the query; disabled with its reason where Cloud Monitoring is not served), Highlight in results and Download; Preferences: Manage summary fields (Custom summary fields with the field names the entries carry suggested, Truncate custom summary fields with the character count and which end is shown, Hide or show default summary fields, here the Resource column, Apply) and Show summary chips. Delete log stays on a Cloud Logging entry's row.
- [x] Query library and Save (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/view/building-queries>, re-read 2026-11-20): Save in the Query pane opens Save query (Name, Description, the query, Include custom summary fields, Truncate summary fields, Share with project; Save query), a Cloud Logging SavedQuery of the project with its summary fields and truncation; the Query library (the Explorer's toolbar button, a dialog) has Recent queries (since #2031 the user's, as Google keeps them: Cloud Logging's `recentQueries.list`, each query the Explorer runs recorded, the last 10,000 unique queries over 30 days), Saved queries (More options: Edit, which patches it, and Delete), Suggested queries (from the entries read: Errors, failed HTTP requests, the busiest resource types) and, since #2021, Queries provided by Google Cloud (Google's sample queries from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/view/query-library-preview>, read 2026-11-20, by product, for the products CloudBurrow serves), each with Run and Stream; a saved query's summary fields come back with it. The Saved queries page stays for SQL (Log Analytics) queries.
- [x] Log scopes (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/log-scope/create-and-manage>, re-read 2026-11-20): the list has `_Default` (the project, which cannot be edited or deleted) and a Default marker on the project's default scope; Create log scope is a page of Add projects and Add log views rows, then Name log scope (name, description), ending in Create log scope; each row's More is Set as default, Edit scope (logScopes.patch) and Delete scope. Since #2020 the list is the **Log Scopes** tab of Settings (`/logs/log-scopes`, the menu marking Settings), and Set as default is Google's: `projects.locations.scopes.patch` of the Observability API's `_Default` scope's `logScope`, served on the Logging port; deleting the default log scope makes `_Default` the default again.
- [x] The Logs Explorer searches a log scope (#2020): Refine scope has **Log scope**, a picker of the project's log scopes (the default marked), and a note naming the projects and log views it searches; the entries are ListLogEntries and TailLogEntries over the scope's resource names together, as Google's Explorer searches a scope's resources. With no scope in the address the Explorer opens on the project's default log scope once one other than `_Default` has been set as default. CloudBurrow's own choice: while `_Default` is the default it opens on All sources, as before, because the pod lines the console holds carry no project and `_Default` names only the project (`TestLoggingLogScopesTabAndExplorer`).
- [x] Log Router (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/export/configure_export_v2>): Name, Enabled, Type, Description, Destination, Created, Last updated, Volume (#2021: the bytes the sink routed in the past 30 days, Cloud Logging's count that Cloud Monitoring serves as `logging.googleapis.com/exports/byte_count`), then CloudBurrow's Delivery (a folder's or organization's adds Include children and Intercept children); row menu in Google's order: View sink details, Edit sink (the sink's edit form), Disable sink / Enable sink, Delete sink, Troubleshoot sink (the create page's checks on the stored sink, answered as rows), View sink log volume and error rates (#2021: Metrics Explorer on `exports/byte_count` for the sink's `logging_sink` resource; disabled with its reason without Cloud Monitoring, or for a folder's or organization's sink, whose metrics no project holds). Create sink is one page of Google's four panels: Sink details (Sink name, Sink description); Sink destination (Select sink service: Cloud Logging bucket, BigQuery dataset, Cloud Storage bucket, Pub/Sub topic, Splunk drawn disabled with its reason, Google Cloud project, Other resource; Destination); Choose logs to include in sink (Build inclusion filter, the child-resource choices, Preview logs opening the Logs Explorer on the filter in a new tab); Choose logs to filter out of sink (a row per exclusion filter with Exclusion filter name, Build an exclusion filter and Disable, Add exclusion, Remove); Troubleshoot sink (checks the destination resolves, a log bucket destination exists, and the filters parse); Create sink.
- [x] Log-based Metrics (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/logs-based-metrics/counter-metrics>, distribution-metrics): the user-defined metrics list; Create logs metric, one page: Metric type (Counter / Distribution) first, Details (Log metric name, Description, Units), Filter selection (Select project or log bucket, Build filter; for a distribution Field name, Regular expression and the histogram buckets, Linear, Explicit or Exponential with Google's parameter names, each shown only for a distribution), Labels; Create metric. Each row's menu is Edit metric, View in Metrics Explorer (Cloud Monitoring's Metrics explorer on `logging.googleapis.com/user/NAME`; disabled with its reason where Cloud Monitoring is not served) and Delete metric.
- [x] Logs Storage (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/buckets>): Name, Description, Retention period, Region, Status (Locked / Unlocked), Observability Analytics available, Linked dataset in BigQuery; row menu View bucket details, Restore deleted bucket, Edit bucket, Delete bucket. Create log bucket (the button) is a page of steps moved through with Next: Name, Description, Upgrade to use Observability Analytics with Create a new BigQuery dataset that links to this bucket and its name, Select log bucket region, then Retention period (30 days), ending in Create bucket; Edit bucket edits the retention. Since #2021 the page has Google's summary, Current month ingestion, Previous month ingestion, Projected ingestion by EOM and Current month billable storage (the bytes written into the project's log buckets, as `billing/bytes_ingested` counts them; billable storage is the stored entries received over 30 days ago), and Create usage alert beside Create log bucket: a dialog (Alert policy name, Threshold in GiB, Retest window of at least 30 minutes, Notification channels) that creates Google's policy in Cloud Monitoring, a threshold on Global's Monthly log bytes ingested (`billing/monthly_bytes_ingested`), sum across series, a 60-minute window of max, Any time series violates, Above threshold; disabled with its reason without Cloud Monitoring. Settings is titled Settings.
- [x] Log Analytics (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/analyze/query-and-view>): the Log views panel lists each log view of the project's buckets upgraded to use Log Analytics by its query name, `PROJECT_ID.LOCATION.BUCKET_ID.LOG_VIEW_ID`, with its linked dataset; choosing one puts a default query on it; Run query runs one read-only GoogleSQL statement in the instance's BigQuery, each log view name rewritten to the view of the bucket's linked dataset; since #2021 a bucket with no link is queried as Google's page queries it, without one ("You don't need a linked BigQuery dataset when you want to query your log data"), over a hidden query dataset the Logging server makes for the query and deletes after it, and the results are a table and a Chart (Bar, Line, Scatter, dimension and measures from the result's column types, as BigQuery's Visualization). Without BigQuery served the page stays in the menu and says why it is not available.
- [x] Logs Dashboard: Log entries by severity, Log entries by resource type and Errors by resource type over the time range, each row opening the Explorer with its query.
- [x] #2031 (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/view/logs-explorer-interface>, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/view/building-queries>, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/analyze/query-and-view>, read 2026-11-20): the Fields pane is Google's three sections, Pinned (a pinned field's Remove), **System metadata** (Resource type and Severity, then Log name and Project ID) and **Frequent fields** (the most frequent `json_payload` fields in the current query results, each with its values), every value with its approximate count, choosing one refining the query; the Explorer's entries carry their `jsonPayload`, so the query editor matches `jsonPayload.*`. Recent queries are the user's (`recentQueries.list`), in the Query library and in Log Analytics' panel. Log Analytics' chart has **Save to dashboard**: a new or existing custom dashboard, the chart title, a Bar or Line chart or a Table, saved as a widget whose query is the SQL (`opsAnalyticsQuery`); the dashboard's page lists the query (`TestLoggingConsoleRound5`).
- [x] #2032 (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/view/logs-explorer-interface>, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/analyze/charts>, read 2026-11-20): System metadata has Google's label dimensions where the entries carry the labels they are made from: **Workload / Service**, after Resource type and Severity, from the `k8s-pod/app` label and `service.name`, each value adding its own label's clause (`labels."k8s-pod/app"=…`); the Explorer's entries carry their labels, so the query editor matches `labels.KEY`. A dashboard's Log Analytics chart (`opsAnalyticsQuery`) is drawn: its SQL runs through Log Analytics, as the Log Analytics page runs it, and the results are charted as that page charts them, with the default chart configuration (Google saves no custom one), a Bar or Line chart, or a table for a widget saved as a Table; where Log Analytics is not served, or the query fails, the chart says why (`TestLoggingConsoleRound6`, `TestDashboardDrawsLogAnalyticsCharts`).
- [ ] Not yet: the metrics list's columns are not named by Google's docs (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-19: https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/logs-based-metrics/counter-metrics). The Fields pane counts the latest 1,000 matching entries the console holds. System metadata's **Application** dimension and Workload / Service's App Hub sources (`apphub.application.id`, `apphub.workload.id`, `apphub.service.id`) are not drawn: the LogEntry this instance serves (the v2 protos) has no App Hub field, so no entry carries them; nor is Workload / Service made from an Audited Resource's `resource.labels.service`, as the Resource menu's types are the console's source products, not Google's monitored-resource types. A dashboard's Log Analytics chart runs its SQL as saved: Google's docs (analyze/charts, read 2026-11-20) do not say how the dashboard's time range applies to it, so none is applied.

#### Cloud Run (#1852)

- [x] Menu: Services, Jobs, Worker pools, Domain mappings, then CloudBurrow's Instances. Build history is Cloud Build's History now (#1854); `/run/builds` and a build's address under it lead there.
- [x] Services list: Google's Deployment type (Container or Function), Region, Authentication (Allow public access when allUsers holds roles/run.invoker, else Require authentication), Ingress, Last deployed and Deployed by ("Cloud Functions" for a function), then CloudBurrow's URL, Image, Revision, Deploying, Reason, Detail and Age. Req/sec and Recommendation are absent: no Cloud Monitoring or Recommender runs locally.
- [x] Deploy container opens the Create service page: Configure (Container image URL, Service name, Region), Authentication (Allow public access or Require authentication; public access is the service's IAM policy, stored and not enforced), Billing (Request-based, the one the adapter maps), Service scaling, Ingress (All, the one the adapter maps), and Containers, Volumes, Networking, Security with the Container(s), Variables & Secrets, Volumes, Networking and Security tabs, ending in Create. Every field of the old form is on it.
- [x] Service details tabs: Metrics and SLOs (each "Not available locally", as Cloud Monitoring is not run), Logs (local Cloud Logging), Revisions (with each revision's traffic share and tags, and Manage traffic), Source (the image, with Deploy from source), Triggers, Containers, Networking, Security, Scaling, YAML (the v1 Service), then CloudBurrow's Configuration and Domain mappings tabs, with Map a domain on the latter; Edit and deploy new revision beside the title. Integrations is absent: Google discontinued Cloud Run integrations from the console (release notes, 2025-02-07).
- [x] Editing on the tabs: Containers (Container(s), Variables & Secrets and Volumes), Networking (VPC connector, egress, session affinity), Security (service account, encryption key) and Scaling (service scaling, minimum and maximum instances, utilization targets) each show the service's current settings under a form of their own fields, ending in View diff & redeploy. It opens the review: each setting changed, its current and its new value (or No changes), and Cancel or Deploy changes, which deploys a new revision through UpdateService; the other tabs' settings are kept as the service holds them. `TestCloudRunServiceTabsCopyAndRevisionMenuThroughTheBrowser`.
- [x] Domain mappings page (`/run/domains`): every mapping of the project, Add mapping (a service, then the domain and certificate mode), each mapping's page with its DNS records, and Delete.
- [x] Job details tabs: History (the executions), Metrics (not available locally), Logs, Triggers, YAML (the v1 Job), Configuration; View and edit job configuration. Create job starts with the Container image URL and ends in Create. The execution's page has Tasks, Logs, Conditions, Configuration.
- [x] Each revision's ⋮ menu: Manage traffic, Manage revision tags and, on a revision that serves no traffic, Delete, as Google's "three-dot vertical ellipse ... then click Delete".
- [x] Services list: each row has a checkbox; Delete and Copy act on the checked service. Copy opens Google's service copy page: the Create service page (`/run/create?from=`) holding the checked service's settings as its serving revision has them, named `<service>-copy`, where any value can be changed before Create. Filter services lists the properties, and a property chosen lists the values the rows hold, so Deployment type > Function is two clicks.
- [x] Revisions tab: Manage traffic is Google's dialog of rows (a revision, the latest healthy revision among the choices, and the percent of requests it receives; Remove on each row, Add revision under them, the total, which must be 100; ending in Save; the service's tags are kept), and each revision has Manage revision tags (one tag per line; removing a line deletes the tag; ending in Save), on its row and its page.
- [x] Triggers tab: the Eventarc triggers whose destination is the service (Trigger name, Event provider, Event type, Filters, Region, Service URL path, Transport topic), through the official Eventarc client on this instance's Eventarc (#1877); Add trigger (Trigger name, Event type, Cloud Pub/Sub's messagePublished or Cloud Storage's finalized, deleted, archived and metadataUpdated, labelled with their provider; Select a Cloud Pub/Sub topic, or Create a topic, which Eventarc makes; Bucket; Region; Service account; Service URL path; ending in Save trigger) and Delete trigger. Each event reaches the service as a CloudEvent in binary content mode. Audit-log, Firestore and Firebase events and channels are not served; without Pub/Sub, Eventarc's transport, the tab says so. `TestCloudRunEventarcTriggersThroughTheBrowser`.
- [x] Copy beside a service's title: a new service with the copied one's containers, variables, volumes, scaling and labels, through GetService and CreateService.
