Write a function on the Services list: Cloud Functions v2's Create function (where Cloud Storage runs), its source written in Google's inline editor (the files beside a code editor, Add file and Delete file, starting from the chosen runtime's sample: index.js and package.json, main.py and requirements.txt, or function.go and go.mod), zipped and uploaded as gcloud functions deploy does.
- [x] Connect repo on the Services list: Google's continuous deployment set up with Cloud Build. Source repository (Repository Provider, Repository: a Developer Connect git repository link to one of this instance's Secure Source Manager repositories; GitHub, GitLab and Bitbucket are hosted services no instance reaches), Build Configuration (Branch, a regex, default ^main$; Build Type, Dockerfile with its Source location, or Google Cloud's buildpacks with the Build context directory, Entrypoint and Function target), then Service name and Region, ending in Create. Create makes the service with Google's placeholder image and the Cloud Build trigger Google's console makes (Build, Push, and Deploy with gcloud run services update, labelled with the commit), and runs it once; each push to a matching branch deploys a new revision.
- [x] Create job: Container image URL, Job name, Region, Number of tasks; Task capacity with Task timeout (default 10) and its Time unit (seconds, minutes or hours; at most 168 hours) and Number of retries per failed task; Parallelism, Run as many tasks concurrently as possible or Limit the number of concurrent tasks with a Custom parallelism limit; Execute job immediately, which runs the job once it is created. View and edit job configuration has neither Region nor Execute job immediately, and is submitted with Update.
- [x] Worker pools: Deploy container opens Create worker pool, in Google's order (Container image URL, Worker pool name of at most 49 characters, Region, Scaling, then Containers, Networking, Security), ending in Create; a pool's Edit and deploy new revision opens Deploy worker pool revision, ending in Deploy.
- [ ] Not yet, tracked by #1877 rather than #1852: Add trigger offers only Google sources' direct events (Pub/Sub and Cloud Storage), not audit-log, Firestore or Firebase events or third-party channels. (Build history moved to Cloud Build, #1854.)

#### Cloud Run functions (#1853)

Shared additions: a list screen opens the list action its address names once (`?action=ID`, as a resource page already did, #1851), so the functions list's Create function, an `Action.Href` to `/run?action=write-function`, lands in Cloud Run's Write a function. The adapter serves a 2nd-gen function's own Cloud Audit Logs and Firestore event triggers (`event_trigger`), on a topic Eventarc makes, as Eventarc's own triggers are served (#1877). Round 3 adds two more, both additive: `Listing.NameAfter`, the data column a list draws its name column after (Google's functions list leads with Environment, then Name), and `Action.Then`, the console route a form's action opens once it succeeds, each `{field}` in it the value submitted (Write a function's Create lands on the function's Source tab); and the inline source editor's **Rename file**, beside Add file and Delete file (`TestNameAfterAndThenReachThePage`).

- [x] Product and list: the drawer row and the page are Cloud Run functions (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/functions/comparison>), listing both 1st gen (v1 API) and 2nd gen (v2 API) functions with Google's Environment, Name, Region, Trigger, Runtime, Memory allocated, Executed function, Last deployed, Deployed by and Authentication columns, then CloudBurrow's URL. Authentication is Allow public access when allUsers may invoke (the Cloud Run service's roles/run.invoker for 2nd gen, the function's roles/cloudfunctions.invoker for 1st gen), else Require authentication.
- [x] Create function opens Cloud Run's Write a function (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/deploy-functions>), its inline editor and Environment choice (1st gen is still created there, through the v1 API); Create from a ZIP upload keeps the archive path, ending in Deploy, as 1st gen's ZIP upload does (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/functions/1stgendocs/deploy>).
- [x] Function page tabs, Google's order (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/functions/1stgendocs/deploy>): Metrics (not available locally: no function metrics are produced), Details, Source (the files of the archive GenerateDownloadUrl returns, one per tab; Edit source, the inline editor with Add file and Delete file, the runtime and the Function entry point, ending in Save and redeploy: GenerateUploadUrl, then UpdateFunction of build_config.source, runtime and entry point, or the v1 sourceUploadUrl, runtime and entryPoint), Variables (Runtime environment variables, Build environment variables, Secrets), Triggers, Permissions, Logs, Testing. Edit function, Copy function, Download source and Delete beside the title.
- [x] Triggers tab: the HTTP trigger with its URL, the function's own event trigger (Edit trigger: UpdateFunction's event_trigger, 2nd gen), and the Eventarc triggers whose destination is the function or its Cloud Run service, with Add trigger (Cloud Pub/Sub, Cloud Storage, Cloud Firestore or Cloud Audit Logs, destination the function) and Delete trigger, through the official Eventarc client.
- [x] Event triggers on create and edit: Trigger type HTTP, Cloud Pub/Sub (topic), Cloud Storage (bucket, event), Cloud Firestore (event, database, document path or pattern) and Cloud Audit Logs (service name, method name, resource name or pattern), each field shown only for its type. 1st gen takes HTTP, Pub/Sub and Storage, as the v1 adapter serves; a 1st gen Firestore or audit-log trigger is refused naming 2nd gen.
- [x] Testing tab: the Test command (curl with an identity token for 2nd gen, `gcloud functions call` for 1st gen) and Test the function, Triggering event `{"name":"Hello World"}`: a POST of the JSON to the function's URL for 2nd gen, the v1 API's CallFunction for 1st gen.
- [x] Copy function: the function's archive uploaded again and CreateFunction with its settings (runtime, entry point, memory, variables, secrets, trigger), under a new name and region; the copy's page shows its deploy.
- [x] 1st gen: served (the Cloud Functions v1 API, #1428), so 1st gen functions are listed and have the same tabs.
- [x] Round 3, the list: Environment first, then Name, as Google's columns run (Listing.NameAfter).
- [x] Round 3, Write a function (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/deploy-functions>, read 2026-11-20): **Service name** (Google's rule: a letter first, at most 49 letters, numbers or hyphens, not ending in a hyphen; "A service name cannot be changed later"), **Region**, **Runtime**, **Trigger** and **Authentication** in that order, Authentication's **Allow public access** ("for a public HTTP function such as a webhook") or **Require authentication** ("for an event-triggered function"), the default. Allow public access grants allUsers roles/run.invoker on the function's Cloud Run service through the Run API's SetIamPolicy (roles/cloudfunctions.invoker through the v1 API's for 1st gen), and the list's Authentication column then reads Allow public access. Create from a ZIP upload has the same Authentication. **Create** lands on the function's **Source** tab, as Google's "You're redirected to the Source tab" (Action.Then). Two fixes on the way: Cloud Run's screen now has the functions page's 30-minute budget, so Write a function's Create no longer answered "did not answer in time" while its build ran, and the function and service name patterns escape their hyphen, which Chrome's `v`-flag pattern check refused (`TestCreateFormPatternsAreValidInTheBrowser` now checks both forms).
- [x] Round 3, Write a function's settings after Authentication, as Google's form lists them ("configure billing, service scaling, ingress control, and the Container(s), Volumes, Networking, Security section"): **Billing** (Request-based, the one the adapter maps), **Service scaling** (Minimum number of instances, 0, and Maximum number of instances, 100: the v2 API's min_instance_count and max_instance_count, or the v1 minInstances and maxInstances), **Ingress** (All, ALLOW_ALL), then the tabs: Container(s) (Memory, CPU, Request timeout, Maximum concurrent requests per instance), Variables & Secrets (Environment variables, secret environment variables), Volumes (secret volumes) and Security (Service account, the customer-managed key, the Binary Authorization policy). Each reaches the function's ServiceConfig and so its Cloud Run service; a 1st gen function refuses CPU and concurrency, and a timeout over 540 s (`TestFunctionSettingsAreChecked`, `TestConsoleFunctionsParity`).
- [x] Round 3, the Source tab: **Show Payload** ("an example payload of incoming events"): an HTTP function's JSON body, `{"name":"Hello World"}`, which Test sends; an event function's CloudEvent in binary mode, its ce-specversion, ce-type, ce-source, ce-subject, ce-id and ce-time and its provider's data (Pub/Sub's message and subscription, Cloud Storage's object, Firestore's document as JSON, said to be delivered as protobuf, an audit log entry's protoPayload); a 1st gen event function's data and context. **Test** beside it, Google's Test button: the Testing tab's Triggering event and answer. Edit source's editor has **Rename file** beside Add file and Delete file (`TestWriteFunctionIsGooglesForm`, `TestShowPayloadIsTheTriggersEvent`, `TestConsoleFunctionsParity`, in headless Chrome `TestCloudRunFunctionsRound3ThroughTheBrowser`, with the WCAG audit of the list, Write a function, the Source tab, Show Payload and Edit source).
- [x] Round 4 (#2049), Deployed by: the account that last deployed the function's Cloud Run service, the Run API's output-only `last_modifier` (Google's "Email address of the last authenticated modifier"), on the functions list and Cloud Run's Services list. The Run adapter now records `creator` and `last_modifier` on every service it writes: the account the request's bearer token was minted for, or the instance's own account for a request with none, as Secure Source Manager names its caller (`TestServiceRecordReadsBack`, `TestDeployedByIsTheLastModifier`).
- [x] Round 4, Metrics: Cloud Monitoring now serves Cloud Run's built-in metrics, `run.googleapis.com/request_count`, `request_latencies` and `container/instance_count` on `cloud_run_revision` (Google's display names, units and labels), sampled each minute from the cluster: requests and latencies from the ingress gateway's Envoy statistics per revision, instances from the revision's pods (active when ready, idle otherwise). A function's and a service's Metrics tab charts Request count, Request latencies (99th, 95th and 50th percentiles) and Container instance count from ListTimeSeries, through the official Monitoring client (`TestRunBuiltinServesGooglesRunMetrics`, `TestRunBuiltinThroughListTimeSeries`, `TestParseEnvoyStatsCountsEachRevision`, `TestRunMetricsSectionChartsGooglesThreeMetrics`). Memory utilization is not charted: the kubelet's per-pod readings are the console's pod Metrics, not a Monitoring metric yet.
- [x] Round 4, the Source tab's **Base image** (the runtime's Google base image, `REGION-docker.pkg.dev/serverless-runtimes/google-22-full/runtimes/RUNTIME`, and its security update policy) with **Edit runtime and security updates** ("Select a different Runtime ... Click Save and redeploy", <https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/deploy-functions>, read 2026-11-20): Runtime and Security updates (Automatic, the default, or On deploy), saved by UpdateFunction of `build_config.runtime` and `build_config.automatic_update_policy` or `on_deploy_update_policy`, which the adapter now serves: automatic is the default, and on deploy records the base image the deploy built on as `runtime_version`. Locally the base image is the builder's, so no patch is applied between deploys.
- [x] Round 4, Write a function's **Add trigger** is Google's Eventarc trigger pane (Trigger name, Trigger type Google Sources, Custom or Third-party, Event provider, Event type, the event's filters, Region, Service account, Service URL path), its providers and event types read from the served Eventarc API's ListProviders and its channels from ListChannels; the trigger is created through CreateTrigger with the function's Cloud Run service as its destination, and the Triggers tab's Add trigger is the same pane, ending in Save trigger (to the function itself, Eventarc's `destination.cloud_function`, while the function has no service yet, as one whose deploy failed) (`TestEventarcPane*`).
- [x] Round 4, Write a function's **Create** makes the service "using a placeholder revision" through the Cloud Run API, and lands on its Source tab, where **Save and redeploy** deploys the source. The placeholder is Google's image, `us-docker.pkg.dev/cloudrun/container/placeholder`, which the adapter runs as the Envoy image the instance's gateway already runs (by digest, never pulled from Google), answering every request with a placeholder page; it reads back as Google's image. The service carries Run's `build_config` (function target, base image, the uploaded source's `gs://` location, automatic updates), which the adapter now stores and returns. Save and redeploy is CreateFunction through the v2 API with the service's settings; the function's deploy builds the source and updates the same service, keeping what Cloud Run set on it (execution environment, VPC access, HTTP/2, session affinity, encryption key and its volumes). Once the function exists the service's Source tab is the function's.
- [x] Round 4, Write a function's remaining settings, in Google's tabs: Container(s) has the Execution environment and Cloud SQL connections, Volumes the in-memory, Cloud Storage and NFS volumes beside the secret ones, and Networking Use HTTP/2 end-to-end, the VPC connector and egress and Session affinity; each is set on the service as the Cloud Run deploy form sets it (stored settings stay stored without effect, as there: no VPC exists locally). Write a function no longer asks for an Environment, as Google's does not (`TestWriteFunctionIsRound4sForm`, `TestPlaceholderServiceCarriesTheForm`).
- [x] Round 4, the Testing tab's **Run in Cloud Shell** runs the test command in the console's terminal (#781), a new `Action.Terminal`: the terminal opens, and the command is sent to its active tab once its shell is attached. The terminal is a pod, where the function's URL names no host, so the curl reaches the URL's host through the cluster's ingress gateway (`--connect-to ::kourier.kourier-system.svc.cluster.local:80`); the identity token is empty, as gcloud there has no credentials, and invoker IAM is not enforced locally.
- [x] Round 4 in headless Chrome, `TestCloudRunFunctionsRound4ThroughTheBrowser`, which runs on arm64 and amd64 alike: Write a function's Add trigger pane with providers from ListProviders and the new tabs; Create with Allow public access and the second generation environment, landing on the placeholder's Source tab; the Run API reads back the placeholder image, `build_config`, `last_modifier` and allUsers as invoker; the placeholder answers at the service's URL, and those requests are `run.googleapis.com/request_count` through the official Monitoring client and the Metrics tab's charts; Deployed by on the Services list is the `last_modifier`; a function's Base image and Edit runtime and security updates, whose On deploy reads back as `on_deploy_update_policy`; and Run in Cloud Shell runs the command in the terminal; then the WCAG audit of Write a function with Add trigger, the placeholder's Source tab, the Metrics tab and Edit runtime and security updates. The placeholder's **Save and redeploy** builds with Google's builder, which makes amd64 images only, so it is submitted only where builds are asked for (CLOUDBURROW_TEST_FUNCTIONS) on amd64 nodes, an amd64 CI run; there it checks that the built function serves and that the service kept Write a function's execution environment. The run shard now serves Cloud Monitoring, and its browser step is given MONITORING and KUBECONFIG.

#### Kubernetes Engine (#1857)

What is served: the Kubernetes Engine API (`container.googleapis.com` v1) with `--services gke` (#1913): clusters, node pools and operations, each cluster a kind cluster of its own. The instance's own kind cluster has a real Kubernetes API, which these pages read through kubectl. Shared additions: `Action.Unavailable` draws a page action disabled with its reason (the provider refuses it too); a route's `pageTabs` draws sibling list pages as one page's tabs (links, the route's `tabTitle`, `aria-current` on the one shown), and `navAs` marks the menu entry a hidden sibling belongs to. Round 2 (shared, additive): a row's action marked `Unavailable` is drawn disabled in the row's menu with its reason, as on a page, and a dialog whose every field is immutable (Connect) puts focus on its first button. Round 3 (#2070, shared, additive): a listing's `CheckTree` is drawn in place of the shared filter bar, above the sticky bar, as a fieldset of checkboxes, each group with a disclosure button for its children and drawn mixed while some are unchecked; a `ChartSeries.Unavailable` keeps its chart card, saying why it has no readings; `PodMetrics` carries the kubelet summary's per-pod `ephemeral-storage` used bytes.

- [x] Menu, from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/concepts/dashboards> (re-read 2026-11-19): Clusters, Workloads, Applications, Secrets & ConfigMaps, Gateways, Services & Ingress, Storage, Object Browser, Backup for GKE, Config, Policy, Security. Applications, Backup for GKE, Config (Config Sync), Policy (Policy Controller) and Security (security posture) are services CloudBurrow does not run: each says "Not available locally" and why. Pods, Jobs, Nodes and Events are not in Google's menu; their pages stay (`navHidden`), reached from a workload's Managed pods, Workloads (Jobs and CronJobs are workloads), a cluster's Nodes tab and Object Browser.
- [x] Kubernetes clusters: the instance's one cluster, with Status, Name, Location, Tier, Mode, Number of nodes, Total vCPUs, Total memory, Notifications, Labels (a kind cluster has no tier, release channel, notifications or labels: "—"). Create cluster is a page with the Cluster mode choice (Autopilot, Standard), Cluster basics (Name, Location type, Region, Release channel, Version), Fleet registration, Networking and, for Standard, the default-pool node pool (Number of nodes, Machine type), from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/how-to/creating-an-autopilot-cluster> and creating-a-zonal-cluster; both modes are drawn disabled with the reason, and Create is refused.
- [x] Cluster details: Details (Cluster basics: name, location, version, total size, external endpoint, created; Networking), Nodes (each node's status, roles, CPU requested, allocatable CPU, memory and storage, version, opening its node page), Storage (persistent volumes), Observability (not available locally: Cloud Monitoring), Logs. Connect shows Command-line access: `export KUBECONFIG=…`, `kubectl config use-context kind-…`, in place of `gcloud container clusters get-credentials`, which needs the Kubernetes Engine API. Edit, Upgrade and Delete are drawn disabled with the reason.
- [x] Workloads: row checkboxes and one **Delete** for the checked workloads, from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/how-to/stateless-apps> (re-read 2026-11-20): "In the workloads list, select one or more Deployments to delete. Click Delete. When prompted to confirm, click Delete." The confirmation is "Delete N workloads?" with Delete, nothing typed; a Kubernetes system object among the checked refuses the whole selection with the reason before anything is deleted, as its row's Delete is drawn disabled with it (`TestGKEWorkloadsListDeletesTheChecked`).
- [x] Workloads: Name, Status, Type (Deployment, Stateful Set, Daemon Set, Job, Cron Job), Pods, Namespace, Cluster; top-level objects only (a Deployment's ReplicaSets and a CronJob's Jobs are not rows); Kubernetes system objects (kube-system and the other kube-, knative- and gke- namespaces, local-path-storage, and CloudBurrow's own components) are hidden by default, with the Kubernetes system objects picker to show them, as "By default, Kubernetes system objects are filtered out". Deploy opens Create deployment: Image path, Application name, Namespace, Replicas (3), Kubernetes cluster, made with `kubectl create deployment` (refused into a system namespace).

Overview's Utilization is Google's "resource utilization" (concepts/dashboards, re-read 2026-11-20): CPU (cores, each pod's rate between its kubelet counters), Memory (working set bytes) and Disk (ephemeral storage bytes: writable layers, logs, emptyDir), each summed over the managed pods from the readings a pod's Metrics charts draw. A sample where one of the pods has no reading is a gap, never a partial sum; a metric no pod reports (Disk, where the kubelet reports no ephemeral storage), a workload with no pods, or an instance without the sampler says so in the chart's place (`TestGKEWorkloadOverviewUtilization`).
- [x] Workload actions, from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/how-to/stateless-apps> (re-read 2026-11-20): on a workload's page and in its row's menu on Workloads, **Edit replicas** (Google's Actions > Scale > Edit replicas: Replicas), **Autoscale** (Minimum and Maximum number of replicas, CPU utilization target: a HorizontalPodAutoscaler), **Expose** (Port mapping: Port, Target port, Protocol; Service type: Cluster IP, Node port, Load balancer; Service name, `<name>-service`), **Rolling update** (each container's image; for a Deployment, Minimum seconds ready, Maximum surge and Maximum unavailable) and **Delete**; Scale and Autoscale for a Deployment or Stateful Set, Expose and Rolling update for those and a Daemon Set, Delete for every workload, a Job's and Cron Job's too. Ingress: Name, Status, Type, Frontends, Namespace, Cluster; its page is Details (host, path, backend), Events, YAML. Gateways lists the Gateway API's Gateways and says it is not available when the cluster serves no Gateway API (kind installs none).

#### Cloud Build (#1854)

Shared additions: a route's `redirectTree` carries the addresses below a moved page (`/run/builds/ID` to `/cloud-build/builds/ID`). The adapter now runs a Developer Connect trigger's `filename` (the config file read from the pushed commit) and `autodetect` (cloudbuild.yaml, cloudbuild.json, else the Dockerfile); off a link both stay refused. Round 2: a list screen can take selection actions on its rows marked selectable (`SelectionLister`, History's Approve); a row action can be a `button` on the row before its menu, and a `RowEditLeader` puts Edit first in the menu; a create page can read its fields for the selected project (`ProjectCreateFormer`, `/api/createform/{service}`); a listing can be its summary cards alone (`summaryOnly`). The adapter runs a build and a trigger as a user-specified service account (#1882): every step's `GCE_METADATA_HOST` names it, so the metadata server and its tokens are the account's. Round 3: a Property's `Chart` draws it as a bar chart, each bar labelled for hover, focus and a screen reader; a SummaryOnly screen's card can carry a `Pin` (pinned cards first, kept per viewer in the browser) and `Filter` text its `CardFilter` box narrows by; `OptionalColumns` stay off until chosen in the column selector; a `RowDeleteIconer`'s Delete is a trash icon on its rows. The edit-as-page flag (`EditForm.Page`, #1855) draws Edit private pool. The adapter clones a build's git source `dependencies` from this instance's Secure Source Manager repositories, by git URL or Developer Connect link, into `/workspace/<destPath>` before the steps run (#2074), and refuses one on another host with Google's requirement. Round 4: a select's `OptionsWhen` gives its options while other fields hold values (ShowWhen's conditions), so Create private pool's Series and Machine type offer the chosen region's (#2073).

A build's page: Build summary (the steps, each opening its own log), Build log, Execution details (options, substitutions, environment), Build dependencies (each git dependency's repository URL, revision, destination path and depth: the dependencies the build cloned from this instance's repositories, #2074) and Build artifacts (images with digests, objects); Rebuild (RetryBuild, Google's word for a rerun), Cancel, Approve and Reject (Message, and URL under Show additional options). While builds await approval, each such row has a checkbox, and Approve or Reject at the top of the page decides every one checked, with one Message and URL (ApproveBuild for each).

Create trigger is one page: Name, Region, Description, Tags; Event (Push to a branch, Push new tag, Pull request, Manual invocation, Pub/Sub message, Webhook event); Source (Repository, a Developer Connect link; Branch or Tag regex, or a pull request's Base branch; Invert Regex; for a pull request, Comment control: Required except for owners and collaborators, Required, Not required, #2077); Configuration (Autodetected; Cloud Build configuration file in the Repository or Inline; Dockerfile; Buildpacks); Substitution variables, Require approval before build executes, Private pool, Service account (the default build account, or one of the project's IAM accounts, which the builds then run as); Create. Repository, Private pool and Service account list the selected project's, read each time the page opens. Edit is the same page prefilled, ending in Save.

Edit (with Enable password policies and its Set password policy dialog, and Backup location, #1956), Stop on a running primary and Start on a stopped one (activationPolicy NEVER and ALWAYS, #1875; the list and Overview show it STOPPED; confirmed as Google's are, "Stop database instance?" and "Start database instance?" ending in Stop or Start, nothing typed), Import, Export, Restart, Clone, Failover, Upgrade, Promote and Delete are beside the title.

- [x] Round of 2026-11-22 (#2141), from Google's public docs only:
  - **Query insights** (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/sql/docs/postgres/using-query-insights>): Google's **Top queries** table, Query, Database, User (PostgreSQL only: MySQL's statement digests keep no user), Load by total time, Avg execution time (ms), Times called, Avg rows returned, read from the server's own `pg_stat_statements` (now preloaded on every PostgreSQL server, as Cloud SQL preloads it) or MySQL's `performance_schema` statement digests. They are the totals since the server started, not a chosen time range; the database load chart is drawn over them (below). An instance with Query insights off shows Google's **Enable Query insights** (Store client IP addresses, Store application tags, Customize query lengths 256 to 4500, the maximum sampling rate 0 to 20), through `instances.patch`; the instances `up` runs always show theirs.
  - **System insights** (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/sql/docs/postgres/use-system-insights>): Google's summary cards: CPU utilization - P99 | P50 (not available locally: it is a Cloud Monitoring series), Peak connections of `max_connections` (and those open now), Transaction ID utilization (PostgreSQL), Disk utilization of the instance's disk size, and Log errors, linking to the instance's Logs. The charts over a time range are not drawn.
  - Create instance: the root password has **Show password** and **Generate** (`Field.Generate`, a shared addition: 20 random characters from `crypto.getRandomValues`), MySQL's **No password** checkbox (a MySQL create with no password and No password unchecked is refused), Google's encryption labels "Google-managed encryption" and "Customer key-managed encryption key (CMEK)", **Data API access** under Connections, and under Data protection Choose where to store your backups (Multi-region, the default, or a region), how many automated backups to store (7), how many days of logs to retain (7) and Enable retained backups after instance deletion; Maintenance's Order of updates (Any, Earlier, Later, Week 5: labels UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-22: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/sql/docs/postgres/set-maintenance-window>); Query insights' options under its checkbox.
  - Delete instance (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/sql/docs/postgres/delete-instance>): the name typed, with **Take a final backup** (checked), Retain for (days) (30) and Describe this backup; the separate "Delete with final backup" action is gone.
  - **Create a clone** ending in Create clone: Instance ID, Clone current state of instance or Clone from an earlier point in time (a date and time). **Restore an instance from a backup**: Overwrite the source instance, Restore to a new instance (its Instance ID) or Overwrite an existing instance (another instance of the same engine).
  - Instances list row menu: Create read replica and Create clone (each opening on the instance's page), and Restart confirmed with "Restart database instance?". Failover: "Manually trigger a failover", Trigger Failover. Users: Host name (MySQL), Authentication, Password status. The Overview's **Operations and logs** (View all operations, View error logs).
  - Zones and private paths (#2141; <https://docs.cloud.google.com/sql/docs/postgres/create-instance>, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/sql/docs/postgres/configure-private-ip>, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/sql/docs/postgres/configure-private-service-connect>, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/sql/docs/instance-settings>, read 2026-11-22): Choose region and zonal availability's **Primary zone** and, for Multiple zones, **Secondary zone** (each the region's zones, Any first; `settings.locationPreference`, answered as `gceZone` and `secondaryGceZone`, swapped by a failover; a zone of another region, or the same zone twice, is refused). Connections' **Private IP**, then **Private Service Access (PSA)** with its **Network** (the project's Compute Engine VPC networks), **Show allocated IP range option** (the network's ranges allocated for private services access) and **Enable private path**, or **Private Service Connect (PSC)** with Allowed projects. A private IP is a `PRIVATE` entry of `ipAddresses`, taken from the network's allocated range (`NETWORK_NOT_PEERED` without one), and cannot be turned off once on, as Google's instance settings say; PSC answers the instance's `pscServiceAttachmentLink`. The private IP is reachable from the network's VMs: the VPC model's guest ruleset sends a connection to it on to the instance's server. The instance's **Connections > Networking** has **Edit** (Public IP, Private IP), ending in Save, and Summary names the network, allocated range and service attachment.
  - Backups' **Edit**, next to Settings, opens Google's **Manage automated backups** page (Automate backups, the 4-hour backup window; Advanced options: Multi-region or Region, the location, and Number of backups, 7 to 365), ending in Save (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/sql/docs/postgres/backup-recovery/backing-up>).
  - Monitoring charts: Cloud SQL's metrics (`cloudsql.googleapis.com/database/cpu/utilization`, memory, disk, network, connections and the engines' queries and transactions, on the `cloudsql_database` resource) are published into Cloud Monitoring every minute from the server pods' kubelet usage and the servers' own statistics; the Overview's chart has a metric select, CPU utilization first, and System insights' CPU utilization - P99 | P50 reads Monitoring's series.
  - Private Service Connect endpoints (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/vpc/docs/configure-private-service-connect-services>, read 2026-11-22): Network services' **Private Service Connect** page (`/net-services/psc/list/consumers`), its Connected endpoints with status, region, network, IP address, target and global access, and **Connect endpoint** (Target: Published service; Target service, the instance's service attachment; Endpoint name, Network, Region, Subnetwork; IP address: Create IP address, Assign automatically or Let me choose, or a reserved address; Enable global access), ending in **Add endpoint**. The endpoint is a Compute Engine forwarding rule, Accepted while its project is on the instance's allowed projects and Pending otherwise, and the network's VMs reach the instance at its address (an endpoint of another region only with global access).
  - Automatic endpoints (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/sql/docs/postgres/configure-private-service-connect>, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/vpc/docs/configure-service-connection-policies>, read 2026-11-22): Private Service Connect's **Connection Policies** tab (`/net-services/psc/list/policies`, beside Connected endpoints) lists the Network Connectivity API's service connection policies (name, region, network, service class, subnetworks, connection limit, connections), **Create connection policy** (Name, Description; Service details: Source service class Google services, Service class `google-cloud-sql`; Target endpoints scope: Network, Region, Custom service instance scope and its Scopes; Endpoints configuration: Subnetworks, Connection limit) ending in **Create policy**, and a policy's page (Details, Connections) with **Edit** ending in **Update policy**, and Delete. The create page's Private Service Connect has **Endpoint setup** with **Add an Endpoint**, each a Project and a Network (`pscAutoConnections`), made through the network's policy. The list's columns and the policy page's layout are UNVERIFIED (re-read 2026-11-22: not in the docs).
  - **Managed connection pooling** (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/sql/docs/postgres/managed-connection-pooling>, read 2026-11-22): Connections' **Enable Managed Connection Pool** on the create page (Enterprise Plus PostgreSQL) and in Connections > Networking's Edit, with **Advanced pooling options** (Pool mode Transaction or Session, and each pooler flag at Google's default); the Overview's Connect to this instance has a Managed connection pooling line (Enabled with the port, mode and pooler count, or Disabled). The options' labels are the flags' names (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-22: the page names none).
  - **Database load - all top queries** on Query insights (PostgreSQL), over the Top queries: CPU capacity, CPU and CPU wait, IO wait and Lock wait over the last hour, read from Cloud Monitoring's `cloudsql.googleapis.com/database/postgresql/insights/aggregate/execution_time`, `io_time` and `lock_time`, which the server's `pg_stat_statements` (execution, block read and write time) and `pg_stat_activity` (backends seen waiting on a lock at each minute's reading) give. The axis' unit is UNVERIFIED (re-read 2026-11-22: using-query-insights names none).
  - Not built, and why: Service Directory namespaces on Connect endpoint (not served); MySQL's managed connection pooling (a thread pool the community MySQL server lacks).

#### BigQuery (#1861)

Shared additions: `console.Studio` (`StudioCall`, served at `POST /api/studio/{service}?call=…`, recorded in the operations ledger as "studio <call>" without its body) for a product screen whose calls are not a listing; ROUTES `requires: "<service>"` keeps a page with no service of its own (Studio, a "Not available locally" page) out of the drawer, All products and the product menu while that service is not served; the schema field's **Edit as text** (the JSON array, drawn back into rows when turned off); radio choices drawn beside their labels (`.form-row.is-radio .check-line`).

- [x] Menu: Studio, Search, Datasets, Job history, Data transfers, Scheduled queries, Analytics Hub, Dataform, Partner Center, Settings; the drawer's BigQuery row opens Studio. Search (Dataplex Universal Catalog), Dataform, Partner Center and Settings (project configuration settings) are each in Google's place and say "Not available locally" and why. Datasets and Job history are CloudBurrow's list pages, kept. Data transfers and Scheduled queries are served since #1948 (next item). Analytics Hub (`/bigquery-sharing`, #1960) is served: the project's exchanges with Create exchange and Delete, and an exchange's page with its Listings, Subscriptions and Details tabs and Create listing (with the data egress options), Subscribe, Revoke subscription and Delete listing (`TestBigQueryAnalyticsHubThroughThePage`).
- [x] Data transfers (`/bigquery-transfers`) and Scheduled queries (`/bigquery-scheduled-queries`, #1948), from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/scheduling-queries> and <https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/copying-datasets> (read 2026-11-19): the list (Transfer config ID, Display name, Source, Schedule (UTC), Region, Destination dataset, Next scheduled, Last run); Create transfer, a page with Source type (Scheduled Query, Dataset Copy; Scheduled queries' Create scheduled query has none), Transfer config name, Repeat frequency (custom schedule), Destination dataset, Query, Destination table, Destination table write preference (Append to table, Overwrite table), Source project and dataset and Overwrite destination table for a copy, Pub/Sub topic and Send email notifications; a transfer's page with Run history (Run time, Status, Start time, End time, Message) and Configuration (Transfer details, Data source details), and Run transfer now, Disable transfer / Enable transfer, Schedule backfill (start and end dates) and Delete run. Failure emails are listed on the transfer's page, never sent. The column names and the field labels not in those docs are from the console as remembered (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-19: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/scheduling-queries>).
- [x] Studio (`/bigquery-studio`): the Explorer pane (Home, then the project, its Queries (saved queries), its datasets, then their tables, views, routines and models), with Search BigQuery resources, stars (kept in this browser) and Show starred resources only, and each node's View actions: the project's Create dataset, Refresh contents and Copy ID; a dataset's Create table, Open, Open in new tab, Query, Copy ID and Delete; a table's Open, Open in new tab, Query, Copy ID and Delete; a routine's or model's Open, Open in new tab, Copy ID and Delete; a saved query's Open, Copy link, Manage permissions (disabled, below) and Delete. "+ Add data" opens Create dataset.
- [x] Open from the Explorer opens the resource as a Studio tab, as Google's does (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/bigquery-web-ui>, read 2026-11-19): a dataset, table, view, routine or model page with its own tabs (a dataset's Overview first; a table's Schema, Details, Preview, ...), the sub-tab kept on the Studio tab. A plain click opens it in the preview tab (its name in italics), which the next click replaces; Ctrl or Command click, Open in new tab, or a double click on the tab keeps it. The node's name is still a link to the standalone page (`/bigquery/<dataset>[/<table>]`), kept as the deep link. `renderDetail` draws into a tab when its host is marked `data-embedded`: the page's title is an h2, and its sub-tab is not written to the address. The router no longer follows a link whose click a handler already took (`defaultPrevented`).
- [x] The Home tab, opened first and reopened from Home in the Explorer, in Google's sections: Check out what's new in Studio (says Google's list is not shipped), Create new (SQL query; Table, on a chosen dataset; Notebook, Apache Spark notebook, Data canvas, Data preparation and Pipeline disabled with their reasons), Recent (the ten resources and saved queries last opened, kept in this browser), Try with templates (three queries over this instance; Google's public-dataset templates are not shipped), Add your own data (Create dataset, Load from a file).
- [x] The workspace: SQL query tabs (kept for the browser tab; "SQL query" opens one, each closes), with Run, Save (Save query, Save query as), Share (Copy link; Manage permissions disabled), Schedule (disabled: a scheduled query is a Data Transfer Service transfer) and Edit: Query settings (Destination: a temporary table or a destination table with its write preference; Resource management: Job priority Interactive or Batch; Additional settings: SQL dialect GoogleSQL or Legacy SQL, Data location) and Clear query. Query settings is under Edit, where Google's docs put it ("click Edit > Query settings", <https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/running-queries> and <https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/table-explorer>, read 2026-11-19). ⌘/Ctrl + S saves. The validator dry-runs the statement as it is typed: "✓ This query will process 8 B when run.", or the API's refusal. Run runs a query job (jobs.insert, then getQueryResults and jobs.get); a statement that changes data is confirmed first, as the Read-write mode of a dataset's Query tab is (#994).
- [x] Saved queries, CloudBurrow-only: Google keeps a saved query as a Dataform code asset (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/work-with-saved-queries>), and CloudBurrow serves neither Dataform nor the BigQuery Studio assets API, so Save query (Name, Region) writes it to `bigquery-saved-queries.json` in the instance's state directory through `POST /api/studio/bigquery?call=savequery` (`savedqueries`, `deletequery`); each save of an open saved query is a new version, a name is unique in its project, the region is one of Google's and cannot be changed. The tab and the dialog say it is CloudBurrow-only. They are listed under the Explorer's Queries node (Google lists them in the Files pane, which is not drawn). Copy link opens Studio on the saved query (`?savedQuery=<id>`); Manage permissions (Code Owner, Code Editor, Code Viewer) is disabled with its reason: there is no IAM on a record only the console reads.
- [x] Query results: Job information (Job ID, User, Location, Creation, Start and End time, Duration, Bytes processed and billed, Job priority, Use legacy SQL, Destination table), Results, Visualization, JSON, Execution details and Execution graph; the last two say the job reports no query plan, as the local engine reports none. Visualization charts the rows returned, drawn locally as SVG: the Visualization configuration pane has Visualization type (Bar, Line, Scatter; Map disabled, as it needs Google Maps), Dimension (INT64, FLOAT64, NUMERIC, BIGNUMERIC, TIMESTAMP, DATE, DATETIME, TIME or STRING columns) and Measures (the numeric ones), prefilled from the schema and kept on the tab across runs; Zoom in, Zoom out, Toggle legend and Download PNG, as the docs list.
- [x] The left pane's tabs are Google's, in its order (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/bigquery-web-ui>, read 2026-11-20): **Explorer** (the tree above, with Home and Job history); **Data**, the same project > datasets > tables, views, routines and models tree without the code assets ("Code assets aren't listed in the Data pane"), so without Queries; **Files**, the project's saved queries, each with Open, Copy link and Delete (Google's folders and notebooks are Dataform's, not served, and the pane says so); **Source control**, "Not available locally" with the reason (Dataform's Git repositories). Arrow keys move between the four (#1861, round 3).
- [x] Job history is opened from the Explorer, as Google's is ("In the Explorer pane, click Job history", <https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/managing-jobs>, read 2026-11-20): a Job history tab in the workspace (one; a second click selects it) with Personal history and Project history (jobs.list, allUsers false or true), each job with Show job details and, for a query, Open as new query. It was a panel always drawn under the editor before round 3. A job's page offers Open as new query on a query job and Repeat load job on a load job, and neither on a copy or extract job ("You cannot repeat an extract job or a copy job using the Google Cloud console"); Job history lists Duration, User and Bytes processed; a job's page shows its Duration, User (a dash when the engine names none, as the list has), and for a query its Bytes processed and Bytes billed, the fields that page names.
- [x] Create dataset: in Studio a panel on the right (`openModal` draws an `is-side` dialog), the page `/bigquery/create` kept as its deep link. Dataset ID, Location type (Region, from Google's full list of regions named as "us-central1 (Iowa)", or Multi-region "US (multiple regions in United States)" or "EU (multiple regions in European Union)", from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/locations>, read 2026-11-19; any other location is refused), Link to an external dataset (disabled: not served), Tags (when the instance's Resource Manager holds tag values: key and value pairs, each checked before the dataset is made and bound with tagBindings.create on the dataset's full resource name once it is), then Advanced options: Encryption (Cloud KMS key disabled), Enable case insensitive table names, Default collation, Enable table expiration with Default maximum table age (days), Default rounding mode, Storage billing model, Time travel window (days); Create dataset. Each is sent with datasets.insert and kept by the front (measured 2026-11-19); the dataset's Details show them. Description and labels moved to Edit dataset, as Google's create form has neither. Shared: `Field.OptionLabels` (a select or radio option's words when they are not its value) and `ShowWhen` "name=a|b".
- [x] Create table, in Studio a panel on the right, in Google's sections: Source (Create table from: Empty table; Google Cloud Storage with its URI and file format, a load job as Load from Cloud Storage's; Upload, with Select file and File format, the file sent as Load from a file sends it and loaded with WRITE_EMPTY into the new table; Drive, Google Bigtable, Amazon S3 and Azure Blob Storage disabled), Destination (Project, Dataset, Table, Table type, View query: Project is the one chosen in the toolbar, another refused with the reason; Dataset lists the project's datasets, prefilled with the one the form was opened on, and the table, the load or the upload goes to the one chosen; round 3, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/tables>, read 2026-11-20), Schema (Auto detect for Cloud Storage and Upload; Add field rows or Edit as text), Partition and cluster settings (Partitioning, Partitioning column, Clustering order), Advanced options (Header rows to skip, Description, Encryption: Google-owned and Google-managed encryption key, Cloud KMS key disabled as on Create dataset, since the engine encrypts with none).
- [x] A dataset's page opens on Overview (was Tables), which holds the dataset's Tables, Routines and Models, each on a strip inside it (`Section.Group`), as Google's Overview lists tables, models and routines; Details follows (round 3: Routines and Models were tabs of their own; `?tab=routines` and `?tab=models` still open them). A table's tabs are Schema, Details, Preview, then Lineage, Data profile and Data quality, each "Not available locally" (Data Lineage API, Dataplex scans), then CloudBurrow's Partitions, Row access policies and Tags.
- [x] Split tabs (#2005), as Google's workspace "can split the tabs into two panes and view them side by side" (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigquery/docs/bigquery-web-ui>, read 2026-11-20): each tab's Open menu has Split tab to left and Split tab to right (unavailable while only one tab is open; the tab goes to that pane and every other to the other), then Move tab to left pane or Move tab to right pane, Close tab and Close other tabs; a table's View actions has Query in split tab (Google's Query > In split tab), opening the query in the other pane. Each pane has its own tab strip, SQL query button and selected tab; a new tab opens in the pane last used; a pane left with no tab closes. The panes, their tabs and each one's selection are kept for the browser tab, as the tabs are. Keyboard: Google's page lists no split-tab shortcuts, so the menu is the way, and it is all reachable by keyboard: Tab to a tab's Open menu, Enter opens it on its first item, Up and Down move between items, Escape closes it back to its button, and focus follows a moved tab; Left, Right, Home and End move between a strip's tabs. Narrow screens: Google's page says nothing, so below 960px, where the Explorer already stacks, the right pane stacks under the left. `TestBigQueryStudioSplitsTabsIntoTwoPanes`, `TestBigQueryStudioSplitTabsMeetWCAG` (the WCAG audit, reflow at 320px and 640px, and names, contrast, keyboard and reflow at 1440px).
- [ ] Not yet: Google's Data canvas and notebooks, the Files pane's folders and Source control's repositories (Dataform, not served; the panes say so); Recent tabs in the details pane; saved queries' Dataform-backed versions history, folders and IAM (CloudBurrow-only records instead, above); the Map visualization; Google's What's new list and public-dataset templates.

#### IAM & Admin (#1864)

Shared additions: `Field.DeriveFrom` (a field that follows another, lowercased with dashes, until it is edited by hand: Create service account's ID from its name); a top-level list's `Listing.SelectActions` (its rows' checkboxes feed the action's `SelectionField`, sent with an empty path as a `ListActor` action, and the list action of the same ID is drawn once, as the selection's button: Roles' Create role from selection, Audit Logs' Edit selected services); ROUTES `navAfter` (a route of another product's service listed in this product's menu after the page it names, placed after every product's own routes so a lookup by service still finds the owning product's page: Tags and Manage resources). The IAM page is a `console.Studio` screen (`POST /api/studio/iam?call=policy|roles|change`).

- [x] Menu, in Google's order: IAM, Identity & Organization, Policy Troubleshooter, Policy Analyzer, Organization Policies, Service Accounts, Workload Identity Federation, Workforce Identity Federation, Labels, Tags, Settings, Privacy & Security, Identity-Aware Proxy, Roles, Audit Logs, Manage resources, Groups, Essential Contacts, Asset Inventory, Quotas & System Limits, Privileged Access Manager, Principal Access Boundary. Identity & Organization (Cloud Identity's setup checklist), Policy Troubleshooter (troubleshootIamPolicy), Policy Analyzer (Cloud Asset's analyzeIamPolicy), Privacy & Security, Identity-Aware Proxy, Essential Contacts, Asset Inventory, Quotas, Privileged Access Manager and Principal Access Boundary are APIs CloudBurrow does not serve: each is in Google's place and says "Not available locally" and why. Policy Troubleshooter and Policy Analyzer are not built: IAM policies here are stored and never enforced (ADR-0006), so there is no access decision to explain, and neither API is served. Tags is Resource Manager's tag keys page and Manage resources (`/cloud-resource-manager`) its tree of the organization, folders and projects (#1865); Resource Manager has no drawer row of its own. Workload Identity Federation (#1941) is the IAM API's workload identity pools: the project's pools (Pool ID, Name, Status, Description, deleted ones with Restore), New workload provider and pool in Google's steps (the pool, an optional first provider, its attribute mapping and condition), a pool's page with its providers, Add provider (OIDC with issuer, allowed audiences and JWK file; AWS account; SAML IdP metadata; X.509 trust anchors), a provider's page with Edit provider, and Disable, Enable, Delete and Restore for pools and providers. Workforce Identity Federation (#2056) is the IAM API's workforce pools of the instance's organization, whatever project the toolbar has: the pools (Pool ID, Display name, Status, Description, Session duration, deleted ones with Restore), Create pool in Google's steps (the pool and its session duration, an optional first provider, its attribute mapping and condition), a pool's page with its providers and subjects, Add provider (OIDC with issuer, client ID, client secret, response type, assertion claims behavior and JWK file; SAML IdP metadata), a provider's page with Edit provider, Disable, Enable, Delete and Restore for pools and providers, and a subject's Revoke sessions, Delete and Restore.
- [x] IAM (`/iam-admin/iam`): Google's page, "Permissions for project "<id>"", "These permissions affect this project and all of its resources.", View by principals and View by roles, Grant access and Remove access on the title line, Include Google-provided role grants (service agents, `service-N@gcp-sa-…`, hidden until checked) and the filter. By principal: Type, Principal, Name (a service account's display name), Role (each role's title, with its condition), Inheritance (the folder or organization a role is granted on), and Edit principal; by role: each role with its principal count, expanded to its principals. Grant access is a side panel: New principals, then Assign roles, each role chosen with Select a role (a search over every predefined, basic and custom role by title and name), Add IAM condition (title, description, the CEL expression) and Delete, Add another role, Save: one SetIamPolicy for every principal and role. Edit principal is the same panel for one principal's roles, its inherited roles listed read-only. Remove access removes the checked principals' roles on the project. `/iam-admin/iam/<project>` keeps the policy's Permissions tab with Test permissions and the condition editor's Run Linter, linked from the page.
- [x] Service Accounts: Create service account is a page of Google's three steps: Service account details (Service account name, the Service account ID made from it, Service account description); Grant this service account access to project (Role, one per line, each checked); Grant users access to this service account (Service account users role, Service account admins role). The account's page tabs are Details, Permissions, Keys, Metrics (not available locally: Cloud Monitoring) and Logs.
- [x] Roles: Create custom role is Title, Description, ID, Role launch stage (Alpha, Beta, General Availability, Early Access, Deprecated, Disabled), Assigned permissions, then Create in; Create custom role from selection opens Create custom role with the checked roles' permissions that custom roles support listed, each checked, to uncheck the ones to exclude (#2013). The filter's Type chip separates Predefined and Custom roles.
- [x] Organization Policies (`/iam-admin/orgpolicies`): every constraint the instance knows, with Name, Policy source (Inherit parent's policy or Override parent's policy), Effective and Last edited; a constraint's Policy details have Manage policy and Delete policy.
- [x] Labels (`/iam-admin/labels`): the project's labels, Add label (Google's key and value rules, at most 64), Edit label, Delete. Settings (`/iam-admin/settings`): Project name, Project ID, Project number and Location, with Edit of the name.
- [x] Audit Logs: checking services opens the information panel, its tabs Log Types and Exempted Principals, ending in Save; several services are set in one SetIamPolicy; Set Default Configuration opens the same panel for allServices (#2013).
- [x] Policy Troubleshooter (`/iam-admin/troubleshooter`, #1879): Principal email, Resource (a full resource name, the project's by default) and Permission, then Check access: Access granted or Access not granted, and each allow policy's bindings with whether the role has the permission, whether the principal is included (directly or through a group), the condition and its value, and the result; a banner says whether `up --iam-enforcement` is on. Policy Analyzer (`/iam-admin/analyzer`, #1879): Scope, Principal, Permissions, Roles and Resource, then Analyze: the bindings that match, with their policy, role, principals, access and condition. Both call `iam:troubleshoot` and `analyzeIamPolicy` on the same evaluator enforcement uses.
- [x] Groups: Create group is one page: Group name, Group email, Group description, Security group, then Add members (Member email, Google Groups role).
- [x] Round 3: Roles' Create custom role and Edit role hold the permissions as Google's do: each assigned permission with a checkbox, unchecked to remove it, and Add Permissions, a picker of every permission Google supports in custom roles (Testing ones marked), narrowed by Service and a filter (`TestIAMCustomRolePermissionPicker`); Edit role ends in Update; checking custom roles enables Disable (their launch stage set to Disabled); Create custom role from selection is named as Google's is. Re-read 2026-11-20: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/iam/docs/creating-custom-roles>.
- [x] Round 3: Create service account makes the account at the first step's Create and continue, as Google's does, the page's fields then fixed; Done grants the project roles and the users to the account already made (`TestIAMCreateServiceAccountInThreeSteps`). A service account's Details tab has its Name and Description boxes and Save, in place of an Edit service account dialog.
- [x] Round 3: a group's page has Google's Add members (Member email, Google Groups role, Member expiry) over the Members table, Remove members over its checked rows, View in Google Groups (disabled, not available locally) and Delete group; Edit group is Group name, Group description and the member restriction, ending in Save (`TestIAMGroupPageButtons`).
- [x] Round 3: Audit Logs' table is titled "Data Access audit logs configuration"; Set Default Configuration, Edit log types and Edit selected services end in Save. Re-read 2026-11-20: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/audit/configure-data-access>.
- [x] Round 3 accessibility: Create custom role with its picker open, a custom role's page, Edit role, a group's page, Add members, a service account's Details and Audit Logs pass the WCAG 2.1 AA audit (`TestIAMRolesGroupsAndAccountScreensMeetWCAG`).
- [x] Round 4 (#2013): Grant access's Select a role groups the roles by product, as Google's role picker does: Basic, Custom, then each product down the left, a product chosen listing its roles, and the filter's hits (by title, name, product or type) under their products' headings. A predefined role's product is the title of the page Google's role index lists it on (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/iam/docs/roles-permissions>, read 2026-11-20: "Compute Engine" for .../roles-permissions/compute; the nine job function roles are on "Roles for job functions"), embedded as `internal/iamroles/products.tsv.gz`. The granting doc itself names only searching by role name, product, permission or job function (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/iam/docs/granting-changing-revoking-access>, re-read 2026-11-20) (`TestIAMSelectARoleByProduct`, `TestConsoleIAMRolePickerProducts`).
- [x] Round 4: Add Permissions has Google's All Services and All Types drop-downs. The doc names All Types but not what it lists (re-read 2026-11-20: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/iam/docs/creating-custom-roles>). Of the Permission's API fields (name, title, description, stage, customRolesSupportLevel, apiDisabled, primaryPermission), the only one with a type per service is the resource type in its name, service.RESOURCE.verb, so All Types lists the chosen service's resource types (`buckets`, `objects` for storage) (`TestIAMAddPermissionsAllTypes`).
- [x] Round 4: Create custom role from selection opens Create custom role (`/iam-admin/roles/create?roles=…`) with the checked roles' permissions listed, each checked, to uncheck the ones to exclude, then Add Permissions and Create, as Google's steps are. Permissions custom roles do not support are left out (the form says how many), and one a project role cannot hold is marked "(not in a project role)" (`TestIAMCreateRoleFromSelection`, `TestConsoleIAMCreateRoleFromSelection`).
- [x] Round 4: Audit Logs' checked services open Google's information panel beside the table, with the tabs Log Types and Exempted Principals: each exempted principal with its Disabled Log Types, Add exempted principal and Delete, then Save. One service checked shows its own configuration; several are configured together in one SetIamPolicy; Set Default Configuration opens the same panel for allServices. Exempting a principal from a log type enables that type, as the doc's caution says. The doc names the first tab Permission types once and Log Types twice; it is Log Types here (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/audit/configure-data-access>, re-read 2026-11-20) (`TestIAMAuditLogsInfoPanel`, `TestIAMAuditLogsFolder`, `TestConsoleAuditLogsPanel`).
- [x] Round 4: Security insights, the Recommender API's policy insights, is a column of the IAM page's principals table, as Google's is, each cell reading Not available locally (the Recommender API is not served). "Analyzed permissions" is not named in Google's docs (re-read 2026-11-20: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/iam/docs/recommender-managing>, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/iam/docs/managing-insights>, which name only Security insights on the IAM page), so no such column is drawn.
- [x] Round 4: Organization Policies. The list's columns stay UNVERIFIED from docs (re-read 2026-11-20: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/resource-manager/docs/organization-policy/creating-managing-policies>, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/organization-policy/apply-policies> and <https://docs.cloud.google.com/organization-policy/create-custom-constraints> name the Filter field, Policy details and the policy sources, no column). They are the API's fields: Constraint (name), Name (displayName), Policy source (Inherit parent's policy, Google-managed default for a reset policy, Override parent's policy), Effective and Last edited (updateTime). Edit policy is Google's: Policy source, Policy enforcement (Merge with parent, Replace), Add a rule, Enforcement, Policy values, Policy type, a Custom value box per value with Add value, then Set policy (`TestIAMOrgPolicyEditThroughThePage`, `TestConsoleOrgPoliciesPolicySource`).
- [x] Round 4 accessibility: Select a role open on its groups, Create custom role from selection, Add Permissions with All Types, the Audit Logs info panel on each tab, Set Default Configuration's panel and Edit policy's Custom value rows pass the WCAG 2.1 AA audit (`TestIAMRound4ScreensMeetWCAG`).

#### Firestore (#1845)

Shared additions: ROUTES `tabOf: { route, tab }` makes a menu entry one tab of the resource being looked at (the first segment after `route`, `(default)` elsewhere), current while that tab is open; its own address is a `redirect` to the default resource's tab. `console.KindQuery`, a placeholder `Section{ID: "query", Kind: KindQuery, Label: …}`, puts the query tab where the provider lists it and under its label, dropped on a page with no form. A `group` field's `select` subfield is a dropdown of its options.

- [x] Menu, Google's order (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/firestore/native/docs/using-console>, read 2026-11-19): Databases first, then Data, Query builder, Indexes, Import/Export, Disaster recovery, Time-to-live; each of the last five opens that tab of the database being looked at, or of (default). Security, Usage and Insights open the database's Rules, Usage and Query insights tabs (#2048); Key Visualizer is in Google's place and says "Not available locally" and why.
- [x] Databases list: Mode, Edition, Location, Scheduled backups (Daily, Weekly or None, from the database's backup schedules), then CloudBurrow's Concurrency and Delete protection.
- [x] Create a Firestore database is a page (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/firestore/native/docs/manage-databases>): Database ID, Edition (Standard / Enterprise), Data access mode (Firestore Native, or Datastore mode, served through the Datastore API since #1880), Security rules (Restrictive; Open disabled: no Firebase Rules API), Location type (Region from Google's full list, "us-central1 (Iowa)", or Multi-region eur3, nam5, nam7, from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/firestore/native/docs/locations>), Encryption options (Google-managed, or Cloud KMS key since #1881: a key field with Browse, which lists the project's ENCRYPT_DECRYPT keys of this instance's Cloud KMS in the database's Cloud KMS location, `us` for nam5 and `europe` for eur3; `TestFirestoreCreatesDatastoreModeAndKMSKeyDatabasesThroughThePage`), Disaster recovery (Enable point-in-time recovery, Enable delete protection); Create Database. Concurrency mode left the create page, as Google's has none; Edit database keeps it.
- [x] A database's page tabs: Query builder, Indexes (Manual: Create Index, Create vector index; Automatic: Add Exemption, rows Edit exemption, Delete exemption), Import/Export (the jobs table; Export, Import), Disaster recovery (the schedules, Edit, the Backups table), Time-to-live, then User credentials on an Enterprise database.
- [x] Query builder: Query scope Collection (a collection path) or Collection group, WHERE rows (Field, Operator ==, !=, <, <=, >, >=, array-contains, array-contains-any, in, not-in, Value type string, number, boolean, null or timestamp, Value), ORDER BY rows (Field, ascending or descending), LIMIT and Aggregation (COUNT, SUM, AVG of a field); Run runs it through the official client against that database's documents.
- [x] Time-to-live (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/firestore/native/docs/ttl>): the TTL policies table (Collection group, Timestamp field, Expiration offset, Status) with Delete on each row; Create Policy (Collection group name, Timestamp field name, Expiration offset with its unit: days, hours, minutes or seconds) then Create. The local Admin API now keeps the expiration offset. Policies are stored and ACTIVE; the local Firestore deletes no expired document.
- [x] Data is Google's three-column panel (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/firestore/native/docs/using-console>, read 2026-11-19), for every Native database of the project, chosen in its Database picker (`?database=`; the selection is `?path=`): the database's collections (inside a subcollection, the parent document's) with Start collection; the collection's documents with Add document, the filter button's Add filters (field, condition, value; Apply, Clear filter) and its menu's Delete collection (every document and their subcollections); the document's subcollections and fields with Start collection, Add field (Google's type dropdown: string, number, boolean, map, array, null, timestamp, geopoint, reference, bytes), a field edited by clicking it, a delete icon beside each, and its menu's Delete document and Delete document fields. Edit path opens any collection or document path; Panel settings is Google's Project panel settings (Show non-existent parents, Realtime updates enabled, Save). Each column's menu also opens the drill-down page, which stays.

Choose the state of your database to export's Export from an earlier point in time, with its snapshot time (UTC, at the minute), exports at that snapshot_time; it needs point-in-time recovery, as Google's does (#1942). Shared, additive: `Field.Browse: "storage"`.

The role is granted on the project with the condition Google's per-database steps write, `resource.name == "projects/P/databases/D"`, so the MongoDB front allows that user on that database alone. The database's page shows Google's base connection string (no credentials; the SCRAM form is in Add user's dialog), and the Data panel's database column has Google's Explorer View more, Connect using MongoDB tools, with the string and Copy, for an Enterprise database only (`TestConsoleFirestorePipelineAndUserCreds`, `TestFirestoreMongoSecurityAndConnectThroughThePage`).

- [x] Usage (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/firestore/native/docs/monitor-usage>, #2048): reads, writes and deletes by minute over the last 60 minutes, the Firestore front's counts of successful document operations, which Cloud Monitoring serves as `firestore.googleapis.com/document/read_ops_count` (LOOKUP, QUERY), `write_ops_count` (CREATE, UPDATE) and `delete_ops_count` (and `read_count`, `write_count`, `delete_count` on `firestore_instance`). Listen and Write streams are counted as Google bills them (#2050): a listener's read per document added or updated in its results (one for a query with none), a write stream's writes as CREATE, UPDATE or delete; the web SDK's WebChannel is not.
- [x] Query insights (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/firestore/native/docs/query-insights>, #2048): each normalized query (values replaced by `?`) of runQuery, runAggregationQuery, listDocuments, listCollectionIds and partitionQuery over the last 24 hours, with its method, execution and error counts, average duration, results and documents scanned, ranked by total execution time; Google documents these methods' executions as its source, which the front records as they pass. Index entries scanned and index recommendations are not shown: the emulator reports no index scans.
- [x] Key Visualizer stays "Not available locally" (#2048): Google generates a scan only for a database above 3,000 document operations in a minute, over up to 1,000 key buckets of its storage servers in 10-second segments (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/firestore/native/docs/key-visualizer>); the emulator is one process with no key ranges, so any heatmap would be invented.

#### Spanner (#1859)

Shared additions: `Detail.SideNav` draws a page's sections as a left-hand menu (a vertical tablist, up and down arrows) instead of a tab strip; `console.KindStudio` is a section drawn by console.js's `STUDIO_SECTIONS[service]` with the provider's `console.Studio` calls, and a page holding one has no separate Query tab; a `textarea` field's `Options` are templates, drawn as Show DDL templates with Insert.

- [x] Instances list, from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/spanner/docs/create-manage-instances> (read 2026-11-19): Instance ID, Instance name, Edition, Configuration, Compute capacity ("1 node (1000 processing units)" or "300 processing units"), Databases, Labels; Create instance on the title line.
- [x] Create instance, one page of Google's steps: Select an edition (Standard, Enterprise, Enterprise Plus; #1949), Name your instance (Instance name, the Instance ID made from it), Configure your instance (Regional with the regions, Multi-region nam3 and eur3; Dual-region disabled: none is served), Allocate compute capacity (Nodes or Processing units; Manual allocation or Autoscaling with its limits and targets), Backups (Enable default backup schedules, checked), Labels, ending in Create.
- [x] Instance page with Google's left-hand menu: Overview (the databases), Backup/Restore (Create backup at the top), Import/Export, Monitoring and System insights (each "Not available locally": Dataflow and Cloud Monitoring), then CloudBurrow's Instance partitions (Create instance partition at the top), operations, Configuration and Instance configurations; Edit instance, Create database, Move instance and Delete instance beside the title.
- [x] Create database (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/spanner/docs/create-query-database-console>): Database name, Select a database dialect (Google Standard SQL or PostgreSQL; the emulator serves both, measured 2026-11-19), Define your schema (optional) with Show DDL templates, and Encryption (Cloud KMS key disabled: the emulator encrypts nothing with one).
- [x] Database page with Google's menu: Overview (its tables), Spanner Studio, Indexes, Views, Change streams, Sequences, Roles, Backup/Restore (this database's backups, each with Restore and Delete backup; Create backup at the top), Backup schedules, Import/Export, Query insights, Lock insights and Key Visualizer ("Not available locally": Dataflow, SPANNER_SYS statistics and key heatmaps the emulator does not keep), then DDL and Properties; Update schema, Create backup schedule, Create backup, Delete database (was Drop database) and Drop protection beside the title. The catalogue is read from INFORMATION_SCHEMA in the database's own dialect.
- [x] Spanner Studio: the Explorer (the database, Tables with their columns and View actions Preview data, Insert data and Open table page; Views, Indexes, Change streams, Sequences), editor tabs (Untitled query, New tab, each closable, kept for the browser tab), Run, Explain and Clear, and the Results and Explanation tabs. Run runs a query read-only, one DML statement in a read-write transaction and DDL through UpdateDatabaseDdl, a change after a confirmation naming the database. Run runs a query in WITH_STATS mode (Google's Studio uses PROFILE, which adds the plan), and the Explanation tab's Query summary shows its statistics (Rows returned and Elapsed time from the emulator; CPU time and Rows scanned, which it does not report, say so). Explain is AnalyzeQuery (PLAN mode). The emulator returns no query plan (its README: "The SQL query modes PLAN and PROFILE do not return any query plans"), and a plan drawn from the query's text would not be Spanner's optimizer's, so the Spanner front refuses PLAN, PROFILE and WITH_PLAN_AND_STATS UNIMPLEMENTED (#2017, `TestSpannerQueryModes`) and the plan says "Not available locally" with that refusal.
- [x] Table page: Schema, Indexes and Data; Data lists up to 200 rows by key, with Insert row beside the title and Edit row and Delete row on each row (GoogleSQL scalar columns, each value cast to its column's type; an empty nullable value is NULL; a key cannot change).
- [x] Round 2 (#1859): Create instance folds each step with Continue and opens the next; Compare editions beside the edition opens Google's comparison (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/spanner/docs/editions-overview>, read 2026-11-19: Availability SLA, Configurations, Multi-model, Search, Resource management, Analytics, Data protection, CUDs) with a Locally row; Create backup's Expiration date is a date picker (a week out; at the current time of day, UTC) and Create backup from offers Current time or an Earlier point in time (its Version time); the Data tab writes ARRAY columns (a JSON array) and PostgreSQL databases' tables (their information_schema, "quoted" names and $n parameters), each value bound as a parameter of its column's type; Spanner Studio's Run is PROFILE mode (WITH_STATS since #2017) with the Explanation tab's Query summary (Rows returned, Elapsed time).
- [x] Editions and autoscaling (#1949): Create instance's Select an edition offers Standard, Enterprise and Enterprise Plus, Allocate compute capacity's Autoscaling its Minimum, Maximum, High priority CPU utilization target (65%) and Storage utilization target (95%), and Backups' Enable default backup schedules is checked, as Google's console has them (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/spanner/docs/create-manage-instances>, read 2026-11-19); Compare editions' Locally row says what is served; the instance's page shows its Autoscaling and Default backup schedules; Edit instance changes the edition, the scaling mode and its limits and targets, and default backup schedules; Create backup schedule has Backup type (Full backup, Incremental backup).
- [x] Round 3 (#1859, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/spanner/docs/create-manage-instances>, read 2026-11-20): the Instances list's rows have checkboxes, and checking one opens the Info panel on it ("Select the checkbox for the instance. The Info panel appears") with Details and **Labels** tabs; the Labels tab lists the instance's labels and adds, updates and deletes them in place, saved with Save (UpdateInstance's labels). The instance's Overview has **Upgrade** next to the edition ("Click Upgrade next to the edition type"), which opens **Update edition** with the higher editions only, ending in Save; Enterprise Plus has none, and a downgrade stays Edit instance's. Neither is a button beside the title (`TestConsoleSpannerInfoPanelAndUpgrade`, `TestSpannerInfoPanelLabelsAndUpgradeThroughThePage`, with the WCAG audit of the panel and the dialog).
- [x] Round 4 (#2017, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/spanner/docs/reference/standard-sql/protocol-buffers>): PROTO and ENUM columns on the Data tab. The console reads the database's proto bundle (GetDatabaseDdl's proto_descriptors), shows a PROTO value as the message's JSON and an ENUM by its value name (an ARRAY of them as a JSON array), and Insert row and Edit row write them as typed parameters (PROTO or ENUM with proto_type_fqn): a PROTO in a textarea, as the message's JSON, parsed against its descriptor; an ENUM from a select of its value names (and NULL when nullable). A column whose type is not in the bundle stays read-only. Spanner has no STRUCT columns to edit (STRUCT is a query type only). (`TestSpannerProtoColumnTypes`, `TestSpannerProtoValuesRoundTrip`, `TestSpannerProtoRowFields`; compat `TestSpannerProtoBundleColumns`; in headless Chrome `TestSpannerProtoColumnsThroughTheDataTab`, with the WCAG audit of the Data tab and Insert row.)
- [ ] Not yet: query plans are not drawn, and cannot be locally (no local optimizer makes Spanner's plans; the front refuses PLAN and PROFILE, see Spanner Studio above). Investigated in #2029 against the emulator's source (cloud-spanner-emulator d357ec1d, Apache-2.0): a query runs on GoogleSQL's reference evaluator (`backend/query/query_engine.cc` `EvaluateQuery`, `googlesql::PreparedQuery`), every table is read whole from its base table (`backend/query/queryable_table.cc`, `KeySet::All`, no seek), FORCE_INDEX is only validated and JOIN_METHOD is validated and dropped, so the executed structure has no Distributed Union, seek condition, Index Scan, Hash Join or Apply Join, and the evaluator's only view of it, `ExplainAfterPrepare`, is a debug string whose format "can change at any time". Google's plans depend on the optimizer version, its statistics package and the data's splits (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/spanner/docs/query-execution-plans>, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/spanner/docs/query-optimizer/overview>, read 2026-11-20). No query shape can get a plan that is both the executed one and Spanner's, so every shape (scans, filters, joins, index reads) stays refused (`TestSpannerQueryPlansRefusedForEveryShape`). The column order of the database's Indexes, Views, Change streams and Sequences lists, and the menu's order past Google's documented entries, are as remembered: UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-20. What Google's pages do say: the Indexes tab "shows a list of indexes" and an index's name opens "details about an index, such as the columns that it includes" (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/spanner/docs/secondary-indexes>); the Change streams page "displays a list of all of that database's change streams, and summarizes the configuration of each one", and a stream's name opens "the tables and columns that it watches" (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/spanner/docs/change-streams/manage>); neither names the list's columns. Google's views and sequences pages (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/spanner/docs/create-manage-views>, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/spanner/docs/primary-key-default-value>) describe no console list at all, only DDL and INFORMATION_SCHEMA.
