The Bigtable front's `default` application profile of a one-cluster instance now allows single-row transactions, as <https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigtable/docs/app-profiles> says it does (read 2026-11-19).

- [x] Instances list: Instance ID, Instance name, Edition, Storage type, Clusters, Nodes, Labels; Create instance on the title line. The drawer's Bigtable row opens it.
- [x] Create instance, Google's steps with Continue (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigtable/docs/creating-instance>, read 2026-11-19): Name your instance (Instance name, the Instance ID made from it), Select your edition (Enterprise, Enterprise Plus: stored, the emulator serves both alike), Select your storage type (SSD, HDD), Configure your first cluster (Cluster ID, Region, Zone, Enable 2x node scaling, Enable in-memory drawn disabled, Node scaling mode: Manual node allocation with the number of nodes, or Autoscaling with Minimum, Maximum, CPU utilization target and Storage utilization target), then Encryption (Cloud KMS key disabled: nothing is encrypted with one), Labels and the instance type; Create.
- [x] Instance page with Google's left-hand menu: Overview (the instance and each cluster), Tables, Bigtable Studio, Clusters, Application profiles, Backups, Authorized views, Logical views, Materialized views, Monitoring, Key Visualizer and System insights (each "Not available locally": Cloud Monitoring metrics and key heatmaps the emulator does not record), then CloudBurrow's Snapshots and Schema bundles; Edit instance (Instance name, Edition, type, labels, through PartialUpdateInstance) and Delete instance beside the title.
- [x] Tables: Table ID, Clusters, Column families, Change stream, Deletion protection, Current backups; Create table and Undelete table at the top; each row's Edit (Google's Edit table: the column families, each added, deleted or with its policy changed, the change stream, Prevent deletion / Enable deletion) and Delete. Create table (here and on the Tables page of the default instance): Table ID, Column families (Add a column family: Column family ID, Type Standard, Sum, Min, Max or HyperLogLog (the front computes the last three, #1893), Maximum versions, Maximum age, both), Enable change stream with its retention (1 to 7 days), Deletion protection, Automated backup (disabled: no automated backups are taken). Each table still opens its own page with rows, families and every table action.
- [x] Bigtable Studio (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigtable/docs/manage-data-using-console>): the Explorer (tables with their column families and authorized views, logical and materialized views; a table's Query, Open in query editor and Open table page), the Query builder (Table, Row key filter: All rows, Row key prefix, Row key range or Row keys; Column family, Column qualifier, Latest cell versions only, Limit; ReadRows), the Query editor (GoogleSQL for Bigtable through PrepareQuery and ExecuteQuery, which the Bigtable front serves), the results with a Row details pane (every cell version with its timestamp) and Export (CSV, JSON, Copy to clipboard; Google Sheets disabled).
- [x] Clusters: Add cluster at the top (Cluster ID, Region, Zone, 2x node scaling, node scaling mode); each row's Edit cluster (the node scaling mode and its numbers), Edit memory layer and Delete cluster.

- [x] Row key schemas (#1900): Create table's Define row key schema reveals the Encoding (DelimitedBytes, OrderedCodeBytes, Singleton), the Delimiter (# by default) and a row per segment with its Segment name, Type (STRING, BYTES, INT64, TIMESTAMP) and Byte encoding, as Google's Create row key schema does; Edit table creates a schema on a table without one and deletes it (Delete row key schema, with ignore_warnings) from one with one, naming its segments; Bigtable Studio's Query editor reads the key by its segments (`TestBigtableRowKeySchema1900`).
- [x] Round 3 (#1858): Bigtable Studio's saved queries (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigtable/docs/create-manage-saved-queries>, read 2026-11-20): the Query editor's Save opens Save query (a name; Show advanced options reveals the Region, which cannot change once saved), Save on an open saved query saves a new version of it, and Save as offers Save as query, Save as logical view and Save as materialized view (the last two make the view through CreateLogicalView and CreateMaterializedView); the Explorer's Queries section lists the project's saved queries, each opened in a query tab of its own, with View actions Open query, Manage queries (disabled: the project's Saved queries page spans every product's, which is not served) and Delete query (Google's Delete query dialog, confirmed with Delete). Google keeps them as the project's Studio assets, outliving the instance; CloudBurrow keeps its own record in the instance's state directory (`bigtable-saved-queries.json`), says so in the dialog and the tab, and serves it through no API. The Tables page (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigtable/docs/managing-tables>, read 2026-11-20: "the number of clusters that the table is stored on", "the table's status, storage utilization, and the identifiers for any current backups") gains Status, from GetTable's REPLICATION_VIEW cluster states, which the Bigtable front now reports (READY in every cluster of the instance, Google-managed encryption in ENCRYPTION_VIEW and FULL), and Storage utilization, "—" with the reason (Google's is Cloud Monitoring's disk metrics; the emulator keeps tables in memory and reports no size). `TestBigtableStudioSavedQueries`, `TestBigtableTableStatus`, `TestTableClusterStates`, `TestBigtableStudioSavedQueries1858` (with the WCAG audit of the Tables page, the Studio, Save query and Delete query).
- [x] Round 4 (#2027): the Tables list's arrow beside a table ID lists its replications ("Click the arrow next to the table ID to expand a list of replications of the table", <https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigtable/docs/managing-tables>, read 2026-11-20), one row per cluster with its replication state from GetTable's REPLICATION_VIEW; the shared listing gains expandable child rows (`console.Resource.Children`, `Listing.ChildNoun`; a 24px arrow button with `aria-expanded`, its label "Show replications of orders"). Storage utilization is the table's size, SampleRowKeys's last `offset_bytes` (bigtable.proto: "approximate total storage space used by all rows in the table which precede row_key"), not Google's compressed, replicated disk figure. A table's ID opens it in Bigtable Studio (`?tab=studio&table=`), in a builder tab on it, as Google reaches a table there (#1858 item 8); the table's own page (Rows, Schema, Change stream, Snapshots, Authorized views, Schema bundles) is kept as a CloudBurrow extra, reached by the row's Open table page and the Explorer's. The Explorer's View actions for a table are Google's, in its order (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigtable/docs/manage-data-using-console>, read 2026-11-20): Create a new table, Create a backup (the table chosen), Prevent deletion (`preventdeletion`, UpdateTable's deletion_protection), Edit, Delete, View a sample query (a GoogleSQL editor tab), View backups (the instance's Backups page), and Monitor by viewing system insights and Export to Cloud Storage drawn disabled with the reason (Cloud Monitoring; the Dataflow template); CloudBurrow's Query, Open in query editor and Open table page stay. Saved queries stay CloudBurrow's own record: Google's page says "The saved queries capability is available only in the console, and not using APIs" (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/bigtable/docs/create-manage-saved-queries>, read 2026-11-20); its IAM permissions are `databasesconsole.studioQueries.*` (roles `roles/databasesconsole.studioQueryUser`, `studioQueryAdmin`) and no public REST reference documents a `databasesconsole` resource, nor a Dataform or Dataplex asset for them, so there is no API to serve. `TestBigtableTablesReplicationsAndExplorerActions`, `TestBigtableTableClusterStates` (compat), `TestBigtableTablesAndExplorerActions2027` (with the WCAG audit of the Tables page with replications open, the Explorer's table actions and Prevent deletion), `TestBigtableInstancePickerThroughTheBrowser` updated.

A Google lab has Create backup in the Tables row's menu, which the docs do not list (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-20: managing-tables); here it is on the Tables page's title line and in the Explorer.

#### Secret Manager (#1850)

Shared additions: `Field.Upload` (on a textarea, a file chooser whose text file is read into it: Create secret's and Add new version's Secret value, "Upload file"); a detail page opened with `?reveal=1` shows the value as its View secret value button does, recorded the same way, and drops the flag from the address (a version row's View secret value); the Revealer's button is "View secret value". Backend: `Secret.version_aliases` is served (UpdateSecret, GetSecret, and GetSecretVersion and AccessSecretVersion by alias; docs/compatibility.md). CI: `TestSecretManager*` browser tests run in the run shard, whose instance serves Cloud KMS beside Secret Manager.

- [x] Menu: Secret Manager and Settings. The Secret Manager page has two tabs (`pageTabs`), Global secrets (`/secrets`) and **Regional secrets** (`/secrets-regional`, the regional secrets of #1519 and Create regional secret), as Google's docs say: "go to the Secret Manager page ... click the Regional secrets tab" (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/regional-secrets/view-regional-secret-details>, read 2026-11-20). The first tab's name is not in the docs (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-20: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/regional-secrets/view-regional-secret-details>). Settings says what it holds locally: Google's docs describe no Secret Manager setting of a project (UNVERIFIED, docs re-read 2026-11-19: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/docs/view-secret-details>).
- [x] Secrets list: Name, Location (Automatically replicated, the replica locations, or a regional secret's region), Encryption (Google-managed or Customer-managed), Labels, Created, Expiration, and the Actions menu: Add new version (Secret value typed or from Upload file, Disable all past versions; <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/docs/add-secret-version>), Edit (Edit secret) and Delete. The filter is Secret Manager's filter syntax (#1228).
- [x] Create secret, one page as Google's (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/docs/creating-and-accessing-secrets>): Name, Secret value (or Upload file), then Replication policy (Automatically manage locations for this secret, or Manually manage locations with the locations), Encryption (Google-managed encryption key, or Customer-managed encryption key revealing the keys, #1453), Rotation (Set rotation period revealing the Rotation period list, whose Custom reveals its own period, and Starting on; <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/docs/secret-rotation>), Notifications (Pub/Sub topics), Expiration (Set expiration date revealing the time or a TTL), Labels, Annotations, Version aliases (alias=version; <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/docs/assign-alias-to-secret-version>) and Delayed destruction (Set duration for delayed destruction). Create regional secret is the same page with a Location and no replication.
- [x] Secret details: Overview (general details, replication, encryption, expiration, notifications, rotation, delayed destruction, version aliases, labels, annotations; <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/docs/view-secret-details>), Versions (State, Aliases, Created, Scheduled for destruction, Destroyed; each row's Actions menu has View secret value, Disable, Enable and Destroy as its state allows; a row opens the version's page), Permissions, and Logs. Beside the title: Edit secret (the create page's sections after Encryption), Add new version, Change encryption keys, Advance clock (CloudBurrow extension) and Delete.
- [x] Round 3 (#1850). Shared additions: `Action.Reveal` (a row action that shows the value through the Revealer in a dialog ending in Done), `Action.ConfirmWord` (what a name confirmation asks for when it is not the path's last segment), `EditForm.Layout` `page` (an edit form as a page of its own at the resource's address with `?edit=1`, its submit and Cancel going back), and `console.DateTimeFieldType` (a `datetime-local` picker: its RFC 3339 default shown in the browser's time zone, the value sent as the UTC instant). The lists poll with the focus-preserving refresh of #2047. Backend: `Secret.secret_type` is served (CreateSecret keeps it, GetSecret returns it, UpdateSecret refuses it as immutable; docs/compatibility.md).
  - Create secret: Name, **Secret type** (Preview: None, Other DB credentials, Access key, Certificate, Other; Create regional secret adds Cloud SQL DB credentials first), Secret value or Upload file, as <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/docs/creating-and-accessing-secrets> lists them; the Overview names the type. Create regional secret's **Location** is Region (a region typed) or Multi-region (us, eu, ca or in; <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/regional-secrets/create-regional-secret>, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/docs/locations>, read 2026-11-20).

A version row's Actions menu has the same, confirmed the same way, and **View secret value**, which shows the value in a dialog ending in **Done** on the secret's page (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/docs/access-secret-version>). `TestConsoleSecretParityRound3`; in headless Chrome `TestSecretManagerConsoleRound3` and `TestSecretManagerRound3ScreensMeetWCAG`.

  - Expiration (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/docs/creating-and-managing-expiring-secrets>): Set expiration date with a date and time only; the TTL is gcloud's and REST's, so it is no longer on the page.

  - Checking a secret opens the **info panel**, with **Permissions** and **Labels** (Save) (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/docs/manage-access-to-secrets>, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/docs/add-labels-to-secrets>).

#### Cloud KMS (#1851)

Shared additions: `Action.Page`, `Field.OptionsBy`/`Field.OptionsFor` and `Listing.HiddenColumns` (#1851, the components table above); `Resource.Selectable` (a resource page's row that can be checked for its listing's `SelectActions` without being an object: a key's versions); `console.CreateThener` (`CreateThen()`, the create capability's `then`): after Create the page opens the made resource's page with that action (Create key ring goes on to Create key); a detail page opens the action its address names once (`?action=<id>`, then dropped from the address); a resource page's header leaves out the actions a tab's table offers on its checked rows.

- [x] Menu, Google's order: Key management (the key rings), Autokey, EKM connections, KMS Controls, Key inventory, then CloudBurrow's Single-tenant HSM (Google's docs give its instances gcloud steps only, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/kms/docs/create-manage-single-tenant-hsm>). KMS Controls holds the default Key Access Justifications policies (#2053, below).
- [x] Key inventory (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/kms/docs/view-keys-by-project>): every key of the project across its key rings, with Key name, Status (Available / Not available, symmetric keys only), Key ring, Location, Current rotation, Rotation frequency, Next rotation, Protection level, EKM via VPC connection, Purpose and Labels; each row opens the key. Key usage summaries (Cloud KMS Inventory API) are not served.
- [x] Create key ring (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/kms/docs/create-key-ring>): a page of Key ring name and Key ring location ending in Create, which goes on to the new ring's Create key (the docs do not say what follows Create; going on to the key is the audit's account, UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-19: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/kms/docs/create-key-ring>).
- [x] Create key (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/kms/docs/create-key>), a page of its own at the ring's address (`?action=createkey`) ending in Create and Cancel: Key name; Protection level (Software, HSM, Single-tenant HSM with its Single-tenant HSM instance, External, External via VPC with its EKM connection); Key material (Generated key, Imported key, and CloudBurrow's External key, no first version); Purpose and Algorithm in Google's words ("Symmetric encrypt/decrypt", "3072 bit RSA - OAEP Padding - SHA256 Digest"), the enum kept as each option's value, the Algorithm listing the chosen purpose's algorithms only (Google symmetric encryption for Symmetric encrypt/decrypt); Rotation period (Never (manual rotation), 30 to 365 days, 90 by default) and Starting on (one period from now); Additional settings: Labels and Duration of 'scheduled for destruction' state (30 days). A non-symmetric key gets no rotation, which Cloud KMS refuses for it.
- [x] Key details: Overview (the key's details), Versions, Usage tracking, Permissions and Logs tabs. Rotate (confirmed with Rotate alone; a symmetric key's new version is made primary) and Edit Rotation Period (Rotation period with Never (manual rotation), Starting on) beside the title, from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/kms/docs/rotate-key>; Disable, Enable, Destroy and Restore act on the checked versions (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/kms/docs/enable-disable>, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/kms/docs/destroy-restore>), Disable, Enable and Restore confirmed with their own button alone, Destroy asking for the key's name and ending in Schedule Destruction. Each version row's menu has Make primary version (confirmed with Make primary), Get public key, and its Disable / Enable / Destroy / Restore; Add version, Edit key, Encrypt, Decrypt, Import version and the version's own page are kept.
- [x] Import jobs: the key ring's Import jobs tab and Create import job, and Import version on a key, as before (#1219).
- [x] Create connection (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/kms/docs/create-ekm-connection>): Connection name, Region, Service resource ID (self_link), Hostname, Certificates (one or more PEM, or one base64 DER), EKM management mode (Manual / Cloud KMS, the Crypto space path shown for Cloud KMS only) and Set connection as the default.
- [x] Autokey's Configure (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/kms/docs/enable-autokey>), ending in Submit: Configuration source (Configure for project, or for folder on a folder's row, or Inherit, which clears the configuration so the parent folder's applies; Inherit is chosen while nothing is configured), then Configuration in Google's words (Enable dedicated-project key storage, Enable same-project key storage, Disable autokey for project / folder), the Key project shown for dedicated storage, and disabling confirmed.
- [x] Key inventory's EKM via VPC connection is off in Column display options until turned on, as Google's (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/kms/docs/view-keys-by-project>).
- [x] Key usage tracking (#2053, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/kms/docs/view-key-usage>): a key's Usage tracking tab (Key inventory opens the key) shows the key usage summary (Protected resources, Projects, Google Cloud products) over the protected resources with Name, Project, Crypto key version, Cloud product, Resource type, Location, Creation date and Labels, with the table's Filter box, from the Cloud KMS Inventory API this instance serves (`GetProtectedResourcesSummary`, `SearchProtectedResources`), fed by the products' real CMEK resources; a key whose project is in no organization says "Data is limited to the selected project", as Google's notice does. Shared addition: a section listing draws its `Summary` cards over its rows.
- [x] KMS Controls (#2053, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/assured-workloads/key-access-justifications/docs/set-default-policy>): the menu's KMS Controls lists the project's, its folders' and its organization's Key Access Justifications (KAJ) configurations; a configuration's page shows its default policy (and a project's effective one), and Edit, Set Key Access Justifications policy (No default policy / Allow specific reason codes), the Justification reasons, Create, writes it through Cloud KMS's own `kajPolicyConfig` JSON method (#1577). Google's Deny all reason codes is left out: the API reads a set but empty policy as no default policy.
- [x] Disable autokey (#2053, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/kms/docs/enable-autokey>) asks Confirm in a "Disable autokey?" prompt after Submit, with nothing typed; the form's checkbox is gone. Shared addition: `Field.ConfirmValue` and `Field.ConfirmTitle`, a radio choice that prompts before the action is sent.
- [x] A version row's Destroy (#2053) asks for the key's name, as the header's Destroy over the checked versions does (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/kms/docs/destroy-restore>), not the version number. Shared addition: `Action.ConfirmWord`.
- [x] Starting on (#2053), in Create key and Edit Rotation Period, is a date picker (midnight UTC; an unchanged date keeps the time of day of the current next rotation; an RFC 3339 time is still taken).

#### Backup and DR (#1844)

Shared additions: a summary-only list screen (`Listing.SummaryOnly`) draws its own actions beside Refresh, as the Overview has its trial and protected resources actions; summary cards fit a 320px window (`minmax(min(320px, 100%), 1fr)`). The pages use `ProjectCreateFormer` (Schedule backup's Resources and Backup plan, Create Backup plan's Backup vault), a `group` field (Backup rules, Add rule), `UnavailableOptions` and `Field.Unavailable` for Google's choices this instance cannot make, `Action.Steps` (Restore's two steps) and a backup row's own actions (`Resource.Target`). Round 2 (#1844) uses the shared pieces from #1855 and #1857: `EditForm.Page` (Edit backup plan) and a row's `Action.Unavailable` (a backup's Delete). Shared addition: a `console.Filterer`'s pickers now travel with a screen scoped by the project alone (`carriesScope` in console.js), as Vaulted backups' Resource type and the Jobs page's time range are; before, they were carried only beside a `Scoper`'s scope. CI: the `TestBackupDR*` browser tests run in the sql shard, whose instance serves Cloud SQL beside Backup and DR.

- [x] Menu: Overview, Vaulted backups, Backup plans, Backup plan associations, Backup vaults, Jobs and Reports. Google's docs name each page ("Go to Vaulted backups", "Go to Backup plans", "Go to Backup vaults", "Go to Jobs"), but they do not give the menu's order (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-19: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/backup-disaster-recovery/docs/quickstarts/csql-quickstart>). Reports is "Not available locally": it is Looker Studio over a BigQuery log sink of the job logs; the logs are written (#1924), but no Looker Studio runs locally. The drawer's Backup and DR row opens the Overview. `/backupdr` is still the Backup vaults page, at its old address.
- [x] Overview: protected resources, vaulted backups, plans, associations and vaults, each linking to its page; the last 24 hours' jobs (Succeeded, Failed, Running); and the page's own actions, which were the vaults page's before (#1536). Google's Overview is not in its docs (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-19: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/backup-disaster-recovery/docs/concepts/backup-dr>).
- [x] Vaulted backups (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/backup-disaster-recovery/docs/cloud-console/sql/csql-backup>, read 2026-11-19) lists each data source as its resource: Resource name, Resource type, Location, Backup plan, Backup vault, Backups, Last backup and Last successful backup. **Schedule backup** has two steps:
  1. Choose resources: Resource Type (Cloud SQL; Compute Engine, Disk, AlloyDB and Filestore drawn disabled with their reason), Region, Resources (the project's instances).
  2. Apply backup plan: Backup plan, a plan in the instance's region.

  It ends in **Schedule**. Google asks for no association ID, so the page gives one. **Filter table** > **Resource type** narrows the list ("At the top of the table, select Filter table > Resource type. Select Cloud SQL"); the types this instance does not back up list nothing, with the reason. Each row's menu has **Create on-demand backup**, **Change backup plan** and **Remove backup plan** ("Either from the menu, or from the details page for the resource", csql-backup, read 2026-11-20). The resource's page lists its backups (Created, Backup type, Backup rule, Expires, Enforced retention ends). Beside the title are **Create on-demand backup** (choose a rule, then **Create**), **Change backup plan** ("Select a backup plan", **Apply**), **Remove backup plan** (confirmed; the backups stay), **Restore** and **Point-in-time restore**. Create on-demand backup's dialog is Google's "Create an on-demand backup", ending in **Create**. Each backup row has **Restore** and **Delete**; Delete is confirmed by **Delete** alone, and while the backup is inside its enforced retention it is drawn disabled with the date it ends, as "you can delete a backup only after it has exceeded its minimum enforced retention period" (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/backup-disaster-recovery/docs/cloud-console/bv-backup-manage>, read 2026-11-20); the service refuses such a delete with FAILED_PRECONDITION too.
- [x] Restore (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/backup-disaster-recovery/docs/quickstarts/csql-quickstart>): the backup, then Choose restore destination, ending in **Restore**:
  - **Overwrite the source instance**;
  - **Overwrite an existing instance** (an instance of the same engine);
  - **Restore to a new instance**, with its Instance ID: `instances.restoreBackup` on the new name creates the instance with the backup's settings and loads the backup (#1922).
- [x] Create Backup plan (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/backup-disaster-recovery/docs/cloud-console/backup-plan-create>) is one page of steps, ending in **Create**:
  - Resource Type;
  - Identity: Name, Description;
  - Location: Region;
  - Destination: Backup vault, a vault in the plan's region;
  - Backup rules, with **Add rule**. Each rule has a Rule name, Recurrence (Hourly, Daily, Weekly, Monthly, Yearly), Every 6 to 23 hours for an hourly rule, Days of the week for a weekly rule, Days of the month for a monthly or yearly rule, or instead its Week day of the month (gcloud's `week-day-of-month`, `FIRST-SUNDAY` to `LAST-SATURDAY`, #1844), Months for a yearly rule (gcloud's `MON WED`, `1 15`, `MAR JUN` forms), a Backup window (start and end, 24-hour clock, in the plan's time zone) and a Retention period in days; the plan's Time zone, an IANA name (#1923);
  - On-Demand Retention: Maximum custom on-demand retention in days; with it set the plan may have no rules (#1923). Create on-demand backup offers a rule's retention or a custom retention up to it;
  - Database Logs, which reveals the Log retention.

  The page checks Google's limits: hourly at least every 6 hours, a window that ends after it starts, rule names that are unique and in the API's form, and the vault in the plan's region. A plan's page shows Resource type, Description, Status, Location, Backup vault name, Backup storage location, Backup retention period, Log retention, Revision, Created and Last updated, with three tabs: Backup rules, Resources (the associations applying it) and Revisions. Beside the title are **Apply to a resource** (Schedule backup with the plan chosen), **Edit** and **Delete**. Edit opens Google's Edit backup plan as a page of its own (`?edit=1`), with the Backup plan description, the Backup rules to add, change or remove, and the Maximum custom on-demand retention, ending in **Save Changes** (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/backup-disaster-recovery/docs/cloud-console/backup-plan-edit>, read 2026-11-20); each save is a new revision.
- [x] Backup plan associations lists Resource, Resource type, Location, Backup plan, Backup vault, Last backup and Created. An association's page shows its rules' last backups, with **Create on-demand backup**, **Change backup plan** and **Remove backup plan**.
- [x] Create a backup vault (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/backup-disaster-recovery/docs/cloud-console/backup-vault-create>) is a page of Google's six steps, ending in **Create**:
  1. Name your backup vault: name, description and labels.
  2. Choose where to store your data: Region, or Multi-region (us, eu, asia) (#1921).
  3. Prevent backup deletion: the minimum enforced retention in days, 0 days to 99 years; "Prevent deletion for duration specified in backup rule" (`MATCH_BACKUP_EXPIRE_TIME`); "Lock the enforced retention" with its date (`effectiveTime`) (#1921).
  4. Encryption: Google-managed encryption key, or Customer-managed encryption key (CMEK) with its key.
  5. Define access to your backup vault: Restrict access to current organization (the default), current project, or No restrictions.

  A vault's page shows Created, Last updated, Description, Status, Location type (Region or Multi-region), Location, Stored bytes (the size of its backups' dumps), Backups, Enforced retention, Retention lock status (Unlocked, Lock takes effect on …, Locked), Retention inheritance, Encryption type and key, Access restriction, and the Service agent with **Copy service agent email**. Its actions are **Edit minimum enforced retention** (**Save**; it sets, moves or removes a pending lock, and once Locked only increases), **Edit vault** and **Delete**. Its tabs are Data sources (each opening its Vaulted backups page), Backup plans and Protected resources.
- [x] Jobs (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/backup-disaster-recovery/docs/monitor-reports/monitor-jobs-console>) has two tabs:
  - **Jobs**: Current failures (Error type, Source project name, Current failures, Error code, Error message, Resource ID), over each backup and restore job (scheduled, on-demand, restore and point-in-time restore), with Job ID, Job category, Job status, Resource name, Resource type, Resource location, Backup plan name, Backup rule, Backup vault name, Incremental backup size GiB, Start time, Duration, Error code and message, Restore project name, Restore resource name, Recovery Point Time and Backup name.
  - **System Jobs**: each backup plan and backup plan association job (create, update, delete), with Google's columns.

  The time range is Google's list over the table: Last 1 hour, Last 6 hours, Last 12 hours, Last 24 hours (the default), Last 7 days or Last 14 days ("You can change the time range to 1 hour, 6 hours, 12 hours, 7 days, or 14 days", re-read 2026-11-20), kept in the address as `?range=`; any other is refused with the ranges Google offers. The jobs are the service's job logs, which it also writes to Cloud Logging as `backupdr.googleapis.com/bdr_backup_restore_jobs` and `bdr_backup_plan_jobs` (#1924). Shared addition: a list tab's own `Section.Groups`, drawn over its rows (Current failures) or in place of the summary.
- [ ] Not yet:
  - Reports (#1924): Google's are Looker Studio reports over the job logs routed by a sink into a BigQuery dataset. The job logs are written and a sink to BigQuery routes them here, but no Looker Studio runs locally, so Reports stays "Not available locally";
  - workload types other than Cloud SQL and Compute Engine: AlloyDB and Filestore are not served. Compute Engine VMs and disks are backed up into a vault and restored to a new VM or disk (#2075); Google documents no in-place restore of a vaulted Compute Engine backup. The management server and its appliances are not needed: Google's console backs up Cloud SQL into a backup vault without one (docs/backupdr.md).

#### Artifact Registry (#1855)

Shared additions: a `group` field's row may hold a `select` cell (`Field.Group` with `Type: "select"`, its `Options` and `OptionLabels`): a cleanup policy's Policy type and Tag state. `EditForm.Page` draws a resource's edit form at its page's address with `?edit=1` instead of a dialog, opened by the Edit button and a row's Edit item, Cancel and a saved change going back to the page: Edit Repository (#1855). A listing section's `Groups` are drawn as cards over its rows on a resource page: the Vulnerabilities tab's Scan results (#1855). `Detail.Panels` and `Resource.Panel` draw side panels (Setup Instructions, Vulnerability details), `Section.ShowLabel` names a Show list (the Layer bar), `PropertyGroup.Bars` draws counts as a bar graph (Most common licenses) and `Field.Browse` `"artifact-registry"` picks a repository (#2066). Backend (#2066): the scanner runs with `--licenses`, offline, recording each OS package's license from its package database as the PACKAGE occurrence's `license.expression`, and each finding's layer (index, diff ID, build command, base images) as its file location's `layer_details`. Backend: `docker_config.immutable_tags` is served and enforced by the registry store (a tag cannot be moved or deleted, nor a tagged version or image deleted: `FAILED_PRECONDITION`, `DENIED` on the Docker registry API), and `kms_key_name` is accepted in the repository's location and encrypts the repository's content through the instance's Cloud KMS (docs/compatibility.md).

What the local Artifact Registry serves: Docker, Maven, npm, Python, Apt, Yum, Generic, Go and Kubeflow Pipelines repositories; Standard mode for each; Remote mode for Docker, Maven, npm and Python over another repository of this instance (pull-through, cached); Virtual mode for Docker, Maven, npm and Python (reads by priority). Preset and custom remote upstreams (the instance makes no calls off the machine, by design), upstream authentication is not served; vulnerability scanning runs on the machine with OSV-Scanner, offline, when the project's Container Scanning API is enabled and `prefetch --vulnerability-scanning` has stored the scanner (#1912); cleanup policies run about daily (`--artifact-registry-cleanup-interval`), a dry run writing what it would delete to Cloud Logging; a customer-managed key encrypts the repository's content, which cannot be read or written while its key version is disabled or destroyed (#1897).

- [x] Menu: Repositories, Settings (`/artifact-registry-settings`: the project's gcr.io redirection and pull percent, each location's VPC-SC policy and platform logs, with Set project pull percent, Set VPC-SC policy and Set platform logs, which were a repository page's actions before). Google's menu beyond these is not in its docs (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-19: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/artifact-registry/docs/repositories/create-repos>).
- [x] Repositories list: Name, Format (Docker, npm, ...), Mode (Standard, Remote, Virtual), Description, Location, Labels, Encryption (Google-managed key or Customer-managed key), Cleanup policies (how many, dry run or delete artifacts), Size, Created, Updated, with the shared filter. The columns are the ticket's, the docs naming none (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-19: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/artifact-registry/docs/repositories/list-repos>).
- [x] Create Repository opens Create repository, one page as Google's (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/artifact-registry/docs/repositories/create-repos>, read 2026-11-19): Name, Format (Docker, Maven, npm, Python, Apt, Yum, Kubeflow Pipelines, Go, Generic; every one served since #1897), Mode (Standard, Remote, Virtual); for Maven its Version policy (None, Release, Snapshot) and Allow snapshot overwrites; for Remote the Remote repository source (Preset and Custom disabled: no egress; Artifact Registry repository with its upstream, which Browse picks from the project's standard repositories of the format in the location, #2066) and Remote repository authentication mode (Authenticated disabled); for Virtual the Virtual Repository Upstreams rows (Policy name, Repository, Priority; Add Upstream; <https://docs.cloud.google.com/artifact-registry/docs/repositories/virtual-repo>); Location type (Region, Multi-Region) with the Region or Multi-region list, regions named "us-central1 (Iowa)"; Description; Labels (key and value rows); Encryption (Google-managed encryption key, Customer-managed key with the key); Immutable image tags (Docker; Enabled, Disabled); Cleanup policies (Dry run or Delete artifacts, and a row per policy from Add a cleanup policy: Name, Policy type Conditional delete / Conditional keep / Keep most recent versions, Tag state Any tag state / Tagged / Untagged, Tag prefixes, Version prefixes, Package prefixes, Older than, Newer than, Keep count; <https://docs.cloud.google.com/artifact-registry/docs/repositories/cleanup-policy>); Artifact Analysis (Vulnerability scanning, Enabled or Disabled, Docker only, since #1912: Enabled is `enablement_config` `INHERITED`, scanning while the project's Container Scanning API is enabled, Disabled is `DISABLED`; <https://docs.cloud.google.com/artifact-analysis/docs/os-scanning-automatically>, re-read 2026-11-19); Create.
- [x] Repository details: Google's details in the header (Format, Mode with a remote's upstream or a virtual's upstreams, Location, Description, Labels, Encryption with its key, Immutable image tags, Version policy, Cleanup policies, Repository size, Created, Updated, URL); tabs Images (Name, Created, Updated, Versions, Tags) or Packages (Name, Created, Updated, Versions) with a Maven, npm or Python repository's format listing, Rules, Attachments, Permissions. Beside the title: Setup Instructions, which open Google's side panel (#2066), Setup instructions with Copy (docker tag / push / pull to `{location}-docker.pkg.localhost:{run port}`, `.pypirc` and `pip.conf`, `.npmrc`, a `pom.xml` distributionManagement and repositories, an apt source line, a yum `.repo`, `gcloud artifacts generic upload` and `download`, each noting that this instance asks for no credential where Google's console sets up a credential helper first); Edit Repository, Edit rule, Import from Cloud Storage (Apt, Yum), Add rule, Delete rule, Delete attachment and Delete.

An image version's row has More actions > Edit tags (the tags typed, Save; a tag dropped is deleted, one of another version moves here), and Delete takes the checked versions, confirmed by its button alone (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/artifact-registry/docs/docker/manage-images>, read 2026-11-20).

Vulnerabilities (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/artifact-analysis/docs/os-scanning-automatically>, read 2026-11-20): the Scan results summary (package types scanned, total, fixable, unfixable, and each effective severity) over the table of CVE name, Effective severity, CVSS, Fix available, Package, Package type, Installed version and Fixed version; each row's View (no fix) or View Fixed (a fix available) opens the Vulnerability details side panel: the vulnerability's details and its File location(s) table, each location with its layer's index, build command, digest and base image (Google's question-mark dialog; #2066). The Layer bar (Preview, #2066), only when the scan named layers: Layer, "All layers" then each layer with vulnerabilities, bottom first, as "Layer N: build command (count vulnerabilities)", choosing one showing its rows. Dependencies: the SBOM section, Most common licenses as a bar graph of the five licenses most packages carry (#2066), and the list of Package name, Package version, Package type and License type (the license the scanner read from the package database), from the PACKAGE occurrences scanning wrote; or why there are none (scanning not active, or the image pushed before it was).
- [x] Done in #2066 (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/artifact-analysis/docs/os-scanning-automatically> and <https://docs.cloud.google.com/artifact-analysis/docs/view-licenses-dependencies>, read 2026-11-20): Setup Instructions is a side panel; Vulnerability details (View, View Fixed) is a side panel with the File location(s) table; the Layer bar; Most common licenses' bar graph and each package's License type; Browse for a remote's upstream; a version's Created is its build time. A language package's license (Go, npm, Maven, PyPI) is read from the image's own package metadata and LICENSE files (#2072), so its License type and Most common licenses show its SPDX ID (`TestCloudRunArtifactRegistryLanguageLicensesThroughThePage`, with the WCAG 2.1 AA audit).

#### APIs & Services (#1866)

Shared additions: `metrics.Registry.Measured` (whether a service's calls are counted, so an uncounted API says "Not counted" rather than showing zero), `serviceusage.Server.Unserved` (the APIs CloudBurrow serves with other `--services`, with the reason), and `monitoring.RequestBuiltin.Deltas` and `Began`: `up` now builds the request history (one-minute deltas of the counters per project, kept 30 days) whether or not Cloud Monitoring is served, samples it every minute, and hands the same history to Cloud Monitoring's `serviceruntime.googleapis.com/api/*` and to these pages (cmd/cloudburrow/consoleapicharts.go). Read from Google's public docs on 2026-11-20: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/apis/docs/monitoring>. The pages are a `console.Studio` screen of the `serviceusage` provider (`POST /api/studio/serviceusage?call=apis|api|enable|disable`, cmd/cloudburrow/consoleapis.go).

- [x] Menu, in Google's order: Enabled APIs & services, Library, Credentials, OAuth consent screen, Page usage agreements, Domain verification, then CloudBurrow's Operations (Service Usage `operations.list`, which has no Google page). The OAuth consent screen opens Google Auth Platform (#2030, below) and Domain verification the Site Verification API's sites (#2030); Page usage agreements (Google's terms acceptance for the console's pages) is in Google's place and says "Not available locally" and why.
- [x] Enabled APIs & services (`/apis`; Google's `/apis/dashboard` redirects to it): the time ranges (1 hour, 6 hours, 12 hours, 1 day, 2 days, 4 days, 7 days, 14 days, 30 days), the Traffic, Errors and Median latency charts, then the project's enabled APIs with Name, Requests, Errors, Latency, median and Latency, 95%, and Enable APIs and services on the title line. Counts and charts are the project's own calls over the range, from the instance's request history, which a banner says (a call that named no project counts in the default project; there is no history before the instance started); an API whose calls are not counted (Service Usage, Cloud SQL Admin, Backup and DR) reads "Not counted".
- [x] An API's page (`/apis/api/<name>/<tab>`): Overview (service name, status, category, and Traffic by response code), Metrics (the time ranges; Google's four default graphs, Traffic by response code, Errors by API method, Overall latency at the 50th, 95th and 99th percentile and Latency by API method (median); Select Graphs, which adds Traffic by API method or removes any; and its methods' Requests, Errors and Latency), Quotas & System Limits (the API's Cloud Quotas quota infos, with Edit quotas, #2030), Credentials; Disable API on the title line, confirmed in a dialog, or Enable on a disabled API.
- [x] Library (`/apis/library`): Search for APIs & Services and the categories (Big data, Compute, Databases, Developer tools, Management tools, Security, Storage) over every API CloudBurrow serves; an API's Library page (`/apis/library/<name>`) has Enable, or Manage once enabled. An API this instance was not started with is listed "Not available locally" with Enable disabled and the `--services` value that would serve it. APIs CloudBurrow never serves are left out: Google's Library would list them as enableable.
- [x] Credentials (`/apis/credentials`): Create credentials (API key, which shows the new key in Google's "API key created" dialog; OAuth client ID, which opens Google Auth Platform's Create client (#2030); Service account opens IAM & Admin's Create service account), Restore deleted credentials, then API Keys (#1915: Name, Creation date, Restrictions, Show key, Edit API key, Delete API key), OAuth 2.0 Client IDs (Name, Creation date, Type, Client ID, Delete; #2030) and the project's Service Accounts.
- [x] An API key's page (`/apis/credentials/key/<id>`, #1915): Name, Application restrictions (None, Websites, IP addresses, Android apps, iOS apps) and API restrictions (Don't restrict key, or Restrict key to some of the enabled APIs), Save; Show key, Rotate key (a new key with the same restrictions; the old one keeps working until **Delete the previous key** in the Previous key section) and Delete, confirmed. Restore deleted credentials (`/apis/credentials/restore`) restores a key deleted in the last 30 days.
- [x] Per-project counts, the time ranges and Select Graphs (this branch): `TestConsoleAPIsPerProjectHistory`, `TestAPIWindowSteps`; in headless Chrome, `TestAPIsMetricsPerProjectOverATimeRange`, with the WCAG checks over the dashboard, an API's Overview and Metrics and Select Graphs open.
- [x] Quotas & System Limits (#2030): IAM & Admin > Quotas & System Limits (`/iam-admin/quotas`) and each API's tab, over the Cloud Quotas API (`cloudquotas.googleapis.com` v1, served on the Resource Manager port): Type (Quota or System limit), Service, Name and metric, Dimensions, Value (with the default when a preference changed it), Adjustable, Enforced locally; Edit quotas (New value, Region, Request description; Submit request) is a `QuotaPreference` with `CLOUD_CONSOLE` origin, granted at once, and Google's safety checks refuse a decrease below usage or by more than 10%. `TestQuotasThroughThePage`. Read 2026-11-20: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/docs/quotas/view-manage>, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/docs/quotas/reference/rest>.
- [x] Domain verification (`/apis/credentials/domainverification`, #2030), over the Site Verification API (`siteVerification` v1, `www.googleapis.com/siteVerification/v1/`, served on the Resource Manager port): the verified sites (Domain, Type, Owners), Add domain (Site URL, Verification method FILE or META; the token to place, then Verify) and Remove, confirmed. DNS methods are refused: the instance looks nothing up off the machine. `TestDomainVerificationThroughThePage`. Read 2026-11-20: <https://developers.google.com/site-verification/v1>.
- [x] Google Auth Platform (`/apis/credentials/consent/<tab>`, #2030): Overview (the app and the instance's authorization, token and revocation endpoints), Branding (App name, User support email, home page, privacy policy and terms links, Authorized domains, Developer contact information), Audience (User type External or Internal, Publishing status Testing or In production, Test users, at most 100), Clients (Create client for Web application, Android, iOS, Chrome Extension, TVs and Limited Input devices and Desktop app; the client ID and secret shown once; each client's page with its secrets by their last four characters, Add secret (at most two), Disable, Enable and Delete of a disabled secret, Save and Delete; Deleted credentials with Restore for 30 days) and Data Access (scopes); Get started when no app is configured. Google Auth Platform has no public API: the IAP OAuth Admin API that made brands and clients was shut down on 2026-03-19 (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/iap/docs/deprecations>), so neither it nor `gcloud iap oauth-brands`/`oauth-clients` is served; the pages are a local store, and a Web or Desktop client signs the instance's user in at the instance's `/o/oauth2/v2/auth`, `/token` and `/revoke` (authorization code with PKCE, refresh tokens, ID tokens). Console-only on Google, so not here: brand verification and the Verification Center, Branding's logo upload, and the OAuth app's monitoring. `TestGoogleAuthPlatformThroughThePages`, `TestOAuthClientSignsInTheInstanceUser`. Read 2026-11-20: <https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/15549257>, <https://support.google.com/cloud/answer/15549049>.
- [x] WCAG: `TestAPIsConsole2030ScreensMeetWCAG` audits the pages above, Edit quotas and Add domain.
