#### Secure Source Manager (#1856)

Google's Secure Source Manager has two surfaces: the Cloud console's instances, and each instance's web interface (`INSTANCE_ID-PROJECT_NUMBER.LOCATION.sourcemanager.dev`), which holds the repositories. Here the web interface is the instance's page, whose Web interface URL is the instance's `host_config.html`. Backend (#1856): FetchTree and FetchBlob, issues and their comments, pull requests (Create, Get, List, Update, Open, Close, Merge as a merge commit, ListPullRequestFileDiffs) and branch rules (a rule's Require a pull request refuses a direct push to its branches) are served (docs/compatibility.md), and so are hooks, pull request comments and reviews, and pull request triggers (#1927). CI: `TestCloudRunSourceManager*` browser tests and the WCAG sweep run in the run shard.

- [x] Menu: Instances, the only page of the product's Cloud console menu (Google's docs describe no other, UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-19: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/create-instance>).
- [x] Instances list: Instance ID, State, Region, Instance URL (the web interface), Encryption (Google-managed key or Customer-managed key), Created. The columns are the ticket's, the docs naming none (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-19: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/create-instance>).
- [x] Create instance, a page of its own ending in Create (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/create-instance>, read 2026-11-19): Instance ID (lowercase letters, numbers and hyphens, starting with a letter), Region (a list of Google's eleven locations, "us-central1 (Iowa)", <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/locations>), Labels, Private instance (a Private Service Connect instance, revealing its optional CA pool, `projects/CA_PROJECT/locations/CA_LOCATION/caPools/CA_POOL_NAME`, "Required if you use your own CA certificate", <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/create-private-service-connect-instance>, read 2026-11-20; the docs give gcloud, API and Terraform steps only, so the label is the API's; served on the Run port as any other, #1856), Encryption (Google-managed encryption key, or Customer-managed encryption key (CMEK) revealing the Cloud KMS key with Browse, which must be in the instance's region). The docs give gcloud, API and Terraform steps only, so the page's labels are the API's.
- [x] Instance details: Name, Region, State, Web interface (with Copy URL), Git host, API host, Encryption, Cloud KMS key, Private instance, and a private instance's CA pool, HTTP service attachment and SSH service attachment (what describe answers), Labels, Created, Updated; tabs Repositories (the web interface's My repositories, Create repository at the top), Developer Connect connections, Permissions.
- [x] Create repository (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/create-repository>): the web interface's page of its own (`Action.Page`, #1856: the instance's page with `?action=create-repo`, its breadcrumb back to the instance, Cancel), Repository ID, Description, Default branch, Service account, Initialize repository ("Select this option if you want to initialize the repository and add .gitignore, license, and README files"), revealing .gitignore ("Choose which files to not track using the drop-down menu templates") and license ("Select a common license from the drop-down menu"), each a list of the template names InitialConfig's reference gives (230 and 459), ending in Create repository, which opens the new repository's page. Initialize repository is `initial_config` with `readme: default` and the chosen templates (#2071): the backend writes .gitignore, LICENSE and README.md as the repository's first commit, "Initial commit" by the caller, which git clones. Google publishes neither its templates' text nor its default README, so the texts are github/gitignore's (CC0-1.0) and the SPDX License List's (third_party/ssm-templates/PROVENANCE.md), and the README is `# {repository ID}` and the description: UNVERIFIED (docs re-read 2026-11-20: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/create-repository>). The web interface's .gitignore takes one template, as its drop-down does; the API takes several.
- [x] Repository page, the web interface's tabs then CloudBurrow's: Code (the default branch's files, folders first, each folder and file a page of its own at `tree/BRANCH/PATH`, a file's content shown; an empty repository says how to clone and push), Branches (each opens its code; the default marked), Issues (New issue: Title, Description, Create Issue; open issues first), Pull requests (New pull request: merge into, pull from, Title, Description, Create pull request; an empty repository has none, as Google's has no tab), Triggers, Git repository links, Builds, Permissions, Settings (General, Branch rules with Add branch rule and each rule's Edit and Delete, Webhooks). Source: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/work-with-issues-pull-requests>, read 2026-11-19.
- [x] The code view (#2071): the branch and tag selector over Code, a folder and a file (Branches and Tags, each opening the same path at that ref); History, the commits at the ref that touched the folder or file (100 newest; each opens the tree at it); a file's lines numbered and its syntax highlighted by its language, and Blame. History and blame are read from the repository with git, as the branches are: no API method serves them. Google's docs name the code view but not its layout (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-20: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/work-with-issues-pull-requests>).
- [x] The Issues and Pull requests tabs' Sort (Newest, Oldest, Recently updated, Least recently updated: Google's page names the menu but not its choices, UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-20), and both tabs' Labels, Milestones, Assignee and Type menus and Assigned to me ("You can filter or sort the issues by using the menus for Assignee, Type, or Sort", "click Assigned to me"); Type's choices are Created by you and Assigned to you, the page naming none (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-20: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/work-with-issues-pull-requests>), an issue's author being recorded from #2071 on; the choices are kept in the address (#2071).
- [x] An issue's page: its Conversation (the description) and Comments (Comment; each comment's Edit and Delete); Edit, Close issue / Reopen issue and Delete issue beside the title; Labels, Milestone and Assignees in its details, each with its gear (Edit labels, Edit milestone, Edit assignees: "On the Issue details page you can assign Labels, Milestones, Assignees", #2071). The repository's Labels tab (New label: Label name, Color, Description; Edit, Delete, each label's issue count) and Milestones tab (New milestone: Title, Due date, Description; Edit, Close / Reopen, Delete) define them. The pinned API (v1.12.0, and v1.13.1) holds none of these, so CloudBurrow keeps them in the instance's state file beside the repository's issues, for the console only; an issue read through the API carries none.
- [x] A pull request's page: Reviewers, Labels, Milestone, Assignees and Due date in its details, each with its gear ("On the Pull request details page you can assign Reviewers, Labels, Milestones, Assignees, and a Due date", #2071, kept in CloudBurrow's state as an issue's are; Reviewers name who is asked, the reviews themselves being the API's); Conversation and Files changed (each file's lines, old and new numbered, with the Whitespace, Split view/Unified view and diff options menus, #2071); Edit, Merge pull request (confirmed; the base branch gets a merge commit, refused on a conflict or by the branch's rule), Close pull request and Reopen pull request.
- [x] A pull request's review (#1927, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/work-with-issues-pull-requests>, read 2026-11-19): Conversation is the timeline after the description: reviews (Approved, Changes requested, Commented, with the commit reviewed), general comments, and code comments (file and line, Resolved or not) with each reply under its conversation's first comment; Comment adds a general comment; Review ("Leave a comment" and Approve, Comment or Request changes, with an optional line comment) sends one BatchCreatePullRequestComments, as Start review then Review does; a comment's Edit and Delete, a code comment's Reply and Resolve conversation / Unresolve conversation. Files changed shows each code comment under its line, with Add line comment (File, Line, negative for the old side, Line comment) and Review. The plus icon beside a line (#2071: "Click the plus icon next to the line you want to comment on. Click Start review", then "click Add comment") opens a comment form under it: Add single comment sends it at once (CreatePullRequestComment); Start review, then Add comment, keeps it Pending, shown under its line across page loads, until Review sends every pending comment with the review in one BatchCreatePullRequestComments; Discard pending comments drops them. Pending comments are kept in CloudBurrow's state per account, the API having no draft. Ignore whitespace changes folds a removed line and the added line beside it into one when they differ only in whitespace, in the browser, as ListPullRequestFileDiffs takes no whitespace option. Approve and Request changes on your own pull request are refused, as Google's note says.
- [x] Settings › Webhooks (#1927, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/set-up-webhooks>, read 2026-11-19): Hook ID, Target URL, Trigger on, Branch filter, Active; Add webhook (Hook ID, Target URL, Sensitive query string as a password field, Trigger on: Push, Pull request state changed, Pull request comment; Branch filter for push events, shown with Push; Active; Enable service account authentication, #2071: each delivery then carries `Authorization: Bearer` an ID token of the repository's service account, signed by the instance's key, whose audience is the target URL; refused for a repository without a service account, and with a sensitive query string; Google's REST field `serviceAccountAuth` is not in the pinned Hook, so it is kept in CloudBurrow's state, and the token's audience is CloudBurrow's choice, the page naming none, UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-20: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/set-up-webhooks>), each row's Edit, Test delivery and Delete; a webhook's page with its settings (the query string shown only as set), Recent deliveries (request and response) and Test delivery.
- [x] Add branch rule (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/configure-branch-protection>, read 2026-11-19): Branch rule ID, Branch filter (a branch name or `.*`), then Branch protection rule details: Require a pull request before merging, revealing Required number of reviewers, Required number of approvers, Block merge on stale reviews and approvals and Require conversation resolution before merging; Require linear history; Require status checks with the checks; ending in Submit.
- [x] Code owners (#1928, <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/codeowners>, read 2026-11-19): Add branch rule's Require Code Owner Review on Pull Requests (the Branch rules tab's Code owners column); a pull request's Code owners tab lists what the base branch's CODEOWNERS files require of its changed files, one row per section and owner set: Section, Owners, Approvals needed, Approved by (owners whose approval of the head commit counts), Files, each Satisfied, Pending or Optional, under a note naming the files read and whether a branch rule enforces them; the page's summary has Code owners ("1 of 2 satisfied; required by branch rule main"); Merge pull request is refused until they are satisfied. A CODEOWNERS file's page has a Checks tab: its status (valid, or how many lines have syntax errors) and each line's Error or Information (a rule for other branches), with the line's text and the details. Google's page draws these as highlighted lines with the details on hover, and does not document its pull request page's code owner display: the layout here is CloudBurrow's (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-19: <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secure-source-manager/docs/codeowners>).

#### Parameter Manager (#2143)

Round of 2026-11-22, the first for Parameter Manager, from Google's public how-to pages only (under <https://docs.cloud.google.com/secret-manager/parameter-manager/docs/>):

- [x] Create parameter (`create-parameter`): the name, then the **Parameter format** section (Unformatted, YAML, JSON) and the **Location** section (Multi-region, with global (Global) its Region, or Region with a region), Encryption, and Labels' **Add label** rows. Create template (`create-parameter-template`): the name, **Template format** (YAML or JSON), the same Location and Labels.
- [x] The list's Actions menu: **Add new version**, **Edit** and **Delete**, each opened on the resource's page; checking a parameter or template opens the info panel's **Labels**, ending in Save.
- [x] **Create parameter version** (`add-parameter-version`) and **Create template version** (`add-parameter-template-version`, from the template's **Create version**) are pages: the version name (63 characters or less, letters, numbers, - and _, not beginning with a dash), the payload with **Browse** to upload a file, ending in Create.
- [x] **Edit parameter** and **Edit parameter template** are pages ending in Save (`edit-parameter`, `edit-parameter-template`).
- [x] A version row's Actions menu (`view-parameter-version-details`): View payload, Render, Disable or Enable, Delete. Disable and Enable are confirmed with nothing typed; Delete asks for the parameter's (or template's) ID (`delete-parameter-version`, `delete-parameter-template-version`); a parameter's and a template's Delete ask for its name.
- [x] Deleting a template deletes its versions with it (`delete-parameter-templates`: "Deleting a template also deletes all of its template versions"); a parameter is still refused while it has versions (`delete-parameters`).
- [x] The payload's **Secret picker** (`add-parameter-version`): Ctrl+Space in the payload offers it, Enter opens it in a panel: **Secret** and **Secret version** (latest and the enabled versions), the **Select regional secret** switch, entering a secret version by resource ID, or creating a secret; **Insert** puts `__REF__("//secretmanager.googleapis.com/…")` at the cursor (`Field.SecretPicker`, a shared addition).
- [x] **Render template version** (`render-parameter-template-version`) is a page: the parameter, then the parameter version, ending in Render, which opens a new page with the payload and the rendered value.
- [x] Not documented for the console, so kept as they are: the parameters list's columns, the CMEK steps and tags (UNVERIFIED, re-read 2026-11-22).

### 4.8 Cross-product consistency (#1868)

A round across products rather than within one: every list, create, detail and
not-served page of an instance started with the storage, run, emulators,
memorystore and sql shards' services, walked in headless Chrome (each page's
title-line actions, filter bar, column chooser, table, pager, empty, error and
"Not available locally" states, and each create flow's page or dialog and its
buttons), compared product with product and with Google's documented
conventions. The ones fixed are fixed in the shared framework
(`internal/console/assets/console.js`), so every product follows.

| Pattern | Google's convention | What differed | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| Empty list | The table stays: filter bar, column headers, column chooser and pager, with the empty message in the table | An empty list was a bare card: no table, filter bar, View options, info-panel toggle or pager, so a product's toolbar changed shape with its row count (every list with no rows: Buckets, Topics, Queues, Services, Secrets, Key rings, Datasets, …); and it was never polled, so a resource made with an SDK did not appear until Refresh | Fixed: an empty list is drawn by `renderTableInto` like a full one, the empty state inside the table, and polled |
| Empty-state wording | Says what the page can do | The emptied table said "Create one here" on read-only lists (Domain mappings, Functions, Key inventory, Job history, Operations); the empty card did not | Fixed: `emptyListHint` words both from the screen's create capability |
| Service not started | A product whose API is not enabled says so and points at enabling it | A page of a service the instance was not started with (Memorystore, Backup and DR, …) was a red error card, "could not read this", with the internal `no such service` and a Retry that cannot succeed | Fixed: `renderNotStarted`, the "Not available locally" state, naming the product and `up --services`, linking to the API Library |
| "Not available locally" styling | One style for a page the console cannot serve | A provider's own "Not available locally: …" answer (GKE's Gateways, with no Gateway API on kind) was drawn as the red error card with Retry, while every other such page is the neutral "Not available locally" state | Fixed: such an answer is drawn as that state |
| Menu item not available | Google's label, disabled | Row menus draw Google's label disabled with the reason as tooltip; Credentials' Create credentials suffixed the label ("OAuth client ID — not available locally", "Service account — not served") | Fixed: `unavailableMenuItem`, the row menus' convention |
| Title-line actions | Create (with plus), Refresh, info-panel toggle, in that order | Consistent on every list with rows; on empty lists the toggle was missing (above) | Fixed with the empty list |
| Dialog buttons | Cancel, then the action, at the right | Consistent: every dialog draws Cancel before its action | — |
| Create page buttons | Create, then Cancel, at the bottom-left | Consistent: every create page draws its submit before Cancel | — |
| Delete confirmation | Per product: type `delete` (Pub/Sub topic), type the ID (Cloud SQL instance, project), or confirm only (Cloud Storage bucket) | Every delete, and the local AI pages' Download and Deploy, asked for the full name typed back | Fixed (#1931): each kind's confirmation is its row of `DeleteConfirmRules` (`internal/console/deleteconfirm.go`, below), sent as the capability's `deleteConfirm` and drawn by `confirmDestructive`; a destructive action names its own (`Action.ConfirmStyle`); Model Garden's Download and Deploy ask for nothing typed |
| Create flow: page or dialog | A page for a resource's create flow | Projects list's Create project was a dialog while New project and Manage resources' Create project are a page; Create log scope and Create folder were dialogs | Fixed (#1932): Create project opens the New project page (`CreateHrefer`); Create log scope is a page ending in Create log scope; Create folder is a page, one form without numbered sections (Folder name, Location) ending in Create, on the Resource hierarchy list and on Manage resources, which opens it with `?return=/cloud-resource-manager` and gets it back (Google's steps, resource-manager/docs/creating-managing-folders, re-read 2026-11-20: Folder name, the parent resource with Browse, then Create, the steps of Create project, whose form the docs call the New project page; the folder docs do not name the container, so the page follows Create project's). Secure Source Manager's Create instance is a page ending in Create (`CreateOnPage`, #1856; its docs have gcloud, API and Terraform steps only, re-read 2026-11-20). Left a dialog: Pub/Sub's Create snapshot (pubsub/docs/replay-overview, re-read 2026-11-20: Click Create snapshot, a subscription, the Snapshot ID, Create, the container not named), which is now the dialog fixture of `TestDialogTakesFocusClosesOnEscapeAndGivesItBack` and `TestCancelDiscardsWithoutAskingEscapeAsks`. Autokey's Create key handle has no console flow of Google's (a key handle is made from a resource's create page), and Labels' Add label is inline on Google's Labels page, then Save (`TestCreateFlowsArePagesWhereGooglesAre`) |
| Create labels | The product docs' wording | "Create Instance" (Cloud SQL) was title case; some create pages ended in Create, others repeated the button label | Fixed (#1933), each to its product's docs (below) |

#### Delete confirmations (#1931)

How Google's console confirms each kind's delete, from the console steps of
its docs (re-read 2026-11-19). A kind whose docs do not describe the dialog is **UNVERIFIED** (re-read 2026-11-19) and
keeps the name typed back, the strictest, until they do;
`TestEveryDeleterHasADeleteConfirmRule` fails on a provider that deletes and
has no row.

| Style | Kinds (provider) | Google's docs |
|---|---|---|
| Type `delete` | Pub/Sub topic (`pubsub`), BigQuery dataset (`bigquery`, and Delete dataset), BigQuery Delete table | pubsub/docs/delete-topic, bigquery/docs/managing-datasets, bigquery/docs/managing-tables |
| Type the ID | Project (`projects`, Shut down), folder (`hierarchy`), Cloud Tasks queue (`tasks`), secret (`secrets`, `secrets-regional`), Firestore database (`firestore-databases`), Bigtable table (`bigtable`), Cloud SQL database (`cloudsql`, `cloudsql-mysql`) and instance (`cloudsql-instances`), Memorystore for Redis instance (`memorystore`), Redis Cluster (`memorystore-clusters`), Memorystore for Valkey (`memorystore-valkey`) | resource-manager/docs/delete-restore-projects, resource-manager/docs/manage-folders, tasks/docs/manage-queues-and-tasks, secret-manager/docs/delete-secrets, firestore/native/docs/manage-databases, bigtable/docs/managing-tables, sql/docs/mysql/create-manage-databases, sql/docs/postgres/delete-instance, memorystore/docs/redis/create-manage-instances, memorystore/docs/cluster/delete-instances, memorystore/docs/valkey/delete-instances |
| Confirm only | Bigtable Studio saved query (`bigtable-instances`, Delete query), Cloud Storage bucket (`storage`), Pub/Sub schema (`pubsub-schemas`), Cloud Run job (`run-jobs`), Google group (`iam-groups`), label (`iam-labels`), log bucket (`logging-buckets`), Datastore entity (`datastore`, and Delete entity) | bigtable/docs/create-manage-saved-queries, storage/docs/deleting-buckets, pubsub/docs/delete-schema, run/docs/managing/jobs, iam/docs/groups-in-cloud-console, resource-manager/docs/creating-managing-labels, logging/docs/buckets, datastore/docs/store-query-data |
| UNVERIFIED (the ID typed) | Pub/Sub subscription and snapshot, Kubernetes workload (`workloads`, #1857), Cloud Run service, domain mapping, worker pool and instance, Cloud Run functions, Cloud Build trigger and private pool, Eventarc trigger, Cloud Scheduler job, Artifact Registry repository, Secure Source Manager instance, log sink, exclusion, log-based metric, saved query and log scope, service account, custom role, tag key, Bigtable instance, BigQuery job history, Document AI processor, Speech-to-Text recognizer, custom class and phrase set | Their docs say "Click Delete" and nothing of the dialog, or have no console steps (re-read 2026-11-19) |

An action's own confirmation follows Google's too (`Action.ConfirmStyle`, `Action.ConfirmTitle`, #1860):
Cloud SQL's Delete backup asks for the word `Delete` (sql/docs/postgres/backup-recovery/backing-up),
Stop and Start ("Stop database instance?", sql/docs/mysql/start-stop-restart-instance) and a user's
Remove (sql/docs/mysql/create-manage-users) for nothing typed, as do a Compute Engine VM's Stop, Start / Resume,
Suspend, Reset and Delete (compute/docs/instances/stop-start-instance, suspend-resume-instance, reset-instance,
deleting-instance). Cloud Storage's Delete HMAC key
asks for the access ID's first 10 characters (storage/docs/authentication/managing-hmackeys),
Storage Intelligence's Disable for the word `Disable`
(storage/docs/storage-intelligence/configure-and-manage-storage-intelligence), and Version
history's Delete for the word `delete` (storage/docs/using-versioned-objects) (#2047).
A secret version's Disable and Enable are confirmed with nothing typed, their buttons Google's
Disable selected versions and Enable selected versions, and Destroy asks for the secret ID
(`Action.ConfirmWord`), its button Destroy selected versions
(secret-manager/docs/disable-secret-version, enable-secret-version, destroy-secret-version) (#1850).

Cloud Tasks' prompts (tasks/docs/deleting-appengine-queues-and-tasks, #1848): Pause queue is
confirmed by **Pause** and Purge queue by **Purge**, a task's Delete task by **Delete**, each with
nothing typed (`Action.Submit` names the button); Delete queue by the name typed, then
**Confirm**, on the queue's page, its row and the list's header button. A Deleter can name its
list's header Delete and its prompt's button (`console.DeleteWorder`): Cloud Tasks' Delete queue
and Confirm.

#### Create labels (#1933)

Each create button and the last button of its page, as its product's docs
word them: Cloud SQL's Create instance, ending in Create Instance
(sql/docs/mysql/create-instance, the docs' own case); Artifact Registry's
Create Repository, ending in Create (artifact-registry/docs/repositories/create-repos);
Firestore's Create a Firestore database, ending in Create Database
(firestore/native/docs/manage-databases); Memorystore for Redis' Create
instance, ending in Create instance (memorystore/docs/redis/create-instance-console);
Create subscription, Create queue, Cloud Scheduler's Create job, Create
schema, Create role and Create snapshot ending in Create (pubsub/docs/create-subscription,
tasks/docs/creating-queues, scheduler/docs/creating, pubsub/docs/create-schemas,
iam/docs/creating-custom-roles, pubsub/docs/replay-overview); Cloud Run
jobs' Deploy container, opening Create job and ending in Create
(run/docs/create-jobs); Create service account ending in Done
(iam/docs/service-accounts-create); Groups' Create, ending in Submit
(iam/docs/groups-in-cloud-console); Certificate Authority Service's Create
pool, ending in Done (certificate-authority-service/docs/creating-ca-pool).
Create topic, Create secret, Create metric, Create sink, Create dataset and
Create table end in their own label, as their docs do. Eventarc's Create
trigger and Cloud KMS single-tenant HSM's Create instance are UNVERIFIED:
their docs have no console steps (re-read 2026-11-19).
| Detail pages | Back arrow, "<Kind> details", actions on the title line, tabs | Consistent since round 2 (§4.7): every detail route declares `kind` | — |
| Loading | A progress indicator, then the data | Consistent: `loadingState` everywhere, with "Still loading …" and Cancel after 8 s | — |

#### Document AI and Speech-to-Text (#1930)

Shared additions (#2084): an action's answer may carry `Listing.Open`, a console route the page goes to instead of drawing the answer in a dialog (the page left is not redrawn). `console.KindMedia` is a section holding a `Media` player (its `Src` the console's `/api/objects/{service}/media` route for an object of the service's ObjectStore, its `Type` and `Label`) with the section's `Text` under it; the route plays only audio types (an object labelled otherwise is refused 415, never served as a document), answers Range requests so the player seeks, sends the object CSP and nosniff, and plays at most 128 MiB. `console.KindResult` draws a section's `Listing` as an action's answer is drawn (page images with their boxes beside the text and rows), in a region that scrolls sideways on its own.

Shared additions (#1930): an action's answer may carry `Listing.Text`, drawn before its rows as a scrolling block that keeps line breaks (`#action-result-text`): a test document's extracted text, a transcript. It may carry `Listing.Pages`, page images each with boxes over it (`ResultBox`, fractions of the page, each tied to a row by name), drawn beside the text and rows in a wide dialog (side by side when there is room, stacked at 320px); pointing at or focusing a row lights its box, and pointing at a box lights its row. A SummaryOnly screen's cards may carry `PropertyGroup.Section`, a heading over the run of cards it begins.

Google's Document AI console is reconstructed from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/document-ai/docs/create-processor> and <https://docs.cloud.google.com/document-ai/docs/manage-processor-versions> (read 2026-11-19), which name the Processor Gallery (`/ai/document-ai/processor-library`), the Processors page (`/ai/document-ai/processors`) with Name, Status, Region and Type and an action menu of Enable processor and Disable processor, the side panel's processor name and Region, and a processor's Overview (Name, ID, Type, Prediction endpoint) and Manage versions tab. Speech-to-Text's is from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/speech-to-text/docs/migration> (the console's Transcriptions page and New Transcription with its Audio configuration and Transcription options); Google documents no console page for recognizers, so the Recognizers page follows the API's resource (`projects.locations.recognizers`).

- [x] Document AI menu: Overview, Processor gallery, My processors. The Overview says what runs locally and links to the other two.
- [x] Processor gallery: Google's cards, one a type of Google's gallery under its category's heading (Digitize, Extract, Classify and split, Specialized), each with its description and status; Enterprise Document OCR and Layout Parser (the types FetchProcessorTypes names) are Available with Create processor, which opens the type's page with Google's side panel (Processor name, Region: US or EU); every other type's card links to its page, which says *Not available locally* and why.
- [x] My processors: Processor ID, Name, Region, Type, Status (ListProcessors in us and eu); Create processor (type, name, region); Enable processor and Disable processor in each row's menu and on its page; Delete.
- [x] Upload test document opens Google's analysis page (#2084), a page of its own at the processor's address with `analysis/{id}`, headed "Layout parser analysis" for the layout parser (Google's "layout parser analysis page") and "<Type> analysis" otherwise, with a breadcrumb back to the processor; its summary names the processor, the processor version, the document and when it was processed; its document view (Blocks, or Document for the OCR processor) is the page images with their boxes, the text and the elements' rows; the layout parser's chunks are a Chunks tab. ProcessDocument keeps nothing, so the console keeps the last 20 analyses in memory until the instance stops.
- [x] A processor's page: Overview (Name, ID, Type, Region, Status, Prediction endpoint with Copy, resource name); Manage versions (ListProcessorVersions: Version ID, Version name, Status, Default, Google managed, Created; Google's Default version picker, SetDefaultProcessorVersion; Deploy, Undeploy and Delete over the checked versions, each showing Google's refusal for a pretrained or default version); Upload test document (PDF, PNG, JPEG, GIF, TIFF, BMP or WebP sent to ProcessDocument, with Google's Process options: the processor version and the pages, and the processor's own config, the OCR processor's symbols and the layout parser's Edit Layout Config chunk size, read from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/document-ai/docs/layout-parse-quickstart> 2026-11-20; each page image the OCR processor answers with its blocks, paragraphs and lines boxed over it, beside the extracted text and the elements' rows with their confidence and normalized bounding box, as Google's test panel draws the result over the document; the layout parser's blocks, organized by detected type as Google's layout parser analysis page has them, and chunks beside its text; the OCR result's tokens, and its symbols when chosen, are rows and boxes too); Batch process (a gs:// folder or document and a gs:// destination sent to BatchProcessDocuments, with the processor version to use and an optional pages per shard (#1953), each document's status and the folder its result was written to); Evaluate & test (ListEvaluations of the default version, none for a served type, and why, #1953).
- [x] Speech-to-Text menu: Transcriptions, Recognizers, Custom classes, Phrase sets.
- [x] Transcriptions: Google's New transcription (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/speech-to-text/docs/models/chirp-3>, read 2026-11-20): its Audio configuration, a Local upload (sent to Recognize) or a Cloud storage file with an optional gs:// output prefix (sent to BatchRecognize), then Continue to its Transcription options, the Recognizer with a Spoken language and a Model over its default config (config_mask), ending in Submit. With no recognizer, New transcription opens Recognizers.
- [x] Workspaces (#2084): the Audio configuration's Workspace drop-down lists the project's workspaces, buckets of the instance's Cloud Storage labelled `speech-to-text-workspace=true`, and New workspace, which takes a bucket name and creates the bucket, or labels an existing one (the buckets.update Google's console needs). Submit writes the transcription to the workspace through the official storage client, `transcriptions/{id}/metadata.json` (recognizer, model, language, source, audio and transcript URIs, created), a Local upload's audio as uploaded, and `transcript.json` (the RecognizeResponse in JSON); a Cloud storage file's audio stays where it is. Submit then opens its Transcription details. The Transcriptions page lists the workspaces' transcriptions (Recognizer, Model, Language, Audio file, Workspace, Transcript, Created), newest first. Without Cloud Storage the transcriptions are kept in the console's memory, as before.
- [x] Transcription details (#2084): Recognizer (a link), Model, Spoken language, Workspace (a link to the bucket), Audio source, Audio file, Transcript file and Created; the Transcript tab plays the stored audio object in the browser's own player (its keyboard controls, a focus ring on the player, its duration announced in a status line) with the transcript under it; Results lists each result's transcript, confidence, language and end offset. Google documents no word-by-word highlighting, so none is drawn.
- [x] Recognizers: Recognizer ID, Display name, Region, Model, Language, Status, deleted ones too (show_deleted) with Undelete in the row menu; Create recognizer (ID, region, display name, model (chirp, chirp_2, chirp_3, chirp_telephony, long, short, telephony, telephony_short), language, word timestamps, word-level confidence, automatic punctuation); a recognizer's page with its default recognition config, Edit (UpdateRecognizer, under its etag), Transcribe (an uploaded file, its transcript), Delete and Undelete.
- [x] Custom classes and Phrase sets (after Recognizers): each listed with deleted ones (ID, Display name, Region, Items or Phrases and Boost, Status); Create (ID, region, display name; items one a line, or phrases one a line with an optional `| boost` and the set's boost); its page (summary, its items or phrases with their boosts); Edit, Delete, and Undelete on a deleted one.
- [x] No Build tab: Google's Build (Import documents, Manage dataset, Train new version) is a custom extractor's, classifier's and splitter's (<https://docs.cloud.google.com/document-ai/docs/workbench/build-custom-processor>, read 2026-11-20); Enterprise Document OCR's and Layout Parser's pages have none, so a served processor's page has Overview, Manage versions and Evaluate & test.
- [ ] Not yet: Google's New Workspace sidebar browses the buckets to select one; here the bucket is named. Training and evaluation runs (custom processors only, none served) and the Human-in-the-Loop review (ReviewDocument, refused); custom classes and phrase sets are stored but not applied to recognition (local whisper.cpp has no phrase boosting, so `adaptation` is refused); the medical models are refused.

#### Eventarc Advanced (#1947, #2006)

Google's Eventarc Advanced console is reconstructed from <https://docs.cloud.google.com/eventarc/advanced/docs/receive-events/create-enrollment> (Pipelines page: Pipeline details, Enrollments, Event mediation and Destination, ending in Create; a pipeline's Pipeline details pane has Edit, ending in Save), <https://docs.cloud.google.com/eventarc/advanced/docs/publish-events/publish-events-google-sources> (a Google API source is added and removed on its bus's Edit page) and <https://docs.cloud.google.com/eventarc/advanced/docs/publish-events/publish-events-direct-format> (publishing is gcloud eventarc message-buses publish and the API only; the console has no publish control), read 2026-11-20.

- [x] Eventarc menu: Triggers, then Buses (`/eventarc/message-buses`), Pipelines, Enrollments, Google API sources and Channels, each listing the project's resources in every location (#1947).
- [x] Buses: Region, Log severity, Encryption, Created; Create bus (name, region, display name, log severity, Cloud KMS key, labels); a bus's page with its enrollments; Edit (display name, log severity, key, labels) and Delete.
- [x] Pipelines: Region, Destination, Input format, Created; Create pipeline (HTTP endpoint with network attachment and Google OIDC account, Pub/Sub topic, bus or Workflows; input and output payload formats with their schemas; a CEL transformation; retry policy; log severity; Cloud KMS key; labels); a pipeline's page; **Edit**, every setting but its name and region, prefilled, ending in Save (UpdatePipeline with each setting in its mask, against the etag read, #2006); Delete.
- [x] Enrollments: Region, Message bus, CEL match, Destination pipeline, Created; Create enrollment with the project's buses and pipelines; Edit (CEL expression, pipeline, labels); Delete.
- [x] Google API sources: Region, Destination bus, Log severity, Created; Create Google API source; **Edit** on its page (display name, destination bus, log severity, labels, ending in Save: UpdateGoogleApiSource, #2006), where Google's console changes one from its bus's Edit page; Delete.
- [x] **Publish message (CloudBurrow extension)** on a bus's page (#2006): Google's console has none, so it is labelled as CloudBurrow's own. It takes gcloud's event ID (empty for a new one), source, type, JSON data and attributes and sends Publisher/Publish's json_message, a structured CloudEvent; the bus delivers it to each enrollment whose CEL match accepts it, whose pipeline delivers it to its destination, with platform logs at the bus's and pipeline's log severities in the Logs Explorer.
- [ ] Not yet: Google's Create pipeline is one page that makes its enrollments too (Add an enrollment); here enrollments are made on the Enrollments page. Google's Cloud Run service and Cloud Run job destination types are an HTTP endpoint here, the service's URL.

## 5. Routes, scoping, accessibility, viewports

### Routes

Deep-linkable, and readable as text:

Every drillable route also carries `?tab=` for the section on screen, so a
colleague can be sent a tab rather than a resource. The Logs Explorer's filters
are in the query string too — `severity`, `source`, `resource`, `contains`,
`scope`, `operation` — because a log query someone got right is a log query
worth sending to someone else.

A resource lives at its own address, one path segment per level, so a table
inside a database is linkable and readable as text. The depth is the
provider's: `Driller.Detail` receives the ordered path, and one that does not
understand a level says so rather than quietly showing the level above.

An exact route wins over a resource path, so `/run/create` stays the deploy
form rather than resolving to a service called "create", and the longest
prefix wins, so `/run/jobs` is the Jobs page: a service called "jobs" is
listed but its page is not reachable by address. The previous
`?resource=` form still resolves, so a link saved before this keeps working.

**What is still not drillable**, and why, since the previous note here named
three products that have had detail pages for several changes:

- **Events** and the Kubernetes **Services**/**Jobs** list rows that do open, but
  a *cluster event* does not: it is already the whole record, and a page showing
  one field per line would be the same text in a worse layout.
- **Pub/Sub subscriptions** do not open from their topic's page: a row there
  offers **Delete**, performed at the path `[topic, subscription]`, so a
  subscription of another topic cannot be deleted through this one. Each
  subscription's own page is on the Subscriptions screen,
  `/pubsub/subscriptions/{subscription}`, which also lists and deletes the ones
  whose topic was deleted (#595).
- **Pub/Sub snapshots** on a topic's page open the snapshot's own page on the
  Snapshots screen, `/pubsub/snapshots/{snapshot}`, whose rows open the
  subscriptions that can seek to it (#787).
- **Pub/Sub schema revisions** do not open: a revision row on a schema's page
  shows its definition and offers **Roll back to this revision** and **Delete
  revision**, performed at the path `[schema, revision]` (#788).
- A **Cloud Storage object** has no page of its own, but its row offers
  **Download**, **Preview** and **Delete**, and a bucket or folder offers
  **Upload file** (#295). Each streams through the official client: an upload
  goes to the backend as it is read and a download reaches the browser as it is
  produced, so no object is held in memory. Uploads are capped by the **Upload
  limit** in Settings (default 32 MiB, kept by the server that enforces it); a
  larger file is refused with the limit named and leaves no object. A download
  is always `application/octet-stream` with `Content-Disposition: attachment`.
  A **preview** (up to 1 MiB) is served as `text/plain` for text, JSON, XML,
  YAML, SVG and HTML, and as itself only for PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP; every
  object response carries `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; …;
  sandbox` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. Rendering an object as the
  HTML it claims to be would run its script with the console's authority, which
  can delete everything, so it is shown as its source. An upload replaces an
  existing object of the same name, as the API does.
- A **Cloud Storage object version** (#853) opens from its row in Show versions
  at `/storage/browser/_details/{bucket}/{generation}/{object}`, beside the
  object's own `/storage/browser/_details/{bucket}/{object}`: four segments
  where the live page has three, the object's whole name last.

Where a row does not open, it is text rather than a link: a link that leads
nowhere is worse than none. `Driller.Detail` receives the ordered path and a
provider that does not understand a level says so, so the refusal is visible
rather than silently collapsing onto the level above.

Project and location are query parameters (`?project=`, `?location=`), so a link carries its
scope. A link opened with a project that has no resources shows the empty state for that
project, not another project's data.

### Accessibility

The target is WCAG 2.1 AA, which Google's console aims for. Besides the unit
checks on the stylesheet, a browser audit (#1604, `test/browser/accessibility_test.go`)
measures rendered pages in headless Chrome: the Cloud Storage, Pub/Sub, Logs
Explorer, Activity, Request Log and Connect screens with their create forms,
delete dialog, row menu, info panel, search and toolbar panels in the storage
shard, and the BigQuery datasets, dataset, query editor, Create table and Job
history screens in the emulators shard. The rest of the console is audited by
`test/browser/accessibility_services_test.go`, which runs every check below
(names and table headers, contrast in both themes, a Tab walk of each page and
the dialog of each page that opens one, reduced motion, and reflow at 320 and
640px) over every list screen and its create form, in the shard whose
instance serves it: `TestCloudRunAndCICDScreensMeetWCAG` (run: Cloud Run's
services, jobs, worker pools, instances and builds, Cloud Functions, Artifact
Registry, Cloud Build, Secure Source Manager, whose pages carry Developer
Connect, Cloud KMS and Cloud Logging's admin screens),
`TestEventarcAdvancedScreensMeetWCAG` (run: Eventarc's Buses,
Pipelines, Enrollments, Google API sources and Channels with their create
forms, and, in `TestEventarcAdvancedDeliveryThroughTheBrowser`, a bus's,
pipeline's, enrollment's and source's pages, #2006),
`TestDocumentAIAndSpeechScreensMeetWCAG` (run: Document AI's and
Speech-to-Text's pages, a processor's Manage versions tab among them, on the local AI endpoint
Cloud Run starts, #1930), `TestDocumentAIAndSpeechRound3MeetWCAG` (run: the layout parser's
Upload test document with its process options, and New transcription's Transcription options step,
#1930), `TestSpeechAdaptationScreensMeetWCAG` (run: Custom classes and Phrase
sets, their create forms and pages, #1930), `TestDocumentAIAndSpeechRound4MeetWCAG` (run with
Cloud Storage: Transcription details with its audio player, whose media controls are among the
keyboard walk's stops (the walk now tags `audio[controls]` and `video[controls]`), the
Transcriptions page over a workspace, New transcription with New workspace chosen, and the layout
parser's analysis page and its Chunks tab, #2084),
`TestCloudSQLAndBackupDRScreensMeetWCAG` (sql), `TestSpannerAndTheEmulatorScreensMeetWCAG`
(emulators: Spanner, Bigtable, Firestore, Datastore, Memorystore),
`TestBigQueryStudioPanesMeetWCAG` (emulators: BigQuery Studio with each of
its left pane's tabs and the Job history tab, a dataset's Overview and
Create table, #1861), `TestBigQueryStudioSplitTabsMeetWCAG` (emulators:
Studio split into two panes and its tab menu, also at 1440px, #2005),
`TestSecretManagerRound3ScreensMeetWCAG` (run: Secret Manager's Global and Regional secrets
tabs, Create secret and Create regional secret, the Versions tab with its checkboxes, the
Disable selected versions and Destroy selected versions confirmations, View secret value and
the Edit secret page, #1850) and
`TestSecretsSchedulerTasksProjectsIAMAndAIScreensMeetWCAG` (storage, whose
instance serves Secret Manager, Cloud Scheduler and Cloud Tasks: also
Resource Manager, IAM, APIs & Services, Kubernetes Engine, the Agent Platform and
local AI pages, Monitoring, Fault injection, Instance and Cloud Storage's
other pages). It found, and these were fixed: a create form's unset optional
choice was an unnamed blank option (now "None"); a resource-name pattern in a
field's hint (`projects/PROJECT/locations/…`) ran 176px past a 320px window, as
did the Log router's and Logs Storage's scope picker (106px) and every
Monitoring chart (41px, a 320px minimum column); and the Local models table
had no scrolling region of its own, so the page scrolled at 640px.

- [x] Every control reachable by keyboard, in a sensible order. Table rows are inspectable with Enter or Space, which was mouse-only. `TestKeyboardAloneWalksAPageAndCreatesATopic` tags every visible, enabled control on the Pub/Sub topics list, presses Tab until focus comes back round, and fails on any control never reached, any stop with no focus indicator drawn or off screen, and a trap; then it creates a topic with the keyboard alone. It found the first Tab on every page but the dashboard skipping the skip link and the whole toolbar: the drawer revealed the current product with `scrollIntoView`, which in Chrome also moves the sequential focus starting point. The drawer now scrolls its own list instead.
- [x] A visible focus indicator on every focusable element. The global `:focus-visible` rule draws a 2px accent ring, and `TestOutlineSuppressionHasAFocusVisibleRule` enumerates every selector that sets `outline: none` and fails unless a later `:focus-visible` rule for the same selector draws one back. The sweep found three that did not: the toolbar search field (whose `:focus` rule outranked the global ring, so it had none), `main` and the info panel. The ring's contrast against every surface it sits on is in `TestTokensMeetContrast`. This is a rule-level check; the browser suite (#594, part 1) is what will see the rendered ring.
- [x] Focus moves into a dialog on open and returns to the trigger on close. Asserted in headless Chrome by `TestDialogTakesFocusClosesOnEscapeAndGivesItBack` (the Create queue dialog, opened from the keyboard: focus on its first field, the page behind inert, Tab and Shift+Tab kept inside) and by `TestCloudRunEditFormIsPrefilledAndGivesFocusBack` for the Cloud Run edit dialog (#700).
- [x] `Escape` closes any dialog, the overlaid drawer and the collapsed search, and focus goes back to what opened it: the two dialog tests above and `TestViewportsDrawerBadgeAndTableScroll`. The toolbar's panels (project picker, notifications, account, settings) too, by `TestToolbarPanelsCloseOnEscapeAndGiveFocusBack`, which found two that did not close: Account has nothing to focus, so focus stayed on its button where Escape was not heard, and Notifications redraws its list as it opens, removing the link focus had just moved to. A panel with nothing to operate, or one that redraws, now takes focus itself, and Escape is heard from the panel, its button, or wherever focus fell (#1604).
- [x] `Escape` closes a row's actions menu and focus returns to the button that opened it, whether focus was on one of its items or still on that button. Asserted in headless Chrome by `TestRowMenuClosesOnEscapeAndGivesFocusBack` (a queue's row menu, opened from the keyboard; nothing is sent) (#771; found missing by #700).
- [x] Landmarks: `banner`, `navigation`, `main`.
- [x] Every button, link, field, tab and menu item has an accessible name, read from Chrome's accessibility tree on every audited page, dialog, panel and menu, and every table exposes column headers: `TestEveryControlHasAnAccessibleName` and, for BigQuery, `TestBigQueryScreensAreNamedAndLegible`. Icon-only controls carry `aria-label`; none was found unnamed.
- [x] Tables use real `<table>` semantics with `<th scope="col">`.
- [x] Status changes announced through a live region: snackbars are a polite live region and an error one is `role="alert"`, as are form errors. The keyboard test asserts the created topic is announced there.
- [x] Text contrast at least 4.5:1 in both themes. `TestTokensMeetContrast` parses the light, dark and device-dark token blocks and computes the WCAG 2.x ratio of every text/background pairing the stylesheet uses, including hover layers and tinted fills (3:1 for focus rings and status marks; badge text is small, so 4.5:1). Measuring found five below the line, now fixed: light `--accent` `#1a73e8` → `#1967d2` (4.51:1 on white, under 4.5 on any tint), dark `--text-muted` `#9aa0a6` → `#a0a6ac` (4.35:1 on a hovered row), the dark error snackbar (white on `#f28b82`, 2.4:1), dimmed stale meter text (opacity .55, 2.4:1) and the selected picker item's id (opacity .8, 3.35:1). Field boundaries are now measured too (1.4.11): a text field's or select's edge was `--border`, a divider colour at 1.37:1 on white, and the toolbar search was a fill 1.05:1 against the bar. Fields use `--field-border` (`#80868b` light, 3.68:1; `#8e9196` dark, 4.54:1 on a surface), the search field included, and dividers keep `--border`. Placeholders were Chrome's own `#757575` (4.37:1 on the search fill, 2.62:1 in the dark theme) and are now `--text-muted`. The browser audit, `TestContrastInBothThemes`, measures every rendered text run against the colour actually behind it (ancestors composited, with opacity), every shown placeholder and every field boundary, in the light and the dark theme; disabled controls are exempt, as WCAG makes them.
- [x] Nothing conveyed by colour alone; a status has a label as well as a colour. A bar chart's bars (a Cloud Build Dashboard card's Build history, #1854) are a labelled list, each bar named by its outcome, duration and time, focusable, and telling the same beside the chart when hovered or focused; `TestCloudRunBuildConsoleRound3ThroughThePages` audits the Dashboard, Edit private pool, History, a build's Build dependencies and Settings.
- [x] Motion: under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` no element of an audited page runs an animation or transition longer than a frame (`TestReducedMotionStopsMotion`); nothing needed changing.
- [x] Zoom and reflow: at 640 CSS pixels (1280px at 200%) and 320 (1280px at 400%, WCAG 1.4.10) no audited page scrolls sideways and nothing but a table's own region is wider than the window (`TestReflowAt320pxAnd200PercentZoom`). It found the toolbar 72px too wide at 640 (the project picker would not shrink), 1px too wide at 320, the Logs Explorer's filter bar running to 1,492px and the Request Log's table outside a scrolling region, and the project picker's menu, 340px wide, past a 320px window. Action bars now wrap, the picker shrinks, the menu spans the window under the bar below 600px, the bar tightens below 360px, and the Request Log's table scrolls in its own region.

### Viewports

| Width | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| ≥ 1280px | Navigation docked beside the content |
| 960–1279px | Navigation closed, opening as an overlay above a scrim |
| < 960px | Navigation in an overlay; the cross-service search collapses to a control in the toolbar |
| every width | A table's own region scrolls horizontally; the page never does |

Rendered at 1280, 1024, 800 and 360px by `TestViewportsDrawerBadgeAndTableScroll` (#700), which asserts each row of this table at its width, and that the LOCAL badge (§2) and the drawer's first link are painted: read from the pixels Chrome drew, since a check of the DOM passed on the drawer that drew nothing (console-verification.md). At 360px it found the page 442px wide — the project picker's narrow-width limits were outranked by its base rule, and the list's action bar could not wrap — and both are fixed.

Below 360px the bar tightens again, and 320px — the width WCAG 1.4.10 reflows to — is measured by `TestReflowAt320pxAnd200PercentZoom` (#1604). No layout below 320px is supported, and that is a stated limit rather than a silent break.

**On the icon rail.** An earlier version of this table promised "navigation collapsed to
icons, expandable" between 960 and 1279px, and the stylesheet carried rules for a
`data-nav="collapsed"` state to match. Nothing could ever select them: `applyNavState` is
only ever given `closed`, `open` or `docked`. The rail was removed rather than built,
because the console this mirrors has no rail in that range either — the menu is simply
closed and opens as an overlay, which is what this code already did. A styled state the
JavaScript cannot enter is a trap for the next person auditing the drawer, so the rules went
with the promise.

---

## 6. Local AI

[#39](https://github.com/cloudburrow/cloudburrow/issues/39) first concluded that local
inference was not viable, and [local-ai.md](local-ai.md) §4 records the correction: the
LiteRT-LM runtime builds from Google's source and runs on Linux. The console's AI area is
therefore working pages, not a disabled placeholder.

**Agent Platform (formerly Vertex AI, #1839).** Google renamed Vertex AI to *Gemini Enterprise
Agent Platform* on 22 April 2026 and moved its console from `/vertex-ai/` to `/agent-platform/`
([name changes](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/vertex-ai-name-changes)).
The API did not change: `aiplatform.googleapis.com`, the `google.cloud.aiplatform.v1` protos, the
resource names and `gcloud ai` are the same, so clients reach this instance as before. The console
followed: **Agent Platform** is one drawer row under *AI and machine learning* (its product key is
still `vertexai`, so a pin survives), its pages are under `/agent-platform/`, and the old `/ai/...`
addresses and Google's `/vertex-ai/...` prefix answer 301 to the new ones (`RenamedAIPath`).

Google does not publish its left navigation's order; the pages and their groups are reconstructed
from the console pages its documentation links to. Each route carries its `group` (Agent Builder,
Agent Studio, Models, Notebooks, Model development, Deploy and use) for a grouped product navigation.

| Page | Route | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **Overview** | `/agent-platform/overview` | Every page below under its group, each marked as running on this instance, off here (its local backend was not started) or not available locally |
| **Model Garden** | `/agent-platform/model-garden` | Google's cards (Google, partner, self-deploy open and Hugging Face models) with the documented filters (Model collections, Tasks, Providers) and search; each card's licence and link to Google's card. **Deploy** is enabled only on a card CloudBurrow runs locally (Gemma 4 through the LiteRT-LM community file, EmbeddingGemma through the ONNX conversion): it confirms the exact size, downloads the pinned files and loads them, as Local models does, with a progress bar while downloading. Every other card has Deploy disabled and says it is not available locally; a gated local model says why. **Deploy from Hugging Face** (#1840): a model ID is looked up on huggingface.co (licence, gated status, task, revision, size against the free disk); **Download** fetches it at that commit into `<state-dir>/models/huggingface`, each LFS file checked against its SHA-256, with a progress bar, and is refused when it would leave less than 50 GB free; **Cancel** and **Delete** free the space. A gated model needs `HF_TOKEN` in the CloudBurrow process's environment; the console has no token field. **Deploy** on a downloaded model runs ModelGardenService.Deploy (vLLM for text generation, Text Embeddings Inference for embeddings) and opens **Endpoints** (`/agent-platform/endpoints`), which shows it Deploying, with its step and progress, through to Ready, then its details and a **Test your model** panel (Predict, and Chat completion on vLLM); **Undeploy** frees the container and **Delete** removes the endpoint and model, keeping the files (**Delete files** in Model Garden removes them) |
| **Endpoints** | `/agent-platform/endpoints` | Model Garden's deployments (#1840): name, ID, Hugging Face model, serving container and state; the selected endpoint's resource name, model revision and licence, image, and its `:predict` and `/chat/completions` paths; the test panel; Undeploy and Delete. Cloud Run custom prediction endpoints stay on Online prediction |
| **Agent Garden** | `/agent-platform/agent-garden` | The RAG sample Google documents and the Agent Development Kit samples, each with **View code** (its public source). **Deploy** is disabled with the reason: it deploys to Agent Runtime (`reasoningEngines`), which is not served |
| Deployments, Memory Bank, Agent Registry, Evaluation, RAG Engine, Media Studio, Prompt gallery, Model Registry, Tuning, Batch inference, Colab Enterprise, Workbench, Datasets, Feature Store, Training, Pipelines, Experiments, Metadata, Ray on Agent Platform, Vector Search, Monitoring | `/agent-platform/runtimes`, `/memory-bank`, … (Google's paths) | Each says it is not available locally, names the API behind it and links to Google's documentation; none draws an empty list |
| **Local catalogue** | `/agent-platform/local-catalogue` | CloudBurrow's own: every catalogued local model's publisher, repository, access, licence, runtime and status, a row opening its provenance |

The pages that run locally:

| Page | Route | What it does | When it is off |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Studio** (Agent Studio, formerly Vertex AI Studio) | `/agent-platform/studio` | Google's prompt page (#1962), its labels from Google's pages ([Text generation](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/models/capabilities/send-chat-prompts-gemini), [Agent Studio capabilities](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/agent-studio/agent-studio-capabilities), [image understanding](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/models/capabilities/image-understanding), [token counts](https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/models/capabilities/get-token-count), read 2026-11-19): **System instructions**; a **Chat** of every turn, sent again as `contents` with the next (**Clear conversation** empties it); the **Prompt** with **Insert Media** (**Upload**: a PNG, JPEG, WebP, HEIC or HEIF as `inlineData`; **Cloud Storage**: a `gs://` object of this instance as `fileData`; By URL and Google Drive reach outside the instance and are not offered); **Submit** and **Cancel**; the **"&lt;count&gt; tokens"** indicator (`countTokens` of the conversation, system instructions, declarations and prompt, as typed), which opens the prompt tokenizer (`computeTokens`: **Token ID to text** and **Token ID**; media tokens are not shown, as Google's says); the **Response** pane, with the response's `usageMetadata`; **Get code**: the request as the Google Gen AI SDK for Python or curl, pointed at the local endpoint. **Model settings**: Model, **Temperature**, **Output token limit**, **Add stop sequence**, **Advanced** (**Top-K**, **Top-P**, Seed, frequency and presence penalty), Output format (JSON and a response schema), **Function calling** (the declarations as JSON and the mode, AUTO, ANY, NONE or VALIDATED; a returned `functionCall` is shown in the chat and answered with a **Send function response** turn), **Stream model responses** (on: each chunk as it arrives; off: one answer, #1257) and **Raw** (the body as `:streamRawPredict` or `:rawPredict`, #1284, #1285); first-token and total timings and this session's history, held in memory only. The options the endpoint refuses (candidateCount, safetySettings) are listed as refused, not ignored. UNVERIFIED (re-read 2026-11-19: the four pages above, which do not show them): the panel's arrangement, the function declarations editor's layout, the penalties' and Seed's labels, and Get code's languages beyond the Python and curl the Text generation page names. A **Local models** card lists each pinned or served model's state (not downloaded, downloading with its progress, downloaded, loaded) and links to Local models and Embeddings (#1396) | Says local AI is not configured and to start with `-local-ai-model` ([generation.md](generation.md)) |
| **Embeddings** | `/agent-platform/studio/embeddings` | Text sent as text-embedding `:predict`, `:embedContent` (with task type and output dimensionality, #1244) or `:rawPredict` (#1284); each vector with its dimension, norm and first values, the token count and `truncated` flag as the API returns them (#1396), a copy of it, the pairwise cosine similarity and the request sent; the model ID sent as typed, and the API's refusal shown verbatim (#1305); **Images**: each chosen PNG, JPEG, WebP, BMP (#1651), HEIC or HEIF (#1724), MP4 or MOV (#1722, with its audio track when **Videos' audio track** is checked, #1732) or PDF (#1723) sent with `:embedContent` as one `inlineData` part to `embeddinggemma-2-onnx-community` once it is loaded on Local models, its vector compared with the texts' (#1454) | Says to start with `-local-ai-embeddings` ([embeddings.md](embeddings.md)) |
| **Local models** | `/agent-platform/model-garden/local-models` | Each catalogued model's download state and size; **Download** (only a pinned, ungated model, its exact size confirmed, each file checked against its pinned SHA-256), **Cancel**, **Load** and **Unload** (the embedding runtime only), **Delete** the local copy (#1306); the same manager is `/admin/local-models` on the control port, behind the admin token (#1396) | Says local model management is not available on this instance |
| **Online prediction** | `/agent-platform/online-prediction/endpoints` | A custom prediction request (JSON instances and parameters) sent to a deployed contract container, a Cloud Run service with `AIP_*` variables, and the container's status and body verbatim (#869); on an endpoint whose model declares `grpcPorts` (its port named `h2c`), a **DirectRawPredict** form: method name and base64 input sent to the container's gRPC server, the output bytes or its status (#1477); **Explain** with the Sampled Shapley path count and feature baselines, and the attributions per feature (#1454) | Offered only where Cloud Run runs; with no such service it offers **Deploy container** |

**Document AI and Speech-to-Text (#1930)** have products of their own under *AI and machine
learning*, as in Google's console, served by the same local AI endpoint (#1799): Document AI's
Overview, Processor gallery and My processors (`/document-ai/...`), its OCR and layout parser
reading with Tesseract in process, and Speech-to-Text's Transcriptions, Recognizers, Custom
classes and Phrase sets (`/speech/...`), transcribing with whisper.cpp, whose model is fetched the first time audio is
transcribed. Their pages are listed when the endpoint runs (`up` with a local model, embeddings
or Cloud Run); §4.7 has their checklist. With Cloud Storage served, New transcription keeps its
transcriptions in a workspace bucket and Transcription details plays the stored audio (#2084);
Upload test document opens its analysis page.

**Provenance is stated where the output is shown.** No Google-published model is ungated, so
what runs is a community conversion: the Gemma generation model, and the ONNX EmbeddingGemma
(#41). Studio and Embeddings each say so above their output, the Local catalogue says so in its note
and in each model's status detail; `TestProvenanceIsRecordedNotAssumed` holds that a community
conversion is never reported as Google-published. A model with no runtime, no pin or gated access is listed with
the reason and never offered as runnable.

**What is not served is named, not drawn.** Vertex's `Endpoint` resource and batch prediction
have no page, and Explain is Sampled Shapley only; Online prediction names the rest as not served
([playground.md](playground.md) §8). The PredictionService methods the pages send, and the
tests that exercise them, are the Vertex AI (Agent Platform API) rows of §8.

---

## 7. How each claim gets evidenced

Visual fidelity and API compatibility are **separate claims with separate evidence**, and
neither implies the other.

| Claim | Evidence required |
|---|---|
| A screen exists and is reachable | Route test |
| It shows live state | The resource is created through an SDK and appears; created in the UI and visible to the SDK |
| It has all four states (§4.3) | A test per state, including the error state under an injected backend failure |
| Keyboard and focus behaviour | Test per §5 (`TestKeyboardOpensAListRow`, `TestDialogTakesFocusClosesOnEscapeAndGivesItBack`) |
| Responsive behaviour | Rendered at each viewport in §5 (`TestViewportsDrawerBadgeAndTableScroll`) |
| Media plays | The object is written through the official client and played by the page's player from the console's media route, from the keyboard (`TestSpeechWorkspaceAndPlaybackThroughThePage`, #2084) |
| A feature is reachable from the console (§8) | A console unit test of its provider, and a browser test in the shard whose instance serves it (`scripts/compat-shards.sh`) that drives the screen and reads the change back with the official client; a §8 row naming no browser test is offered but not yet exercised from a browser. The parity audits (#1552, #1554, #1591) check every row against the console's code and Google's console |
| **Visual parity with GCP** | **Reference screenshots, which do not exist yet.** Stays `Partial`. |

[#49](https://github.com/cloudburrow/cloudburrow/issues/49) is where this checklist is
walked and the result recorded. Until then, every box above is unchecked — they describe
what is required, not what has been done.

---

## 8. Feature parity: every implemented feature and its console surface

"Every feature we build should be accessible by the UI" (#782). The tables below place every
method a coverage page ([docs/coverage](coverage/README.md)) classes **Verified** or
**Implemented**, every `cloudburrow` command and every admin route in exactly one row, and each
row is one of three things: the console screen that offers it with the tests that exercise it; an
**exclusion** with the reason it has no screen; or a **gap** naming the open issue that builds it.

The tables are generated from `parityRows` in `cmd/cloudburrow/consoleparity_test.go`, and
`TestConsoleParityTables` fails when a coverage page gains a Verified method, the usage text a
command, or `internal/admin` a route, that no row claims, when a row names a method that is no
longer Verified, a provider the registry does not build or a test that does not exist, or when
this section drifts from the rows. Regenerate it with
`go test ./cmd/cloudburrow -run TestConsoleParityTables -update`. When a gap is built, its row
loses the issue and gains the screen and the test.

A screen named here offers what the Console column says because its provider's code offers it;
the Tests column names what exercises it, and an action with no test of its own there is offered
but not yet exercised from the console. Which opt-in services have a screen at all is §3, held by
`TestEveryServiceHasAParityRow`.

a copy of a table as it was at a past time (a table@time source, #1460) is the API's and bq cp's, as Google's Copy table dialog takes no time, and the query editor's Read-write mode makes the same copy with CREATE TABLE … CLONE … FOR SYSTEM_TIME AS OF | `TestConsoleBigQueryLoadExportAndJobHistory`, `TestBigQueryLoadFormBecomesTheLoadsSource`, `TestBigQueryCopyTableFormRunsACopyJob`, `TestBigQueryCopyTableSendsEveryMoreSource`, `TestBigQueryCopyTableSendsTheOperation`, `TestConsoleBigQueryCopyTablesAndAvroExport`, `TestConsoleBigQueryCloneAndSnapshotTables`, `TestConsoleBigQuerySnapshotCloneAndRestoreActions`, `TestBigQueryCopyTableAndAvroExportThroughTheForms`, `TestBigQueryLoadExportAndJobHistoryThroughTheForms`, `TestConsoleBigQueryParquetAndSeveralURIExports`, `TestBigQueryExportFormOffersAvroAndParquet`, `TestConsoleBigQueryLoadFromAFile`, `TestBigQueryLoadFromAFileSendsTheFileAsTheLoadsData`, `TestActionUploadStreamsTheFileToAnOfferedAction`, `TestBigQueryLoadFromAFileThroughTheForm`, `TestBigQueryLoadFormSendsHiveAndGeoJSONOptions`, `TestBigQueryHiveAndGeoJSONLoadsThroughTheForm`, `TestBigQueryLoadFormSendsFileSetSpecType`, `TestConsoleBigQueryLoadHiveCSVAndManifest` |

| Instance status | `status` | `/`: Dashboard: instance, cluster, endpoints, services, components | `TestStatusReportsLiveInstanceState` |
| Logs | `logs` | `/logs`: Logs Explorer: emulator, component and Cloud Run logs, live, filtered by source | `TestFiltersNarrowTheView`, `TestStreamSendsBacklogThenLiveEntries` |
| Admin events | `events`, `GET /admin/events` | `/requests`: Request Log: the recorder's request events, filtered by service, code and project, live, Pub/Sub's and Bigtable's calls through their fronts (#1266, #1267) and Spanner's, Firestore's and Datastore's through the call fronts in their pods, a pod's calls as well as the host's (#1288), among them, and on /monitoring their request rate, error and latency charts beside each emulator pod's container CPU and memory (#1267, #1288); its fault events are Recent faults on the Fault injection page (#800) | `TestTheRequestLogShowsServedCalls`, `TestPubSubCallsReachMetricsAndTheRequestLog`, `TestFirestoreCallsReachMetricsAndTheRequestLog`, `TestBigtableCallsReachMetricsAndTheRequestLog`, `TestFirestoreOnMonitoringChartsItsCallsAndContainer`, `TestSpannerCallsReachMetricsAndTheRequestLog`, `TestAPodsFirestoreCallsReachMetricsAndTheRequestLog`, `TestSpannerOnMonitoringChartsItsCalls` |
| Request quotas | `GET /admin/quotas`, `PUT /admin/quotas`, `DELETE /admin/quotas` | `/apis/api/parametermanager.googleapis.com/quotas`: an API's Quotas & System Limits tab (#2063): its per-minute request quotas as the project has them (the value in force, Google's default, adjusted or not), Edit quota for the project and Reset to default, the limits /admin/quotas lists and sets | `TestConsoleAPIQuotas`, `TestRequestQuotasThroughThePage`, `TestParameterManagerRequestQuotas` |
| Fault injection | `POST /admin/faults`, `GET /admin/faults`, `DELETE /admin/faults` | `/faults`: Fault injection: the rules held, Add rule, Delete per rule, Clear all, the refused services with the reason, Recent faults, with the admin token pasted once per tab (#800) | `TestConsoleFaultsActThroughTheAdminAPI`, `TestConsoleFaultRuleFailsTheSDKCall`, `TestFaultRuleAddedAndDeletedThroughTheForm` |
| State save and load, reset, seed | `state`, `reset`, `seed`, `POST /admin/state/export`, `POST /admin/state/import`, `POST /admin/reset`, `POST /admin/seed`, `GET /admin/instance` | `/instance`: Instance: Save state (download), Load state (upload, confirmed by the instance's name), Reset (every service or those chosen, optionally one project, with Reseed, confirmed by the scope), Seed (upload, If not exists) | `TestConsoleInstanceActsThroughTheAdminAPI`, `TestConsoleStateSaveResetLoadRestores`, `TestInstanceResetConfirmedByTypingTheScope` |
| Connect, About and diagnose | `env`, `gcloud-setup`, `terraform`, `version`, `diagnose` | `/connect`: Connect: what `env` exports in each of its formats with Copy, client snippets, the gcloud-setup, gcloud-teardown and terraform commands to copy; About, as `version` prints it; Download bundle, the `diagnose` bundle (#802) | `TestConsoleConnectIsTheInstancesEnv`, `TestConnectVariablesAreEnvJSON`, `TestAboutIsTheBuildsVersion`, `TestConsoleDiagnoseBundleHoldsNoCredentials`, `TestConnectPageShowsTheEnvironmentAndDownloadsTheBundle` |
| Local AI models | `GET /admin/local-models`, `POST /admin/local-models/{id}/{action}` | `/agent-platform/model-garden/local-models`: Local models: each catalogued model's state (not pinned, not downloaded, downloading with progress, downloaded, loaded), Download (a pinned, ungated model, its exact size confirmed, each file checked against its pinned SHA-256), Cancel, Load, Unload and Delete (#1306), through the same manager the admin routes drive; the Studio lists each model's state and links to it and to Embeddings (#1396) | `TestAdminLocalModelsNeedTheTokenAndRunEachAction`, `TestLocalModelsPageRoutesEachActionAndNeedsTheConfirmedSize`, `TestStudioReachesModelManagementAndTheEmbeddingView`, `TestConsoleLocalModelsLoadEmbedUnload`, `TestConsoleLocalModelsUnloadDeleteDownloadLoad`, `TestStudioManagesLocalModelsAndTestsEmbeddings` |
| Vertex AI custom prediction (serving contract) |  | `/agent-platform/online-prediction/endpoints`: Online prediction: the Cloud Run services configured with the contract's AIP_* variables, JSON instances and parameters, Predict, the container's status and body verbatim, and Explain with its Sampled Shapley spec (#1454); Vertex's Endpoint, the explanation methods that need gradients or an Example index, and batch prediction named as not served (#869) | `TestConsoleOnlinePredictionThroughTheConsoleAPI`, `TestCloudRunPredictorAnswersOnlinePredictionInTheBrowser`, `TestPredictRelaysThroughTheIngressAndShowsTheAnswerVerbatim`, `TestPredictRefusesWhatItCannotSend` |
| Starting the instance | `up` | **Excluded:** starts the process that serves the console, so the console cannot exist before it |  |
| Stopping and destroying | `stop`, `delete` | **Excluded:** ends the process that serves the console (stop) or destroys the cluster it runs on (delete); neither page could report its own outcome |  |
| Workstation checks and offline cache | `doctor`, `prefetch` | **Excluded:** run before an instance exists, to check prerequisites or fill the offline cache `up --offline` reads; a running instance's health is the dashboard's Components card |  |
| Trust and lifecycle hooks | `trust` | **Excluded:** trusting ./cloudburrow.json and its hooks lets `up` run host scripts; that decision stays at the terminal of whoever owns the checkout, so no page can grant it |  |
| Scripting and standalone tools | `wait`, `storage-server`, `gcloud-teardown`, `help` | **Excluded:** a script's exit status (wait), a server run without a console by design (storage-server), removing host gcloud configuration the console never writes (gcloud-teardown), and usage text (help) |  |

<!-- parity-table:end -->
